Binary polynomial multiplicity degree

Determine the per-order function on the cube over fields of characteristic other than two: for such a field \(F\), \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), the least degree \(\delta_F(n,k,\ell)\) of \(P\in F[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) with Hasse multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point of \(\{0,1\}^n\) and exactly \(\ell\) at the origin, below Menezes' range \(n\ge k-1\). The first target is dimension three, in every odd characteristic and in characteristic zero.

Origin: starting research record.

Starting point

The thread starts from the binary multiplicity note (audit:binary-multiplicity-cover-question, preprint draft in publications/binary-polynomial-multiplicity/): the multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma of Dvir, Kopparty, Saraf and Sudan gives \(D(n,k)\ge2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) for all positive \(n,k\), answering the bound asked in Question 4.3 of Bishnoi, Boyadzhiyska, Das and Mészáros (CPC 2023). In correspondence with an author of that paper (24 September 2026, paraphrased), the question's threshold was explained as the range where hyperplane covers attain this value, and two directions were suggested: decide whether the algebraic bound is tight for smaller \(k\), where extremal polynomials need not be products of linear forms, or improve it; and consult the open questions of Sauermann and Wigderson, who study the algebraic problem.

Where we stand

The goal is open. Write \(m=k-\ell-1\) and \(\delta_F=n+2k-2-h\), so that \(h\) is the savings. The value is known:

  • for \(n\le2\) over every field (square theorem);
  • for \(n\ge m\), by Catalan linear forms extending Menezes' formula (capped bound, sandwich);
  • for large \(\ell\), where the savings are the root-curve orders \(\kappa_n(m)=\min\{d:N_{2,n}(d)>m\}\): in characteristic 0 for every \(n\) (theorem), for \(n=3\) in every odd characteristic (theorem), and for \(p>2^{n-1}d\) (theorem).

For \(n=3\) the pre-stable thresholds are at most the torsion-partition value \(B(d,\rho)\) (theorem), with equality at \(\rho=1\) (boundary theorem), at \(\rho=d\), and in every computed case through \(d=7\) over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\) (computation, pending review). The order-zero end is proved: \(\delta_F(3,k,0)=2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\) for \(k\ge2\), and \(\ell_0\ge2\) whenever \(\rho\le d-1\) (order-zero theorem). The rest of the lower half is reduced to one local statement at three points (open statement 1).

The original goal is achieved. Until 25 September 2026 the goal was \(D(n,k)\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) below Bishnoi et al.'s threshold \(k\ge2^{n-2}\). It is proved for all \(n,k\ge1\) and verified in Lean (complete formula), as the minimum over \(\ell\) of the per-order value \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\) (theorem), which holds over every commutative ring of characteristic two (descent). Menezes (arXiv 2609.19009) proves it for \(n\ge k-1\); ours is \(2\le n<k-1\) (novelty check). The preprint is publications/binary-multiplicity-degree/, tag binary-multiplicity-degree-preprint-2026-09-25-rev3.

Open statements

By the threshold-ideal lemma the savings are \(h(k,\ell)=\max\{h:\ell_0(h,m)\le\ell\}\), where \(\ell_0(h,m)\) is the initial degree of an explicit homogeneous ideal \(I^{(h)}_m\subseteq F[y_1,\ldots,y_n]\). The goal is the value of \(\ell_0\) outside the proved ranges above.

  1. Torsion-partition lower bound. On \(\{0,1\}^3\), over every field of characteristic \(\ne2\), for \(d\ge3\) and \(2\le\rho\le d-1\): \(\ell_0(d+1,4d+\rho-1)\ge B(d,\rho)\). Here \(j=d-\rho\), \(q=\min(\rho,j)\), and \(B=\sum_{i\le q}(p_i^2-1)\) over the balanced partition of \(j+q\) into \(q\) parts. Status: open, highest risk. The reverse inequality is proved, and so is \(\ell_0\ge2\): every solution vanishes at the three double points (order-zero theorem, which gives \(\delta_F(3,k,0)=2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\)). By the zero-scheme lemma the statement follows from the zero-scheme conjecture: no curve of degree below \(B\) contains the double-point part of the finite scheme \(Z_{4d+\rho-1}\). That is verified for \(d\le7\) over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\). By the line-restriction bound it follows from two conjectures, each checked in every computed case: every solution vanishes to order \(\mu=\sum\lfloor p_i^2/2\rfloor\) at the double points, and on a line in general position through a double point the restricted solutions have minimal degree \(B-\mu\) (sharp pair; a first version with \(2j\) failed for parts \(\ge4\)). The bound already certifies \(\ell_0\ge9\) at \((6,2)\) and \(\ge7\) at \((7,3)\). The record states that, with the proved results, it gives \(\delta_F(3,k,\ell)\) for all \(k,\ell\).
  2. Stable orders at small primes. For \(n\ge4\), odd \(p\le2^{n-1}d\) and \(d\ge2\), the root-curve orders of degree \(\le d\) are \(\{0,\ldots,N_{2,n}(d)-1\}\), where \(N_{2,n}(d)=\#\{(Q,r)\in\mathbb N\times\{0,1\}^n:2Q+|r|\le d\}\) (refined law). Status: conjecture, certified for \(n\le6\), \(m\le43\) (certificates).
  3. Pre-stable thresholds for \(n\ge4\). \(\ell_0(h,m)\) for \(h\le\kappa_n(m)\) and \(m>n\). Status: open, with no conjectured formula; at the \(\kappa\)-boundary \(\ell_0\) is a determinant degree minus the degree of the minors' gcd (threshold-ideal lemma, part 2).

The remaining route

  1. The cube over odd characteristic: stable values found. Dimension two is proved over every field (theorem). On every finite grid the per-order savings stabilize at the orders of the root curve \(z_i=r_0(y_iT)\) (stable law), and Catalan linear forms extend Menezes' formula to all \(n\ge k-\ell-1\) (capped bound, sandwich). Below that range the stable orders fill initial segments in characteristic 0 (theorem), for \(n=3\) in every odd characteristic (theorem), and for \(p>2^{n-1}d\) (theorem). The pre-stable thresholds are initial degrees of explicit ideals (lemma). For \(n=3\) they are \(d^2-1\) at every boundary (theorem) and at most a torsion-partition value \(B(d,\rho)\) everywhere (theorem), with equality in all computed cases (conjecture). Order zero is proved: \(\delta(3,k,0)=2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\) (theorem), from the two collision lines through each double point (window lemma). The lower half now reduces to a vanishing order at a double point and a splitting type on one line (line-restriction bound). Open, highest risk: the lower half \(\ell_0\ge B\). For \(\rho\ge2\), \(\ell_0\ge\alpha(Z_m)\) for a finite scheme at the arrangement's seven multiple points (zero-scheme lemma); computing that scheme at the double points gives \(\alpha=B\) and the full law for \(d\le7\) over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\) (computation). Small primes for \(n\ge4\): open statement 2.
  2. \(\mathbb F_q\) grids: solved. \(\delta_q(n,k,\ell)=n(q-1)+q\ell+(q-1)\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/q^j\rfloor\) for every prime power \(q\) (theorem). This gives a polynomial lower bound for the odd-field thread's covering numbers. On additive-subgroup grids \(S^n\) the base-\(s\) law fails whenever the \(x^2\) coefficient of \(\prod_{t\in S}(x-t)\) is nonzero, in every dimension \(n\ge2\) (second-coefficient theorem); so for four-element \(S\) it holds exactly on scaled subfields. For those \(S\) in dimension two a different law is conjectured (subspace grids); it is now proved for all \(k,\ell\) (four-element square theorem), by counting orders on the root curve \(z_i=x(y_iT)\) (lemma). In every dimension, an attained law whose count matches \(N_{s,n}\) is exact (counting principle); this reproves the binary per-order lower bound in about a page. For large \(\ell\) the savings on every additive grid are the root-curve orders \(\kappa(m)\), with exactly \(N_{s,n}(d)\) values \(\le d\) (stable per-order law). All of this holds in every characteristic for \(\mathbb F_p\)-subspaces, which gives a counting proof of the \(q\)-ary lower bound for every prime power (characteristic \(p\)). The line is closed in general form; only arithmetic questions about particular \(S\) remain.
  3. Per-order values over characteristic two: solved. \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\) in every dimension (theorem), over every commutative ring of characteristic two (descent), in Lean.
  4. Formalize the original answer in Lean: complete. The complete formula, the per-order theorem and every claim of the preprint are verified, down to multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel and BBDM's hyperplane covers (route, side claims).
  5. The preprint. publications/binary-multiplicity-degree, tagged through -rev3, was sent to the Bishnoi et al. co-author and to Menezes on 25 September. The open items of the second referee pass are in its FEEDBACK.md. Journal submission is the user's decision.
  6. Place the answer in the literature. Menezes, arXiv 2609.19009, covers \(n\ge k-1\) over every field; Huang–Wang–Wang the real range \(n\ge k-1\). The citing works contain no determination for \(2\le n<k-1\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) (novelty check); the search is bounded.

Proposed next step

Route item: cube-odd-characteristic; open statement 1. By the line-restriction bound the law follows from the sharp pair: every solution vanishes to order \(\mu=\sum\lfloor p_i^2/2\rfloor\) at the double points, and \(b_{\min}(E_L)=B-\mu\) on a line in general position through one. Both hold in every computed case.

  • One part first. Prove that the boundary generator \(\Delta_{3,p}/g_{3,p}\) (the case \(\rho=1\), \(d=p\)) vanishes to order exactly \(\lfloor p^2/2\rfloor\) at a double point. Its zero set is the \(p\)-division locus of \(2c_1\), so this is a question about how that locus passes through the degenerate fibre at the double point. Then show that the vanishing orders add over the parts of the product witnesses.
  • Then the splitting type \(\beta=\sum(\lceil p_i^2/2\rceil-1)\), from the family of elliptic curves over a line through the double point.

Publication, Lean and correspondence remain the user's decisions.

Working mathematical context

Conventions. Degree is total degree of the formal polynomial. \(\operatorname{mult}_a(P)\) is the least \(|\alpha|\) with a nonzero coefficient of \(z^\alpha\) in \(P(a+z)\); equivalently all Hasse derivatives of order below it vanish at \(a\). The origin condition \(\operatorname{mult}_0(P)\le k-1\) excludes \(P=0\) and is not linear, so minimum degree is a comparison of two linear spaces rather than one solve. Never reduce modulo the field equations.

Linked results. The multiplicity lower bound: binary multiplicity note. The origin-order bounds: high-origin cover theorem and punctured first-jet lemma (\(q=2\), \(k=2\): degree \(\ge n+1\) with nonzero origin). The odd-field thread's higher-jet obstruction concerns \(3\le k\le q\) and is vacuous at \(q=2\). Reuse these by link and keep their working-proof status; the odd-field thread's hyperplane-cover goal is a different question.

External sources. Alon–Füredi, Covering the cube by affine hyperplanes (1993), Theorem 5; Dvir–Kopparty–Saraf–Sudan (SICOMP 2013), Definition 2.2 and Lemma 2.7; Sauermann–Wigderson, arXiv 2010.00077v2 (JLMS); Bishnoi–Boyadzhiyska–Das–Mészáros, CPC 32(5) 2023, Question 4.3 (arXiv v1 Question 5.2) and Theorem 1.2(a) (hyperplane covers for \(k\ge2^{n-2}\)); Menezes, arXiv 2609.19009 (Theorem 1.1, Corollary 1.3; unrefereed, statements only read). Read the primary text before relying on a statement.

Gaps identified by formalization

  • Prose beyond the Lean scope (resolved). The extended-range entry added an unformalized claim that the Schwartz–Zippel bound is attained only for \(k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\); it is false, and is corrected by a verified equivalence.

The main notebook's companion-degree gap concerns ENS companions, not this problem.

Research record

24 September 2026 — Exact small values point to one extra degree per dimension above the threshold

Finite check for \(n\le7\) and a conjectured formula; no general proof. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. Below the published threshold \(k\ge2^{n-2}\), is the multiplicity bound \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) attained, and if not, what is \(D(n,k)\)? The missing implication for the thread is a formula for \(D(n,k)\) valid for all \(n,k\). This cycle reads the Sauermann–Wigderson statements, computes exact values in the range they leave open and stops at a stated general conjecture supported by predictions made before the larger cases were run.

What Sauermann–Wigderson settle over \(\mathbb F_2\)

Read from arXiv 2010.00077v2 (Theorems 1.3–1.5 and Section 4.2). Over \(\mathbb R\), for \(k\ge2\) and \(n\ge2k-3\): origin order at most \(k-2\) forces degree at least \(n+2k-3\), and origin order exactly \(k-1\) forces \(n+2k-2\), both attained. Theorem 1.5 (origin order \(k-1\)) holds over every field and every \(n\ge1\). Their proof of the other bounds uses characteristic zero only through the nonvanishing of the Catalan numbers; in characteristic two \(C_2=2\) vanishes, so their Theorem 1.3 holds for \(k=2,3\) and fails first at \(k=4\), where their explicit \(\mathbb F_2\) polynomial \(\prod_\ell(x_\ell+1)\cdot Q\) has degree \(n+4\) and value one at the origin. They pose no problem about \(\mathbb F_2\) beyond this discussion; their concluding remarks concern real hyperplane covers and fixed \(n\) with large \(k\). So over \(\mathbb F_2\) the large-\(n\) values are \(D(n,2)=n+1\) and \(D(n,3)=n+3\), far above the note's bound, and the open range is \(k\ge4\), in particular \(k>(n+3)/2\), where their theorem does not apply at all.

Exact values

Method. At the origin the Hasse conditions only say that \(P\) has no monomials of degree below its order. Hence a polynomial of degree at most \(d\) with multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and origin order exactly \(\ell\) exists if and only if \(r(\ell,d)<r(\ell+1,d)+N_\ell\), where \(r(\ell,d)\) is the \(\mathbb F_2\)-rank of the nonzero-point conditions restricted to monomials of degree in \([\ell,d]\) and \(N_\ell\) is the number of degree-\(\ell\) monomials. The condition for point \(a\) and \(|\beta|<k\) at monomial \(x^\alpha\) is \(\prod_i\binom{\alpha_i}{\beta_i}a_i^{\alpha_i-\beta_i}\bmod2\), evaluated by Lucas' theorem. The kernel research/tools/bmd_min_degree.cpp computes the rank profiles by bitset elimination with columns in ascending degree and extracts witnesses by one Gauss–Jordan pass in descending degree: in reduced form a free degree-\(\ell\) column gives a kernel vector vanishing on all lower degrees, hence a witness of origin order exactly \(\ell\).

Validation. A brute-force enumeration (research/tools/bmd_reference.py) agrees on \((n,k)=(2,1),(2,2),(3,1)\), including per-order minima. The kernel reproduces every known value: \(D(n,1)=n\) (Alon–Füredi), \(D(n,2)=n+1\) and \(D(n,3)=n+3\) for \(n\ge3\), origin order \(k-1\) at exactly \(n+2k-2\), and \(D(3,4)=7\), \(D(4,4)=8\). Every witness is re-checked by direct evaluation of its Hasse coefficients with exact binomials, and for \(n\le5\) a direct solve also confirms nonexistence one degree below each minimum. The faster three-elimination mode agrees with the per-order mode on all 21 cases where both ran.

Results (research/results/binary-multiplicity-degree/c1-exact-table/, one JSON per case with witnesses, table.txt and a hash manifest). \(D(n,k)\), with the multiplicity lower bound in parentheses:

\(n\)\(k=1\)2345678
33 (2)4 (4)6 (6)
44 (2)5 (4)7 (6)8 (8)
55 (2)6 (4)8 (6)9 (8)11 (10)13 (12)15 (14)16 (16)
66 (2)7 (4)9 (6)10 (8)12 (10)14 (12)
78 (4)10 (6)11 (8)

The minimizing origin orders vary: for example only order \(1\) attains \(D(5,5)=11\) (order \(0\) needs 12), orders \(0,2\) attain \(D(5,6)\), and orders \(0,1,3\) attain \(D(5,7)\). At \(n=7\), \(k=4\) the value \(11=n+4\) shows the Sauermann–Wigderson degree is optimal there, a case their characteristic-two remark leaves open.

The conjectured formula

With \(m(k)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\), the least dimension at which \(k\) reaches the published threshold, every computed value satisfies

\[ D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor=2k+\bigl(n-m(k)\bigr)\qquad(1\le k<2^{n-1}). \]

For \(2^{n-2}\le k<2^{n-1}\) the right side is \(2k\), the multiplicity bound, which the question's authors report is attained there by hyperplane covers (the exact covering theorem was not re-read this cycle; the computed threshold cases \((3,2),(3,3),(4,4),(5,8)\) agree). Below the threshold it says that each dimension beyond \(m(k)\) costs exactly one degree. For large \(n\) it reads \(n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\), recovering \(n+1,n+3,n+4\) for \(k=2,3,4\), and it lies below the real answer \(n+2k-3\) exactly when \(k\ge4\). The formula was stated after the \(n\le5\) and \(n=6,k\le4\) data and then tested with the degree cap set to the prediction: the new cases \((6,5),(6,6),(7,4)\) gave 12, 14, 11 as predicted, while \((7,2),(7,3)\) were already fixed by Sauermann–Wigderson.

A reduction for the upper half. \(\prod_i(1+x_i)\) has multiplicity exactly \(|a|\) at each point \(a\). So if a single polynomial \(Q_k\), independent of \(n\), has degree \(2k-m(k)\), order below \(k\) at the origin and multiplicity at least \(k-|a|\) at every point of weight \(1\le|a|<k\), then \(\prod_i(1+x_i)Q_k\) gives \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-m(k)\) for every \(n\). This holds for \(k=2\) with \(Q=1+\sum_ix_i\) and for \(k=4\) by Sauermann–Wigderson. The \(D(5,5)\) data show \(Q_k\) must be allowed a positive origin order.

General statement. The all-parameter claim of this cycle is the conjecture conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula; the finite evidence is check:binary-multiplicity-degree-exact-table. Neither half is proved beyond the recorded cases.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read from their sources: the note's deduction, the high-origin cover theorem and the Sauermann–Wigderson Theorems 1.3–1.5 and Section 4.2. The claim registry has no equivalent statement (searched by content words). The computation checks are listed above. No new lemma is claimed, so no reviewer pass was needed for this cycle.

Assessment. The missing implication is now a precise formula rather than an open window, with the answer to the co-author's question: the multiplicity bound is not attained below the threshold, and the excess is \(n-m(k)\). Both halves need proofs, most promisingly the one-dimension steps \(D(n,k)\ge D(n-1,k)+1\) and a product construction \(Q_k\). Process: the first kernel re-eliminated the same matrix for every origin order and degree and was launched without a sizing run, costing about 20 minutes on the largest case; the three-elimination mode and a tightened computation rule address this.

24 September 2026 — The upper half reduces to powers of two and holds for every k up to 15

Working proofs of a general reduction and of the upper bound for k≤15 from finite certificates; powers of two from 16 open. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The conjectured formula needs an upper-bound construction of degree \(n+2k-m(k)\), \(m(k)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\), for all \(n,k\). The previous entry reduced this to an \(n\)-independent factor \(Q_k\) with \(P=\prod_i(1+x_i)Q_k\). This cycle looks for such \(Q_k\), proves what they give for all \(n\), and stops at a proved bound or a precise reduction.

Facts used. (A) For nonzero \(P,Q\), \(\operatorname{mult}_a(PQ)=\operatorname{mult}_a P+\operatorname{mult}_a Q\): the lowest homogeneous parts of \(P(a+z)\) and \(Q(a+z)\) multiply to a nonzero form. (B) \(F_n=\prod_i(1+x_i)\) has \(\operatorname{mult}_a F_n=|a|\), since \(1+a_i+z_i\) is \(z_i\) or \(1+z_i\). (C) \(g=x_1^2+x_1\) satisfies \(g(a+z)=z_1+z_1^2\), so \(\operatorname{mult}_ag=1\) at every point.

The power-of-two reduction

Working lemma. Let \(j\ge0\), \(n\ge1\) and \(Q\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) with (i) \(\deg Q\le2^{j+1}-j-2\), (ii) \(\operatorname{mult}_0Q=t<2^j\), (iii) \(\operatorname{mult}_aQ\ge2^j-|a|\) whenever \(1\le|a|<2^j\). For \(0\le r<2^j\) and \(k=2^j+r\), the polynomial \(P=F_nQg^r\) has degree at most \(n+2k-m(k)\), origin order \(t+r<k\) and multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point. Hence \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\).

Proof. Here \(m(k)=j+2\) and \(\deg P\le n+2^{j+1}-j-2+2r=n+2k-m(k)\). By (A)–(C), \(\operatorname{mult}_0P=t+r<2^j+r\). For \(1\le|a|<2^j\), \(\operatorname{mult}_aP\ge|a|+(2^j-|a|)+r=k\); for \(|a|\ge2^j\), \(\operatorname{mult}_aP\ge|a|+r\ge k\). The factor \(g^r\) is what makes the computed origin orders equal \(k-2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}\).

Certificates valid in every dimension

Working lemma. For a finite set \(L\) of partitions let \(Q^{(n)}=\sum_{\lambda\in L}m_\lambda^{(n)}\), the monomial symmetric polynomials in \(n\) variables (zero when \(\lambda\) has more than \(n\) parts). For \(w\ge1\), a vector \(b\in\mathbb N^w\) and a multiset \(c\) of positive integers, let \(N(\lambda;b,c)\) be the number of maps from the \(w+\#c\) positions to values, with equal parts of \(\lambda\) not distinguished, that use each nonzero part of \(\lambda\) exactly once, put at a "one" position with value \(b_i\) either nothing (only if \(b_i=0\)) or a part \(p\) of which \(b_i\) is a bit-submask, and put at a "zero" position with value \(c_l\) exactly the part \(c_l\). Then for every \(n\), every point \(a\) of weight \(w\) and every \(\beta\) that restricts to \((b,c)\) on the coordinates with \(a_i=1\) and on those with \(a_i=0,\beta_i>0\), the coefficient of \(z^\beta\) in \(Q^{(n)}(a+z)\) is \(\sum_{\lambda\in L}N(\lambda;b,c)\bmod2\). So if these sums are even for all \(1\le w<K\) and all \(\Sigma b+\Sigma c<K-w\), then \(\operatorname{mult}_aQ^{(n)}\ge K-|a|\) for every \(n\) and every \(1\le|a|<K\).

Proof. The coefficient of \(z^{\beta_i}\) in \((a_i+z_i)^{\alpha_i}\) is \(\binom{\alpha_i}{\beta_i}\), odd exactly when \(\beta_i\) is a bit-submask of \(\alpha_i\) (Lucas), if \(a_i=1\), and \([\alpha_i=\beta_i]\) if \(a_i=0\). So the coefficient in \(m_\lambda^{(n)}(a+z)\) is the parity of the number of distinct exponent vectors \(\alpha\) in the orbit of \(\lambda\) with every factor odd. Such \(\alpha\) vanish at zero positions with \(\beta_i=0\), equal \(\beta_i\) at the other zero positions, and at one positions are \(0\) (only when \(\beta_i=0\)) or a nonzero part satisfying the submask condition. These \(\alpha\) correspond exactly to the maps counted by \(N\), and the zero parts fill the remaining coordinates uniquely, so the count does not involve \(n\). If \(N>0\) then \(\lambda\) has at most \(w+\#c\le n\) nonzero parts; if \(\lambda\) has more than \(n\) parts both sides are zero. A pair \((b,c)\) with \(w+\#c>n\) does not occur in dimension \(n\).

The upper bound for every \(k\le15\)

Working theorem (finite certificates). For every \(n\ge1\) and \(1\le k\le15\), \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\).

Proof. A symmetric search (research/tools/bmd_symmetric_q.py, the conditions of the certificate lemma over all partitions of size at most the degree bound) found sets \(L_0,\ldots,L_3\) for \(K=1,2,4,8\): \(Q_1=1\), \(Q_2=1+e_1\), \(Q_4\) equal to the Sauermann–Wigderson characteristic-two factor \(1+\sum_i(x_i^3+x_i^2+x_i)+\sum_{i\ne j}(x_i^3+x_i^2)x_j+\sum_{i<j}x_ix_j+\sum_{i<j<l}x_ix_jx_l\), and \(Q_8\), a sum of 72 monomial symmetric functions of degree at most 11 (listed in research/results/binary-multiplicity-degree/c2-symmetric-q/symmetric-q-k1-11.json). research/tools/bmd_check_certificates.py verifies directly that each \(L_j\) contains the empty partition (so \(Q^{(n)}(0)=1\) in every dimension), has sizes at most \(2^{j+1}-j-2\), and satisfies all 216 parity conditions (certificates-check.json). By the certificate lemma, \(Q_{2^j}^{(n)}\) satisfies (i)–(iii) of the reduction for every \(n\); the reduction with \(j=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\le3\) and \(r=k-2^j\) gives the bound.

Cross-check, not part of the proof. Independently of the parity conditions, the products \(F_nQ_k\) for symmetric \(Q_k\), \(k\le11\), were expanded in \(n=3,\ldots,7\) and their multiplicities computed by shifting each monomial (bmd_min_degree verify, cross-checked against the direct Hasse evaluation where cheap): every case has degree exactly \(n+2k-m(k)\), multiplicity \(\ge k\) off the origin and origin order \(k-2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}\) (verification.json). Symmetric \(Q_k\) of the target degree exist for every \(k\le11\).

What remains of the upper half. By the reduction, the upper half of the conjecture for all \(k\) is equivalent to the existence, for every \(j\), of a factor \(Q_{2^j}\) satisfying (i)–(iii) in all dimensions; this is recorded as the power-of-two factor conjecture (conj:power-of-two-factor-existence). The certificates show no closed form yet: \(Q_4\) and \(Q_8\) contain every \(m_\lambda\) of degree below \(2^j\), so \(Q_{2^j}\) agrees with the truncation of \(\prod_i(1+x_i)^{-1}\) below degree \(2^j\) and \(F_nQ_{2^j}=1+(\text{terms of degree}\ge2^j)\), plus a correction of 1 and 27 terms. Products of the "bit" functions \(1+e_{2^i}\) cannot supply it, since weight-one points already need multiplicity \(2^j-1\) from the bit-zero factor.

General statement. The all-parameter results of this cycle are lem:power-of-two-factor-reduction and lem:dimension-free-symmetric-certificates; the finite-certificate theorem thm:binary-multiplicity-upper-bound-small-k applies them for \(k\le15\), and conj:power-of-two-factor-existence states the remaining upper-half obligation.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the previous entry's definitions and the Sauermann–Wigderson Section 4.2 polynomial, which matches \(L_2\) term by term. Claim searches under "power of two", "Frobenius", "product construction" and "symmetric functions" found nothing equivalent. A fresh-context reviewer given only the draft found Facts A–C, both lemmas and the theorem correct, and required three changes, all made: \(N\) counts distinct exponent vectors with equal parts undistinguished (the code already does), separate symbols for the size and the sum of \(c\), and the theorem's status as resting on the named finite certificates. It noted that \(n\ge1\) is needed only when \(r>0\).

Assessment. The upper half of the missing implication is now reduced to one family of objects, the power-of-two factors, and proved for \(k\le15\); the lower half is untouched. The line looks productive: the reduction is exact and explains the observed origin orders. The next risk is whether \(Q_{2^j}\) has a uniform construction; the lower bound may be the harder half. Process: fast symmetric search (seconds up to \(k=10\)); one Python run timed out before saving because output was written only at the end, fixed by writing after each \(k\), and the first verifier was too slow and was replaced by a shifting evaluator.

24 September 2026 — A discrete derivative proves the whole lower half; the formula holds for every k up to 15

Working proofs of the lower bound for all k and of the exact value for k≤15; the upper half beyond 15 remains the power-of-two factor conjecture. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The lower half of the conjectured formula, \(D(n,k)\ge2k+n-m(k)\), had no progress. It would follow from a one-dimension step \(D(n-1,k)\le D(n,k)-1\) and the multiplicity bound at the threshold dimension \(m(k)\). This cycle proves that step, tested first against every adjacent pair of the exact table (16 pairs, all differing by exactly one).

Admissible polynomials exist. With \(h=\prod_{y\ne0}(1+y(x))\) over the nonzero linear forms \(y\), \(h(0)=1\) and \(h\) has multiplicity at least \(2^{n-1}\) at every nonzero point (the \(2^{n-1}\) forms with \(y(a)=1\) each vanish there), so \(h^k\) is admissible and \(D(n,k)\) is finite.

The one-dimension step

Working lemma. For \(n\ge2\) and \(1\le k<2^n\), \(D(n-1,k)\le D(n,k)-1\).

Proof. Let \(P\) attain \(D(n,k)\), with degree \(d\), origin order \(\ell\le k-1\) and lowest homogeneous component \(L\ne0\) at the origin. At most \(\ell\le2^n-2\) distinct linear forms divide \(L\), fewer than the \(2^n-1\) nonzero forms, so some nonzero form \(y\) does not divide \(L\). An invertible linear substitution \(A\) with \(y\circ A=x_n\) preserves degree, permutes the nonzero points and preserves multiplicities (\(\operatorname{mult}_a(P\circ A)=\operatorname{mult}_{Aa}P\), since an invertible linear substitution in \(z\) keeps the lowest component nonzero); so assume \(x_n\nmid L\). Write \(P=\sum_{t\ge0}x_n^tP_t(x')\) and set

\[ S(x')=P(x',0)+P(x',1)=\sum_{t\ge1}P_t(x'). \]

(a) \(\deg S\le d-1\), since \(\deg P_t\le d-t\). (b) For \(a'\ne0\) both \((a',0)\) and \((a',1)\) are nonzero; setting \(z_n=0\) in \(P((a',c)+z)\) sends each monomial to itself or to zero, so \(P(x',c)\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at \(a'\), and so has \(S\). (c) \(P(z',1)\) is the restriction at the nonzero point \((0',1)\), so it has no terms of degree below \(k\). \(P(z',0)\) has lowest component \(L(z',0)\), nonzero of degree \(\ell\) because \(L=x_nL'+L(x',0)\) and \(x_n\nmid L\). Hence \(\operatorname{mult}_{0'}S=\ell\le k-1\). So \(S\) is admissible in dimension \(n-1\).

The lower bound

Working theorem. For every \(k\ge1\) and \(n\ge m(k)\), \(D(n,k)\ge2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\).

Proof. With \(m=m(k)\), \(2^{m-2}\le k<2^{m-1}\), so the multiplicity bound gives \(D(m,k)\ge2k-\lfloor k/2^{m-1}\rfloor=2k\). For \(m<n'\le n\), \(k<2^{m-1}<2^{n'}\), so the step gives \(D(n',k)\ge D(n'-1,k)+1\); summing, \(D(n,k)\ge2k+n-m\).

This is the lower half of conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula for every \(k\) (its range \(1\le k<2^{n-1}\) is exactly \(n\ge m(k)\)). At \(k=1\) it is the Alon–Füredi bound \(D(n,1)\ge n\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) for \(n\ge2\), by their own induction; \(n=1\) is immediate. It explains the answer to the co-author's question: below the threshold the multiplicity bound is never attained, because each extra dimension costs a full degree.

The exact value for \(k\le15\)

Working corollary. For every \(1\le k\le15\) and every \(n\ge m(k)\), \(D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\). This settles the conjectured formula for \(k\le15\), combining the lower bound with the small-k upper bound. For example, over \(\mathbb F_2\) and large \(n\): \(D(n,4)=n+4\), \(D(n,8)=n+11\), \(D(n,15)=n+25\), while the real-field answer is \(n+2k-3\).

General statement. The all-parameter results are lem:one-dimension-degree-step and thm:binary-multiplicity-lower-bound; cor:binary-multiplicity-degree-small-k gives the exact value for \(k\le15\). The full formula is now equivalent to conj:power-of-two-factor-existence.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the note's multiplicity deduction (imported bound \((2^n-1)k\le d\,2^{n-1}\)) and this thread's upper-bound theorem. The step was tested on all 16 adjacent computed pairs before drafting. Claim searches for the step's own words ("hyperplane restriction", "discrete derivative", "Alon–Füredi induction") found nothing equivalent; a fresh-context reviewer's two searches also found none. The reviewer checked the linear-form count (including \(\ell=0\)), the coordinate change, steps (a)–(c), the induction range, the base-case arithmetic and the corollary's range, found no gap and no unused hypothesis, and asked for the existence sentence, the \(n=1\) remark and the link to the conjecture, all added.

Assessment. The lower half of the missing implication is proved for all \(k\) by an elementary argument, and the formula is proved for \(k\le15\). The whole question is now the uniform construction of the power-of-two factors \(Q_{2^j}\). The reverse step fails for simple lifts: \(S(x')(1+x_n)+x_nS(x'+v)\) loses at \((v,0)\) or \((0',1)\), where only order \(\ell+1\) is available, so the product vehicle \(\prod(1+x_i)Q\) remains the route. Process: short cycle; testing the step on the table before drafting and one focused review were enough.

Preparation and checkpoint work2 min 25.00 s
Individually measured conversion, checks, and local processing0.14 s

24 September 2026 — Two natural routes to the power-of-two factors fail

Refutation of a multiplicative recursion for j=2,3 and finite checks of two ansätze; the factor conjecture stays open. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The formula is now equivalent to conj:power-of-two-factor-existence: for each \(j\), a factor \(Q_{2^j}\) with value one at the origin, degree \(2^{j+1}-j-2\) and multiplicity \(\ge2^j-w\) at weights \(1\le w<2^j\). This cycle tests the two most natural structured constructions and stops at a construction or precise obstructions.

Observations that shaped the tests

The unique top-degree term of the certificates is the staircase \(m_{(2^j-1,2^{j-1}-1,\ldots,3,1)}\): \(m_{(3,1)}\) in \(Q_4\) and \(m_{(7,3,1)}\) in \(Q_8\), whose size \(\sum_{i\le j}(2^i-1)\) is exactly the degree budget. At the threshold dimension \(j+2\) a factor is available from coding theory: the generator columns \(V\) of the first-order Reed–Muller code \(\mathrm{RM}(1,j+1)=[2^{j+1},j+2,2^j]\), in systematic coordinates, give \(\prod_{v\in V}(1+v\cdot x)=\prod_i(1+x_i)\cdot\prod_{w\in W}(1+w\cdot x)\) with \(|W|=2^{j+1}-j-2\); but this factor lives in \(j+2\) variables and fails at points outside them. A product of hyperplanes of the target degree in dimension \(n\) is a binary \([n+2^{j+1}-j-2,\,n,\,2^j]\) code, exactly at the Griesmer bound, and such codes stop existing as \(n\) grows (no \([9,5,4]\) code); so for large \(n\) the factors are not products of linear forms, as the co-author anticipated. Incidentally \(D(n,2^j)\) coincides with the Griesmer length for dimension \(n\) and distance \(2^j\).

The bit-function ansatz

Since \(e_{2^i}(a)\) is bit \(i\) of \(|a|\) by Lucas, polynomials in \(e_1,e_2,e_4,\ldots\) are the natural candidates for weight-dependent conditions. research/tools/bmd_bit_ansatz.py expands each product \(e_\mu\) in the monomial basis (parity of 0–1 matrices) and imposes the dimension-free conditions: no factor exists in \(\mathbb F_2[e_1,e_2,e_4]\) for \(K=4\) (10 unknowns) or in \(\mathbb F_2[e_1,e_2,e_4,e_8]\) for \(K=8\) (74 unknowns). Indeed \(Q_4\) in the elementary basis involves \(e_3\).

No multiplicative recursion

The staircase suggests \(Q_{2^j}=Q_{2^{j-1}}R_j\) with \(\deg R_j=2^j-1\), the exact degree increase. By multiplicity additivity this works for every choice of \(Q_{2^{j-1}}\) meeting its bounds exactly only if \(R_j(0)\ne0\) and \(\operatorname{mult}_aR_j\ge r_j(|a|)\), where \(r_j(w)=2^{j-1}\) for \(1\le w<2^{j-1}\) and \(r_j(w)=2^j-w\) for \(2^{j-1}\le w<2^j\).

Working proposition. For \(j=2,3\) and every \(n\ge2^j-1\), no polynomial \(R\) (symmetric or not) of degree at most \(2^j-1\) with \(R(0)\ne0\) satisfies these bounds.

Proof. Restrict \(R\) to the coordinate subspace of dimension \(N=2^j-1\); restriction does not lower multiplicity and keeps \(R(0)\ne0\). The multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma gives \(\sum_{a}\operatorname{mult}_a\le N\,2^{N-1}\), while the bounds force \(\sum_{w\ge1}\binom Nw r_j(w)\): \(13>12\) for \(j=2\) and \(470>448\) for \(j=3\). With \(h=2^{j-1}\) the excess is \(\tfrac32\,4^{h-1}-\tfrac{2h-1}2\binom{2h-2}{h-1}-h\) (checked against the direct sums), positive for \(h=2,4\) but negative from \(h=8\), so this counting says nothing for \(j\ge4\). For \(j=4\) the symmetric solver finds no symmetric \(R_4\) (recursive-R.json, 684 unknowns, 2030 conditions).

General statement. The all-parameter claim tested is conj:power-of-two-factor-existence; it is neither proved nor refuted. The obstructions obs:bit-algebra-factor-ansatz-fails and obs:multiplicative-factor-recursion-obstruction exclude two constructions of it.

Verification gate. The counting proof reuses the multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma exactly as in the note (with \(S=\mathbb F_2\)) and the restriction fact already reviewed in the one-dimension step; the sums were computed by script. No new lemma is used elsewhere, so no reviewer pass was run for this negative result.

Assessment. The missing implication (a uniform \(Q_{2^j}\)) is unchanged; two natural routes are closed, and any construction must be additive rather than a product of per-level factors. The line's prospects are uncertain: the certificates exist for \(j\le3\) and the lower bound is sharp there, but no structure beyond the staircase top term is visible. Next, study the solution space itself (its dimension and a canonical element) or look for a non-symmetric additive construction built from the Reed–Muller threshold factor. Process: the ansatz tests were seconds; the symmetric \(R_4\) search took 51 s in Python, acceptable here, but a \(j=5\) test would need the compiled kernel.

24 September 2026 — The factors' low part is forced, and a reverse step reduces to one jet condition

Finite check of forced structure for K=4,8 and a conditional reformulation of the upper half; no construction. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. After the two structured routes failed, determine what every power-of-two factor must look like, and reformulate the missing upper-bound step so that the obligation is a single, testable condition. Stop at forced structure or a construction.

Forced structure of the symmetric factors

research/tools/bmd_factor_space.py computes the full space of symmetric solutions of the dimension-free conditions. For \(K=4\) (degree \(\le4\)) the solutions with constant term one form an affine space of dimension one; for \(K=8\) (degree \(\le11\)) of dimension 30. In both cases the part of degree below \(K\) is forced and equals the truncation of \(\prod_i(1+x_i)^{-1}\), so every symmetric factor gives \(\prod_i(1+x_i)Q=1+(\text{terms of degree}\ge K)\). The top term is forced to be the staircase: \(m_{(3,1)}\) for \(K=4\); for \(K=8\), 25 higher terms are forced, including \(m_{(7,3,1)}\) and every forced term of degree 10 (\(m_{73},m_{721},m_{7111},m_{631},m_{3331}\)). The forced terms alone do not form a factor (14 of 204 conditions fail), so no canonical minimal factor falls out.

A Frobenius shortcut is too strong: for \(K=2^j\), \((x_i+a_i)^K=x_i^K+a_i\), so membership in \((x_1^K+a_1,\ldots,x_n^K+a_n)\) suffices for multiplicity \(K\) at \(a\), but writing \(P=\sum_\beta x^{K\beta}P_\beta\) in base-\(K\) digits turns this into an Alon–Füredi problem in \(y_i=x_i^K\) and forces degree \(nK\).

A reverse step reduces to one jet condition

Conditional lemma (gate incomplete: no fresh-context review yet). Let \(S\) be admissible in dimension \(n-1\) for multiplicity \(K\), of degree \(s\) and origin order \(\ell<K\). Suppose \(C\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) has degree at most \(s-1\), multiplicity at least \(K-1\) at every point \((a',c)\) with \(a'\ne0\), and satisfies \(S(z')+(1+z_n)\,C((0',1)+z)\in(z)^{K-1}\). Then

\[P=S(x')+x_nS(x')+x_n(x_n+1)\,C(x)\]

is admissible in dimension \(n\) with degree at most \(s+1\), so \(D(n,K)\le D(n-1,K)+1\) whenever such \(C\) exists for an extremal \(S\).

Proof. Degree: \(\max(s,s+1,2+\deg C)\le s+1\). At \((a',c)\), \(a'\ne0\): \(P((a',c)+z)=S(a'+z')(1+c+z_n)+(c+z_n)(c+1+z_n)C((a',c)+z)\); the first term has order \(\ge K\) and \((c+z_n)(c+1+z_n)=z_n(1+z_n)\) for both \(c\), so the second has order \(\ge1+(K-1)\). At \((0',1)\): \(P=z_n\bigl[S(z')+(1+z_n)C((0',1)+z)\bigr]\), of order \(\ge1+(K-1)\) by the jet condition. At the origin the \(x_n\)-free part of \(P\) is \(S(z')\), of order \(\ell<K\), which the other terms cannot cancel.

So the upper half of the formula follows from the threshold values (realized for every \(k\) by the Reed–Muller factor times \((x_1^2+x_1)^r\)) if, at each dimension step, a polynomial \(C\) of degree one below \(D(n,K-1)\) exists that is exempt at one point, carries a prescribed jet of order \(\ell\) at another, and has multiplicity \(K-1\) elsewhere. The lower bound does not forbid this, because of the exempt point. The simplest choice \(C=S\,U(x_n)\) fails on degree.

General statement. The all-parameter claim is conj:power-of-two-factor-existence, unchanged; this cycle adds the finite check obs:power-of-two-factor-forced-structure and the conditional reformulation lem:reverse-step-sufficient-condition.

Verification gate. The solution-space computation reuses the dimension-free conditions (validated in the certificate entry). The reverse-step lemma was checked term by term here but not yet reviewed by a fresh-context reader, so it is recorded as conditional.

Assessment. The missing implication is unchanged: no uniform factor. The forced structure narrows what a construction must produce, and the reverse-step reformulation gives a second, independent target. Five consecutive research entries on this route: the next cycle is a route review. Process: all computations took seconds.

24 September 2026 — Route review: the formula holds for every k up to 31; the uniform factor is the one open step

Review with a finite-certificate extension to k≤31, independently rechecked; the upper half for all k stays conjectural. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

The line's general claim. For \(1\le k<2^{n-1}\), \(D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) (conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula). This is the thread's whole goal: it answers the co-author's question of whether the multiplicity bound is tight below the published threshold, and it would give the exact value.

What the goal needs, and what is done. The lower half is proved for every \(k\) by the one-dimension step. The upper half is equivalent, by the power-of-two reduction, to one factor \(Q_{2^j}\) per \(j\) (conj:power-of-two-factor-existence). Certificates existed for \(j\le3\). A second, independent route is a reverse dimension step, reduced to one jet condition (reverse-step condition).

Falsification attempt at \(K=16\)

The factor conjecture could fail first at \(j=4\): degree budget 26, 11,732 partition unknowns and 10,839 dimension-free conditions. The compiled solver research/tools/bmd_factor_kernel.cpp builds each condition row from one generating function per one-position pattern \(b\) (the zero-positions only shift it), sharing prefixes in a depth-first search; it reproduces the Python solver for \(K=8\) exactly (204 rows, kernel dimension 31). For \(K=16\) it finds a symmetric factor with constant term one (rank 8,840, kernel dimension 2,892), a 2,367-term certificate of maximal size 26 (research/results/binary-multiplicity-degree/c6-review/). An independent C++ checker, research/tools/bmd_recheck.cpp, enumerates the conditions directly and counts placements by a memoized dynamic program; it passes the \(K=8\) certificate, fails a copy with one term removed (7 violations), and passes all 10,839 conditions for \(K=16\) (recheck-cpp.txt; the Python reference also passed the 4,138 conditions of weight at most two plus 20 random ones before its full run was refused as too slow).

Working theorem (finite certificates). For every \(n\ge1\) and \(1\le k\le31\), \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\); with the lower bound, \(D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) for every \(k\le31\) and \(n\ge m(k)\). Proof: the certificate lemma applied to the \(K=16\) certificate gives \(Q_{16}\) in every dimension, and the reduction covers \(16\le k\le31\); the earlier certificates cover \(k\le15\).

Assessment

The lower half needed one elementary idea and is complete. The upper half has strong but finite evidence: factors exist for \(j\le4\), their solution spaces grow quickly (kernel dimensions 2, 31, 2,892 for \(K=4,8,16\)), and two natural uniform constructions are excluded (bit functions, multiplicative recursion). The fast growth of the kernel suggests existence might follow from counting: for \(K=16\) the unknowns exceed the conditions by 893 and the kernel is larger still; but a solution also needs the right constant term, which counting alone does not give, and the forced low part shows the conditions are far from independent. The \(j=5\) certificate would need partitions up to size 57, beyond this solver, and would not change the general question. This line's prospects for the general statement are uncertain; its prospects for a paper-worthy result are already good, since the lower bound, the reduction and the exact values for \(k\le31\) answer the co-author's question in the range that matters.

Highest-risk item and next step. Before more construction work, the highest risk to the thread's value is prior art: the lower bound is short, and the characteristic-two degree \(n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) may already be known from work following Sauermann–Wigderson. Next: a bounded literature check (papers citing Sauermann–Wigderson, Clifton–Huang, and characteristic-\(p\) punctured Nullstellensatz results), then an existence argument for the factors, starting from why the low part is forced.

General statement. The line's claim is conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula; this review proves it for \(k\le31\) (cor:binary-multiplicity-degree-through-31) and leaves \(k\ge32\) to conj:power-of-two-factor-existence.

Verification gate. The theorem reuses the reviewed reduction and certificate lemmas; the new ingredient is the \(K=16\) certificate, verified by two independent programs with a negative control. The reverse-step lemma of the previous entry was reviewed by a fresh-context reader: all expansions, the identity \((c+z_n)(c+1+z_n)=z_n(1+z_n)\), the point \((0',1)\) and the degree bound are correct; at the origin the order of \(P\) is \(\min(\ell,1+\operatorname{ord}_0C)\le\ell\), not necessarily \(\ell\), which is all admissibility needs (the recorded statement claims only admissibility). The hypothesis \(\ell<K\) repeats the admissibility of \(S\). The lemma is therefore upgraded from conditional to a working proof. Process: the first rechecker enumerated placements without memoization and timed out; subtracting the zero-positions first and memoizing made the full check take seconds.

24 September 2026 — Prior art: Menezes (2026) proves the formula for n ≥ k−1, and the one-dimension step extends it to the whole range

Imported statement plus conditional theorem: the formula for every k and n ≥ m(k), assuming Menezes' upper bound; the large-n formula is a rediscovery. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The route review named prior art as the highest risk to the thread's value. The check is a bounded literature search for three things: the characteristic-two value \(n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\), the one-dimension step and the power-of-two reduction. It stops at the exact matches and at what they leave open.

The imported result

Two web searches were run: one for vanishing to high order on the hypercube in positive characteristic, following Sauermann–Wigderson, and one for a punctured multiplicity Nullstellensatz in characteristic two. They found Dean Menezes, Characteristic drops for high-order vanishing on the hypercube, arXiv 2609.19009 (submitted 14 August 2026; unrefereed). Its order of vanishing is defined through Hasse derivatives, the coefficients of \(P(a+z)\), which is exactly our multiplicity.

Theorem 1.1 (as stated in the arXiv HTML). Let \(F\) be any field, \(k\ge2\), \(n\ge k-1\) and \(0\le\ell\le k-1\). Let \(\delta_F(n,k,\ell)\) be the least degree of a polynomial of order at least \(k\) at every nonzero vertex of \(\{0,1\}^n\) and order exactly \(\ell\) at the origin. Then

\[ \delta_F(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-\rho_F(k-\ell), \]

where \(\rho_F(s)\) is the least \(r\) such that \(s-1=a_1+\dots+a_r\) with every Catalan number \(C_{a_i-1}\) nonzero in \(F\).

Corollary 1.3 (characteristic two). \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\), where \(s_2\) is the binary digit sum.

The upper bound comes from an explicit basis construction valid for \(n\ge k-1\). For \(\ell=k-1\) the paper also gives a single polynomial that works for every \(n\ge1\). Section 8 remarks that for small \(n\) the hypothesis \(n\ge k-1\) is substantial, because the basis elements cancel. Only the statements were read, not the proofs, so this is recorded as an unrefereed import.

Consequence for this thread. Over \(0\le t\le k-1\) the digit sum \(s_2(t)\) is at most \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\), with equality at \(t=2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}-1\). Minimizing Corollary 1.3 over \(\ell\) therefore gives

\[ D(n,k)=n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\quad\text{for }n\ge k-1. \]

In that range our conjectured formula is a rediscovery. The corollary also gives exactly the minimizing orders we observed in the exact table: \(\{1\}\) for \(k=5\), \(\{0,2\}\) for \(k=6\) and \(\{0,1,3\}\) for \(k=7\).

The range \(n\ge k-1\) misses most of the co-author's question, whose range is \(k<2^{n-1}\), that is \(n\ge m(k)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\). For example, \(k=2^{n-2}\) lies in the question's range, but there \(n=\log_2k+2\) is far below \(k-1\).

The full range from the one-dimension step

Conditional theorem (assuming the upper bound of Menezes' Corollary 1.3 at \(n=k-1\)). For every \(k\ge1\) and \(n\ge m(k)\),

\[ D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor. \]

Proof. For \(k=1\), \(D(n,1)=n\) by Alon–Füredi and \(\prod_i(1+x_i)\). Let \(k\ge2\). The lower bound gives \(D(n,k)-n\ge2k-m(k)\).

  • If \(n\ge k-1\), the consequence above gives equality.
  • If \(m(k)\le n<k-1\), then for every \(n<n'\le k-1\) we have \(k<2^{m(k)-1}\le2^{n'}\). So the one-dimension step applies at each \(n'\), and chaining it gives \(D(n,k)-n\le D(k-1,k)-(k-1)=2k-m(k)\).

Only the upper bound at \(n=k-1\) is imported; the lower bound and the step are this thread's working results. The theorem is unconditional for \(k\le31\) by our certificates.

The per-order step

Working lemma. For \(n'\ge2\), \(k<2^{n'}\) and \(0\le\ell\le k-1\), if \(\delta(n',k,\ell)\) is finite then \(\delta(n'-1,k,\ell)\le\delta(n',k,\ell)-1\).

Proof. Repeat the proof of the one-dimension step with \(P\) attaining \(\delta(n',k,\ell)\) in place of \(D(n',k)\). That proof uses only \(\ell\le k-1<2^{n'}-1\): distinct linear forms over \(\mathbb F_2\) are pairwise coprime, so at most \(\ell\) of them divide the lowest component, and some nonzero form does not. Part (c) shows that the origin order of \(S\) is exactly \(\ell\): \(P(z',0)\) has lowest part of degree \(\ell\), while \(P(z',1)\) has order at least \(k>\ell\). Part (a) holds for formal polynomials: \(\deg S\le\deg P-1\). Minimizing over \(\ell\) gives back the recorded step.

A per-order upper bound below the published range

Conditional corollary (same import). For \(k\ge2\), \(\max(1,\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor)\le n<k-1\) and \(0\le\ell\le k-1\),

\[ \delta(n,k,\ell)\le n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1). \]

Proof. Corollary 1.3 makes \(\delta(k-1,k,\ell)\) finite. For \(n<n'\le k-1\) we have \(n'\ge\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+1\), hence \(k<2^{n'}\), and \(n'\ge2\). Descending induction with the per-order step keeps every \(\delta(n',k,\ell)\) finite and gives the bound.

The corollary is vacuous for \(k\le5\), where \(k-1\le m(k)\). The upper half of the conditional theorem holds by the same chain for \(n\ge\max(1,m(k)-2)\); only the lower bound needs \(n\ge m(k)\). A matching per-order lower bound below \(n=k-1\) is proved here only for the orders that attain \(D\).

Per-order values below the published range

All 69 exactly computed per-order minima in the exact table equal \(n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\): 54 inside Menezes' range and 15 at \((n,k)=(5,7)\) and \((5,8)\), where \(n<k-1\). The \(D(n,k)\) values all agree as well. The comparison is research/tools/bmd_menezes_compare.py, and its output is in research/results/binary-multiplicity-degree/c7-prior-art/per-order-compare.txt. The data support the per-order formula for every \(n\ge m(k)\).

What remains ours

No novelty is claimed for the large-\(n\) formula. The thread's own contributions are:

  • the one-dimension step and its use to reach every \(n\ge m(k)\), which is the co-author's range;
  • the simple lower bound for every \(k\);
  • the power-of-two reduction with dimension-free certificates, which make the formula unconditional for \(k\le31\);
  • the exact small-dimensional table.

Menezes cites Bishnoi et al. on multiplicity subspace covers over \(\mathbb F_2\). Two searches found no other statement of the characteristic-two value, but two searches do not establish novelty for the rest.

General statement. The all-parameter results are thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-all-k-conditional and lem:per-order-dimension-step and thm:binary-multiplicity-per-order-upper-bound-conditional, which rest on imp:menezes-characteristic-drop-degree. The first implies conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula, conditionally.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read from this notebook: the step's statement and proof, including parts (a)–(c), and the lower bound. The imported statements were re-read from the arXiv HTML, including Corollary 1.3, the range of the construction and the Section 8 remark; the proofs were not read. The digit-sum maximum and the table comparison were checked by script. A fresh-context reviewer, given only the two claims and their dependencies, found no gap. It confirmed the chain's hypotheses, the edge cases \(k\le5\) and the exact preservation of the origin order. It asked for three changes, all made: state the per-order step as its own lemma, record why \(\delta\) stays finite along the chain, and widen the corollary's range to \(n\ge\max(1,\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor)\), since \(n\ge m(k)\) was unused there.

Assessment. The thread's goal is now reached for every \(k\): conditionally on one unrefereed import, and unconditionally for \(k\le31\). The thread's independent content is the small-dimensional range, which is where the co-author's question lives. Two steps would remove the conditionality: read Menezes' characteristic-two construction and its proof, and test the construction with our exact verifier at small \((n,k)\), or replace it by a uniform power-of-two factor. How the note cites Menezes, and whether it still aims at a tightness paper, is the user's decision.

24 September 2026 — Menezes' construction works in every dimension: the formula is proved for all k

Working proof: D(n,k)=2k+n−2−⌊log₂k⌋ for all k and n≥m(k); the construction is Menezes', with a short characteristic-two proof. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), default reasoning).

Question and stopping point. The conditional theorem rested on one import: Menezes' upper bound at \(n=k-1\). The missing implication was an upper bound \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-m(k)\) for every \(k\), with a proof checked here. This cycle reads his characteristic-two construction, proves its properties directly and checks it exactly. A failure in the exact check, or a gap in the proof, would have sent the thread back to the power-of-two factors.

The truncated product and its vanishing

Definition (Menezes' Catalan truncation \(\hat g_s\), specialized to \(\mathbb F_2\)). Write \(y_i=x_i^2+x_i=x_i(1+x_i)\), \(F_n=\prod_i(1+x_i)\), and let \(T_s\) be the set of powers of two at most \(s-1\). In characteristic two the Catalan number \(C_{a-1}\) is odd exactly when \(a\) is a power of two, so his polynomial becomes

\[ g_s=\sum_{S\subseteq[n]}\ \sum_{\substack{a\in T_s^S\\ \sum_{i\in S}a_i\le s-1}}\ \prod_{i\in S}y_i^{a_i}\prod_{j\notin S}(1+x_j). \]

Working lemma. For \(s\ge1\) and \(n\ge1\): \(\operatorname{mult}_ag_s\ge s\) at every nonzero \(a\in\{0,1\}^n\), and \(g_s(0)=1\).

Proof. Let \(2^J\le s-1<2^{J+1}\), and put \(N_s(x)=(1+x)+\sum_{j=0}^{J}y^{2^j}\). For \(s=1\) the sum is empty and \(N_1=1+x\).

With \(t=1+x\) we have \(y=t^2+t\), so by Frobenius \(y^{2^j}=t^{2^{j+1}}+t^{2^j}\). The sum telescopes to \(t+t^{2^{J+1}}\), hence \(N_s=(1+x)^{2^{J+1}}\) with \(2^{J+1}\ge s\). This is the characteristic-two case of Menezes' Lemma 3.1, \(N_s\equiv0\pmod{(x-1)^s}\), which he proves from the Catalan series.

Expanding \(\prod_iN_s(x_i)\) chooses, for each \(i\), either \(1+x_i\) or \(y_i^{a_i}\) with \(a_i\in\{1,2,\dots,2^J\}=T_s\). So \(\prod_iN_s(x_i)=g_s+R\), where \(R\) collects the terms with \(\sum a_i\ge s\).

  • By facts (A) and (C) of the power-of-two reduction, each term of \(R\) has multiplicity at least \(\sum a_i\ge s\) at every cube point, so \(R\) does too.
  • \(\prod_iN_s(x_i)=F_n^{2^{J+1}}\) has multiplicity \(2^{J+1}|a|\ge s\) at nonzero \(a\), by (A) and (B).

So \(g_s=F_n^{2^{J+1}}+R\) has multiplicity at least \(s\) at nonzero \(a\). At the origin every term with \(S\ne\emptyset\) vanishes, and the term with \(S=\emptyset\) is \(F_n(0)=1\).

Degree. \(\deg g_s\le n+2(s-1)-s_2(s-1)\), with equality when \(n\ge s_2(s-1)\).

Proof. The term \((S,a)\) has degree \(2\sigma+n-|S|\), where \(\sigma=\sum a_i\), and leading monomial \(\prod_{i\in S}x_i^{2a_i}\prod_{j\notin S}x_j\). Writing \(\sigma\) as a sum of \(|S|\) powers of two needs \(|S|\ge s_2(\sigma)\). The function \(\sigma\mapsto2\sigma-s_2(\sigma)\) is strictly increasing, so every term has degree at most \(n+2(s-1)-s_2(s-1)\).

The terms of exactly that degree put the binary digits of \(s-1\) on distinct coordinates. Their leading monomials are pairwise distinct, because \((S,a)\) can be read off from the exponents that are at least 2. Such a term exists exactly when \(n\ge s_2(s-1)\).

The per-order construction

Working theorem. For all \(n\ge1\), \(k\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell\le k-1\), the polynomial \(P=y_1^\ell\,g_{k-\ell}\) has:

  • multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point;
  • origin order exactly \(\ell\);
  • degree at most \(n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\) (equality when \(n\ge s_2(k-\ell-1)\); only the inequality is used).

Hence \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\le n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\) in every dimension.

Proof. By (A), (C) and the lemma, \(\operatorname{mult}_aP\ge\ell+(k-\ell)\) at nonzero \(a\). At the origin \(y_1(z)=z_1+z_1^2\) has order 1 and \(g_{k-\ell}(0)=1\), so the order is \(\ell\). The degree is \(2\ell\) plus the degree bound.

Menezes states his construction for \(n\ge k-1\), the range his degree formula needs. The argument above uses no condition on \(n\), so the upper bound holds in every dimension. This supersedes the conditional per-order bound.

The formula for every k

Working theorem. For every \(k\ge1\) and \(n\ge m(k)\), that is, the whole range \(k<2^{n-1}\) of the co-author's question,

\[ D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor. \]

Proof. For the upper bound, take \(\ell=k-2^J\in[0,k-1]\) with \(J=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) in the per-order construction; then \(s_2(2^J-1)=J\), the largest digit sum below \(k\). This bound, \(D(n,k)\le n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\), holds for every \(n\ge1\). Only the recorded lower bound needs \(n\ge m(k)\).

This proves conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula. It supersedes the conditional theorem, which is no longer needed, and extends the certificate range \(k\le31\) to every \(k\).

The power-of-two factors

Working corollary. For every \(j\ge0\) and every \(n\), \(Q_{2^j}=g_{2^j}/F_n\) is a polynomial with \(Q(0)=1\) (so \(\operatorname{mult}_0Q=0<2^j\)), degree at most \(2^{j+1}-j-2\), and \(\operatorname{mult}_aQ\ge2^j-|a|\) at every nonzero \(a\).

Over \(\mathbb F_2\), for a power of two \(a\), \(x^a(1+x)^{a-1}=x^a+x^{a+1}+\dots+x^{2a-1}\), because every \(b\le a-1\) is a bit-submask of \(a-1\). So \(Q_{2^j}\) is a truncation of the geometric series \(\prod_i(1+x_i)^{-1}=\prod_i\sum_{e\ge0}x_i^e\):

\[ Q_{2^j}=\sum_{e\,:\,\sum_{e_i>0}2^{\lfloor\log_2e_i\rfloor}\le2^j-1}x^e, \]

where the cost of a coordinate is the power of two at or below its exponent.

So conj:power-of-two-factor-existence holds, and \(F_nQ_{2^j}y_1^r\) is the per-order construction with \(\ell=r\).

Proof. Every term of \(g_{2^j}\) contains \(1+x_i\) for each \(i\): through \(y_i^{a_i}\) when \(i\in S\), and directly otherwise. The multiplicity bound is (A) and (B) applied to \(g=F_nQ\).

This explains the forced structure found by search. The low part is the truncation of \(\prod_i(1+x_i)^{-1}\), since every exponent vector of degree below \(2^j\) has cost below \(2^j\). The staircase term \(m_{(2^j-1,\dots,3,1)}\) is the top-degree term, with cost \(2^{j-1}+\dots+1\).

Exact checks

  • Polynomials. research/tools/bmd_catalan_construction.py built \(P=y_1^\ell g_{k-\ell}\) for every \(2\le k\le12\), \(\max(1,\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor)\le n\le7\) and \(0\le\ell\le k-1\): 417 cases, 305 of them below Menezes' range \(n<k-1\). The compiled kernel's shift-based verify checked each one, cross-checked by direct evaluation on the small cases. All 417 have multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point, origin order exactly \(\ell\) and degree equal to the prediction. Minimizing over \(\ell\) gives \(2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) for all 41 pairs with \(n\ge m(k)\). The results are in research/results/binary-multiplicity-degree/c8-construction/ (cases.jsonl, summary.txt, the factor certificates catalan-Q*.txt and recheck.txt).
  • Factors. research/tools/bmd_catalan_certificate.py wrote \(Q_4,Q_8,Q_{16}\) as symmetric certificates with 8, 72 and 1,824 terms. The independent placement checker bmd_recheck passes all of them: 11, 204 and 10,839 conditions, so they are valid in every dimension. Both differ from the searched certificates, which are other points of the solution space: \(Q_8\) in 5 terms and \(Q_{16}\) in 1,275.

What is Menezes' and what is ours. The construction is Menezes' (arXiv 2609.19009, Section 3), and the upper bound for \(n\ge k-1\) is his theorem. This thread contributes:

  • the telescoping proof in characteristic two, and the observation that the construction needs no condition on \(n\);
  • the lower bound for \(m(k)\le n<k-1\), where Menezes' formula is not stated, via the one-dimension step;
  • the identification of the construction with the power-of-two factors.

Together these answer the co-author's question in full. No novelty is claimed for the construction.

General statement. thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula (every \(k\), \(n\ge m(k)\)), built on thm:catalan-truncation-per-order-construction and lem:catalan-truncation-vanishing; cor:power-of-two-factor-construction settles conj:power-of-two-factor-existence.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read from this notebook: facts (A)–(C) and the reduction lemma, the lower bound and its range; Menezes' definitions of \(\hat g_s\) and \(N_s\) and his Lemma 3.1 from the arXiv HTML. The claim searches for the truncation, the product and the power-of-two factors found the recorded forced-structure check, now explained, and the odd-field thread's truncated-lift counterfamily for \(3\le k\le q\), which is vacuous at \(q=2\). Neither implies the construction. Checks run: the 417 exact polynomial verifications and the three all-dimension factor rechecks above. A fresh-context reviewer found no gap. It checked the telescoping, the split \(\prod N_s=g_s+R\), the multiplicity of \(R\), degree equality, the exact origin order, divisibility and the cases \(s=1\), \(j=0\), and recomputed \(g_s\) for \(n\le3\), \(s\le6\). Its notes are applied: the upper bound holds for every \(n\), the equality clause is not used, and \(\operatorname{mult}_0Q=0\).

Assessment. Route item low-origin-degree is complete: the formula holds for every \(k\) and every \(n\) in the question's range, with a self-contained proof of about a page. The multiplicity bound, the one-dimension step and the Catalan truncation suffice; the imported statement is no longer used. Two items remain: the per-order lower bound for \(n<k-1\), which the goal does not need, and the note-revision decision, which is the user's.

24 September 2026 — Formalization route for the degree formula, down to multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel

Formalization plan: dependency route for thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula; no Lean written. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. The user assigned formalization of the formula theorem under the Spin-formalize-parallel protocol. The user asked for the planning phase only: no partition and no assignments. The user also asked that the input be formalized too. The planning phase ends with a dependency route that reaches every result the proof uses, down to the imported multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma. The route is saved in formalization/BINARY_MULTIPLICITY_DEGREE_FORMALIZATION_ROUTE.md.

Target statement. The Lean target states the minimum as two conjuncts. (i) Every admissible \(P\) has total degree at least \(2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) when \(n\ge m(k)\). (ii) For every \(n\ge1\), some admissible \(P\) has total degree at most that value. Multiplicity is defined as the least total degree in the support of \(P(a+z)\), and \(\infty\) for \(P=0\). So the origin condition \(\operatorname{mult}_0P<k\) excludes \(P=0\), as in the notebook.

Dependency trace. The proof uses five ingredients, which the route orders into fifteen obligations:

  • the lower bound;
  • the one-dimension step;
  • the note's deduction, with its imported lemma: Dvir–Kopparty–Saraf–Sudan, Lemma 2.7, which is not yet indexed and gets a new third-party claim when formalized;
  • the truncated product;
  • the per-order construction.

Supporting obligations extracted from these proofs:

  • multiplicity foundations and product superadditivity, with equality over a domain;
  • the elementary factor bounds;
  • multivariate Hasse coefficient polynomials and the univariate root-multiplicity bridge, needed only by Schwartz–Zippel;
  • invertible linear substitutions over \(\mathbb F_2\);
  • a linear form not dividing a low-degree homogeneous polynomial, via unique factorization;
  • binary digit arithmetic.

Two results the proof does not use need no formalization: Alon–Füredi (the case \(k=1\) follows from the deduction at \(n=2\)) and Menezes' theorem (the construction is proved independently). The degree drop of \(P(x',0)+P(x',1)\) is a case of the existing binary cube degree drop module, which is to be checked for fit. No local Mathlib checkout exists, so every Mathlib name in the route is marked provisional.

Remaining route and process assessment. Nothing is formalized yet. All fifteen obligations remain, and the heaviest is expected to be the Schwartz–Zippel induction with its multivariate Hasse coefficients. Process: the protocol text is in PROMPTS.md, which nothing in the instructions or the resume guide referenced, so the search first went through skills and formalization guides. A one-line pointer in CLAUDE.md fixes this, committed separately.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma, over every field

Formalized: DKSS Lemma 2.7 for every field, finite S and nonzero P (route item M06, with M01 and M05). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. The user asked for the Formalize protocol, applied sequentially to the three highest-risk items of the route. The first is M06, the imported multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma, which the note's deduction and the thread's lower bound rest on. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof of the full statement, with the multiplicity foundations it needs (M01) and the univariate root count (M05).

The formalized statement

Source. Dvir, Kopparty, Saraf and Sudan, Extensions to the method of multiplicities, SICOMP 2013, Lemma 2.7. The same result is Lemma 8 in arXiv 0901.2529v2, whose statement and proof were read for this cycle. Their Definition 3 sets \(\operatorname{mult}(P,a)\) to the largest \(M\) with \(P^{(i)}(a)=0\) for all \(\operatorname{wt}(i)<M\), and \(\infty\) if there is no largest. Here \(P^{(i)}(a)\) is the coefficient of \(Z^i\) in \(P(a+Z)\). Lemma 8 states: for a nonzero \(P\in F[X_1,\dots,X_n]\) of total degree at most \(d\) and a finite \(S\subseteq F\), \(\sum_{a\in S^n}\operatorname{mult}(P,a)\le d\,|S|^{n-1}\).

Lean. MathResearch.HasseMultiplicity.mult a P is the order in \(\mathbb N_\infty\) of the power series of \(P(a+z)\): the least total degree of a nonzero coefficient, and \(\top\) for \(P=0\). This is their definition, since their \(P^{(i)}(a)\) is exactly that coefficient. The theorem MultiplicitySchwartzZippel.multiplicity_schwartz_zippel covers every field \(F\), every finite variable type \(\sigma\) with \(|\sigma|\ge1\), every finite \(S\subseteq F\) and every nonzero \(P\):

\[ \sum_{a\in S^\sigma}\operatorname{mult}_a(P)\le\deg P\cdot|S|^{|\sigma|-1}\quad\text{in }\mathbb N_\infty. \]

This is the source statement with \(d=\deg P\); a larger \(d\) follows trivially. The scaled form multiplicity_schwartz_zippel_scaled, \(|S|\sum\operatorname{mult}\le\deg P\cdot|S|^{|\sigma|}\), also holds with no variables. The induction uses it, so that no natural-number subtraction appears.

The proof, as formalized

The formal proof is theirs, with one substitution. They apply the Hasse derivative \(P^{(i,0)}\) and restrict it to a line; the formal proof takes a coefficient slice instead, which is the same polynomial and avoids multivariate Hasse derivatives. The complete argument follows.

Induction. The induction runs over finite types, empty or of the form \(\mathrm{Option}\,\sigma\), up to equivalence.

  • Equivalence step. Renaming variables along an equivalence \(e\) preserves total degree. It also preserves multiplicity: \(\operatorname{mult}_a(\operatorname{rename}_eP)=\operatorname{mult}_{a\circ e}P\), because nonzero coefficients of the renamed shift come from coefficients of the same degree. It permutes the points of \(S^\sigma\).
  • No variables. A nonzero constant has multiplicity 0, so the scaled sum is 0.
  • Option step. Write \(P=\sum_{j\le t}P_j(x')\,x_0^j\) with \(P_t\ne0\), where \(x_0\) is the new variable (Mathlib's optionEquivLeft). Then \(\deg P_t+t\le\deg P\).

The line bound. Fix \(a'\in S^\sigma\) and choose a monomial \(z'^\beta\) of \(P_t(a'+z')\) of least degree, so \(|\beta|=\operatorname{mult}_{a'}P_t\). Let \(g\in F[y]\) be the coefficient of \(z'^\beta\) in \(P(a'+z',y)\). Its \(y^j\)-coefficient is the \(z'^\beta\)-coefficient of \(P_j(a'+z')\). So \(g\ne0\), its \(y^t\)-coefficient is nonzero, and \(\deg g=t\).

For \(c\in F\) put \(r=\operatorname{mult}_c g\), which equals the trailing degree of \(g(c+w)\). Shifting \(x_0\) by \(c\) commutes with taking the \(z'^\beta\)-coefficient: this is the lemma sliceMap_taylor, checked on monomials using \(\binom{n}{r}c^{n-r}\). Hence the coefficient of \(z'^\beta w^r\) in \(P((a',c)+(z',w))\) is the \(w^r\)-coefficient of \(g(c+w)\), which is nonzero. Therefore \(\operatorname{mult}_{(a',c)}P\le|\beta|+\operatorname{mult}_c g\).

Summing over \(c\in S\), and using that the root multiplicities of \(g\) in \(S\) sum to at most \(\deg g=t\):

\[ \sum_{c\in S}\operatorname{mult}_{(a',c)}P\le|S|\operatorname{mult}_{a'}P_t+t. \]

Summing over \(a'\). With the induction hypothesis \(|S|\sum_{a'}\operatorname{mult}_{a'}P_t\le\deg P_t\,|S|^{|\sigma|}\) and \(|S^\sigma|=|S|^{|\sigma|}\):

\[ |S|\sum_{a}\operatorname{mult}_aP\le|S|\deg P_t|S|^{|\sigma|}+t|S|^{|\sigma|+1}\le\deg P\,|S|^{|\sigma|+1}. \]

Unscaled form. Every multiplicity is finite because \(P\ne0\). If \(S=\emptyset\), the sum is empty because \(\sigma\) is nonempty. Otherwise divide by \(|S|\).

Files. claims/HasseMultiplicity.lean is the supporting module, with 21 audited declarations. It contains the shift and multiplicity, the characterization by coefficients below \(n\), sums, products (superadditive, and additive over a domain through Mathlib's MvPowerSeries.order_mul), the value criterion \(\operatorname{mult}=0\iff P(a)\ne0\), and renaming. third-party-claims/MultiplicitySchwartzZippel.lean has 8 audited declarations. Both pass verify.sh with only the standard axioms; the reports are in research/results/bmd-f1-schwartz-zippel/.

Verification gate. The theorem types printed by the verifier were compared with Lemma 8 of arXiv 0901.2529v2 and its Definition 3. The field, the finite \(S\), \(P\ne0\), the degree and the exponent \(|\sigma|-1\) all match. The extra hypotheses are \(|\sigma|\ge1\) and a decidable-equality instance on \(\sigma\). The first is implicit in the source's \(n\) and is dropped in the scaled form. The second is needed to form \(S^\sigma\) as a finite set, and every finite type has one classically. The multiplicity definition was checked against the source's: both are the least weight of a nonzero coefficient of \(P(a+Z)\).

Remaining route and process assessment. M06 is complete, and with it the parts of M01 and M05 it needs. The route's M04 (multivariate Hasse derivatives) is no longer needed and is removed. The two remaining high-risk items are M09 (a linear form not dividing a low-degree form) and M13 (the truncated product). The note's deduction M07 is now short arithmetic on top of this theorem. Process: the first compile failed because the copied Mathlib package lacked some compiled modules. The documented cache command fixed that in seconds, and it should be run for every new import set before compiling.

24 September 2026 — Lean: a linear form avoiding a low-degree polynomial, and straightening it to a coordinate

Formalized: new helper lemma lem:binary-linear-form-avoidance (route item M09), extracted from the one-dimension step. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This is the second of the three highest-risk items of the route. The one-dimension step needs a coordinate \(x_n\) that does not divide the lowest component \(L\) at the origin. Its proof says that at most \(\ell\le2^n-2\) distinct linear forms divide \(L\), and then changes coordinates. This cycle states and verifies both facts. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof.

The lemma

Working lemma (lem:binary-linear-form-avoidance). Let \(\sigma\) be a finite set of variables, and write \(\lambda_v=\sum_iv_ix_i\) for the linear form of a vector \(v\).

  1. If \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_\sigma]\) is nonzero with total degree below \(2^{|\sigma|}-1\), then some nonzero \(v\in\mathbb F_2^\sigma\) has \(\lambda_v\nmid P\).
  2. Over any field \(F\), for every nonzero \(v\in F^\sigma\) and every variable \(x_{i_0}\), there is an invertible matrix \(A\) such that the substitution \(x_i\mapsto\sum_jA_{ij}x_j\) sends \(\lambda_v\) to \(x_{i_0}\).

Proof of (1).

  • \(F[x_\sigma]\) is a unique factorization domain (Mathlib).
  • A nonzero \(\lambda_v\) has total degree 1, and the gcd of its coefficients is a unit, so it is irreducible (Mathlib's irreducible_of_totalDegree_eq_one) and hence prime.
  • Over \(\mathbb F_2\) the only unit of \(\mathbb F_2[x_\sigma]\) is \(1\): a unit is a constant unit, and \(\mathbb F_2^\times=\{1\}\). So distinct forms are not associated.
  • The forms are distinct for distinct \(v\), because the coefficient of \(x_i\) in \(\lambda_v\) is \(v_i\).

If all \(2^{|\sigma|}-1\) nonzero forms divided \(P\), their product would divide \(P\) (Finset.prod_primes_dvd, which needs exactly this uniqueness of units). Degrees add in a domain, so the product has total degree \(2^{|\sigma|}-1\). Writing \(P=\prod\lambda_v\cdot c\) with \(c\ne0\) then gives \(\deg P\ge2^{|\sigma|}-1\), a contradiction.

Proof of (2). The substitution sends \(\lambda_v\) to \(\lambda_{vA}\), where \(vA\) is the row vector \(v\) times \(A\). Extend the isomorphism \(F v\cong F e_{i_0}\) between the two lines to a linear automorphism \(g\) of \(F^\sigma\), which exists in finite dimension. Put \(A=(\text{matrix of }g)^{\mathsf T}\). Then \(vA=g(v)=e_{i_0}\), and \(A\) is invertible because \(\det g\) is a unit.

Use in the step. There \(L\) is nonzero with degree \(\ell\le k-1<2^n-1\). Part (1) gives a form not dividing \(L\), and part (2) makes it the split coordinate. The substitution's effect on degree and multiplicity is route item M08, left for the step's own cycle.

Files. claims/LinearFormAvoidance.lean has 10 audited declarations and passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f2-linear-form/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. lem:binary-linear-form-avoidance, for every finite variable set.

Verification gate. The statement was compared with the step's proof, as re-read from this notebook: the degree bound \(\ell\le2^n-2\), then a coordinate change making the chosen form a coordinate. The printed Lean types state exactly (1) and (2), with no extra hypotheses beyond a finite variable type and decidable equality for (2). A claim search for linear forms dividing the lowest component found only the step itself. The proof is checked by Lean, so no separate reviewer pass was run.

Remaining route and process assessment. M09 is complete; M08 (the substitution's effect on degree and multiplicity) remains for the step. The third high-risk item, M13 (the truncated product), is next. Process: every Mathlib ingredient existed, and the only friction was deprecation warnings, which verification treats as errors. No framework change is needed.
