24 September 2026 — Lean: the truncated product vanishes to order s off the origin

Formalized: lem:catalan-truncation-vanishing for every s and every finite variable set (route item M13, with M03 and M12). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This is the third of the three highest-risk route items. It is the truncated product lemma behind the upper bound. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof of the indexed statement. Over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(g_s\) has multiplicity at least \(s\) at every nonzero point, \(g_s(0)=1\), and \(\deg g_s\le n+2(s-1)-s_2(s-1)\).

The formalized statement

Encoding of \(g_s\). In Lean, \(g_s\) is a sum over choice maps \(c:\sigma\to\mathrm{Option}\,(\mathrm{Fin}\,s)\) of cost \(\sum_{c_i=j}2^j\le s-1\), of the products \(\prod_if_i(c_i)\), where \(f_i(\text{none})=1+x_i\) and \(f_i(j)=y_i^{2^j}\). These maps correspond exactly to the notebook's pairs \((S,a)\) with every \(a_i\) a power of two at most \(s-1\) and \(\sum a_i\le s-1\): take \(i\in S\) and \(a_i=2^j\) when \(c_i=j\). The bound \(j<s\) is automatic, because \(2^j\le s-1<2^s\). The digit sum \(s_2\) is the sum of Mathlib's binary digits. The theorem TruncatedProduct.truncated_product holds for every \(s\) and every finite \(\sigma\), with \(n=|\sigma|\).

The proof, as formalized

The proof is the notebook's, with one simplification: \(j\) runs over all of \(\mathrm{Fin}\,s\), not only over \(2^j\le s-1\).

Telescoping. In characteristic two, \(\sum_{j<K}(t^2+t)^{2^j}=t^{2^K}+t\), by Frobenius and induction. With \(t=1+x\) we have \(t^2+t=x^2+x=y\), so \((1+x)+\sum_{j<s}y^{2^j}=(1+x)^{2^s}\).

Expansion. By Finset.prod_univ_sum, \(\prod_i(1+x_i)^{2^s}\) is the sum of the products \(\prod_if_i(c_i)\) over all choice maps. Splitting by cost gives \(g_s+R\), where \(R\) collects the maps of cost at least \(s\). This includes every map that uses some \(2^j>s-1\).

Multiplicity off the origin.

  • Each term of \(R\). Every \(y_i\) has multiplicity at least 1 at every point of \(\mathbb F_2^\sigma\), since \(a^2+a=0\) on \(\mathbb F_2\). Multiplicity is superadditive on products and powers. So each term of \(R\) has multiplicity at least its cost, which is at least \(s\).
  • \(R\) itself. A sum has multiplicity at least the minimum over its terms, so \(R\) also has multiplicity at least \(s\).
  • The product. At a nonzero \(a\), pick \(a_{i_0}=1\). Then \(1+x_{i_0}\) vanishes at \(a\), so \((1+x_{i_0})^{2^s}\) has multiplicity at least \(2^s\ge s\), and so does the product.

In characteristic two \(g_s\) equals the product plus \(R\), so \(g_s\) has multiplicity at least \(s\).

Value at the origin. Only the all-\(\text{none}\) map survives evaluation at \(0\), and its term is \(\prod(1+0)=1\).

Degree. A term has degree at most \((n-|S|)+2\cdot\mathrm{cost}\). Writing the cost as a sum of \(|S|\) powers of two forces \(|S|\ge s_2(\mathrm{cost})\), by \(s_2(a+b)\le s_2(a)+s_2(b)\) and \(s_2(2^j)=1\). The map \(\sigma\mapsto2\sigma-s_2(\sigma)\) is monotone, stated without subtraction as \(2a+s_2(b)\le2b+s_2(a)\) for \(a\le b\) and proved from \(s_2(m+1)\le s_2(m)+1\). With cost at most \(s-1\), this gives the bound. The digit-sum facts are proved by strong induction on \(s_2(m)=m\bmod2+s_2(\lfloor m/2\rfloor)\) (route item M12). Mathlib has none of them.

Files. claims/TruncatedProduct.lean has 13 audited declarations and imports the multiplicity foundations. It passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms; the report is in research/results/bmd-f3-truncated-product/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. lem:catalan-truncation-vanishing, now formalized in full.

Verification gate. The indexed statement and the notebook's lemma and proof were re-read before drafting. The printed Lean type states the three conclusions for every \(s\), which also covers the claim's range \(s\ge1\). The encoding of \(g_s\) was checked against the notebook's sum over \((S,a)\), as above. The notebook's degree-equality clause was never part of the indexed claim, is not used downstream, and is not formalized. The proof is checked by Lean.

Remaining route and process assessment. The three highest-risk items are done: M06, M09 and M13, with M01, M03, M05 and M12 as far as they need. What remains for the target is short:

  • M07, the note's arithmetic;
  • M08, the substitution's degree and multiplicity;
  • M10, the step;
  • M11, the lower bound;
  • M14, \(y_1^\ell g_{k-\ell}\);
  • M15, the assembly.

Process: this module needed three rounds of API fixes, mostly renamed lemmas and a missing instance import, and no mathematical change. No framework change is needed.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the note's degree bound for the multiplicity question

Formalized: audit:binary-multiplicity-cover-question, the note's deduction from multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel (route item M07). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. The user asked for the rest of the route to be formalized sequentially, bottom up. This cycle does M07, the binary multiplicity note's deduction, which the lower bound uses at \(n=m(k)\). The endpoint was a verified Lean proof of the indexed statement.

The formalized statement and proof

Statement (BinaryMultiplicityCoverBound.cover_bound). For \(n\ge1\), every \(k\), and every nonzero \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\dots,x_n]\) with multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point:

\[ \deg P\ge2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor. \]

The variant cover_bound_of_origin assumes the question's condition \(\operatorname{mult}_0P<k\) in place of \(P\ne0\).

Proof. This is the note's proof.

  • The \(2^n-1\) nonzero points contribute at least \(k(2^n-1)\) to \(\sum_{a\in\mathbb F_2^n}\operatorname{mult}_aP\).
  • The multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma with \(S=\mathbb F_2\) bounds that sum by \(d\,2^{n-1}\), where \(d=\deg P\).
  • The integer step: with \(m=2^{n-1}\) and \(q=\lfloor k/m\rfloor\), if \(d+q<2k\) then \(dm+(q+1)m\le2km\). Since \((q+1)m>k\), this gives \(dm<2km-k=k(2m-1)\), a contradiction.

Files. claims/BinaryMultiplicityCoverBound.lean passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f4-cover-bound/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. audit:binary-multiplicity-cover-question, now formalized for all positive \(n\) and all \(k\).

Verification gate. The note's Proposition 2.1 and proof (publications/binary-polynomial-multiplicity/sections/02-deduction.tex) were re-read. The Lean type matches it, with \(k\) unrestricted and \(P\ne0\) as the only use of the origin condition, as the note remarks. The Schwartz–Zippel input is the verified third-party claim.

Remaining route and process assessment. The lower-bound branch now needs M08 and M10 (the substitution facts and the step), then M11. The upper-bound branch needs M14, and then M15 assembles both. Process: the only friction was the quoted import syntax for the hyphenated directory. It is documented in the formalization README, and I did not read that part first. No framework change is needed.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the one-dimension step, keeping the origin order

Formalized: lem:one-dimension-degree-step and lem:per-order-dimension-step, with the substitution facts (route items M08, M10). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This cycle continues the bottom-up formalization of the route. The target is the one-dimension step in the order-preserving form of the per-order step, together with the facts about invertible linear substitutions it needs. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof.

The formalized statement

Statement (OneDimensionStep.one_dimension_step). Let \(\sigma\) be finite, \(n=|\sigma|+1\) and \(k<2^n\). Let \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_{\mathrm{Option}\,\sigma}]\) have multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and origin order below \(k\). Then some \(S\in\mathbb F_2[x_\sigma]\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point, the same origin order as \(P\), and \(\deg S+1\le\deg P\).

Applied to a \(P\) of least degree, this gives \(D(n-1,k)\le D(n,k)-1\). Applied to a \(P\) of least degree with a given origin order \(\ell\), it gives \(\delta(n-1,k,\ell)\le\delta(n,k,\ell)-1\). The statement also holds for \(n=1\), which the claims exclude.

The proof, as formalized

Substitution facts (M08). Let \(x_i\mapsto\sum_jA_{ij}x_j\) be a linear substitution.

  • It maps a homogeneous polynomial of degree \(d\) to one of degree \(d\) (Mathlib's IsHomogeneous.aeval). Hence it commutes with taking homogeneous components, and it does not raise total degree.
  • Substitutions compose as matrix products, so an invertible \(A\) gives an injective substitution.
  • The origin order is the least \(j\) with a nonzero component of degree \(j\). So an invertible substitution preserves it.
  • Shifting commutes with substitution: \(\operatorname{shift}_a(P\circ A)=(\operatorname{shift}_{Aa}P)\circ A\). Hence \(\operatorname{mult}_a(P\circ A)=\operatorname{mult}_{Aa}P\), and \(A\) permutes the nonzero points.

Choosing coordinates. Let \(\ell\) be the origin order and \(L\) the degree-\(\ell\) component, which is nonzero with \(\ell\le k-1<2^n-1\).

  • The linear-form avoidance lemma gives a nonzero form \(\lambda_v\nmid L\), and an invertible \(A\) with \(\lambda_v\circ A=x_{\text{none}}\).
  • Put \(Q=P\circ A\). Its degree-\(\ell\) component is \(L\circ A\).
  • If \(x_{\text{none}}\mid L\circ A\), then substituting back with \(A^{-1}\) would give \(\lambda_v\mid L\). So \(x_{\text{none}}\nmid L\circ A\).

The core (step_core). Let \(Q'\) be \(Q\) viewed as a polynomial in \(x_{\text{none}}\), and write \(r_c(Q)=Q'(c)\). Put \(S=r_0(Q)+r_1(Q)\).

  • Restriction. Restricting to \(x_{\text{none}}=c\) never lowers multiplicity: \(\operatorname{mult}_{(c,a')}Q\le\operatorname{mult}_{a'}r_c(Q)\). The reason is that \(\operatorname{shift}_{a'}r_c(Q)=r_0(\operatorname{shift}_{(c,a')}Q)\), and the coefficients of \(r_0\) are coefficients of \(Q\) of the same degree. So \(S\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero \(a'\).
  • Origin order, lower bound. \(\operatorname{mult}_0S\ge\min(\ell,k)=\ell\), since \((1,0)\) is a nonzero point.
  • Origin order, upper bound. \(r_0(L\circ A)\) is the constant coefficient of \(L\circ A\) in \(x_{\text{none}}\), and it is nonzero because \(x_{\text{none}}\nmid L\circ A\). Any nonzero coefficient \(z^\beta\) of it has degree \(\ell\) and appears in \(S\), because \(r_1(Q)\) has no terms of degree below \(k\). So \(\operatorname{mult}_0S\le\ell\).
  • Degree. \(S=\sum_{i\ge1}Q'_i\), since the \(i=0\) terms cancel in characteristic two. Mathlib's bound gives \(\deg Q'_i+i\le\deg Q\), so \(\deg S\le\deg Q-1\). Also \(\deg Q\ge1\), because otherwise \(S=0\), contradicting \(\operatorname{mult}_0S=\ell\).

This is the notebook's proof with the lowest-component argument spelled out. It needs no use of the reduction modulo \(x_i^2-x_i\).

Files. claims/OneDimensionStep.lean passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f5-dimension-step/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. lem:one-dimension-degree-step and lem:per-order-dimension-step, both covered by one Lean theorem for every finite variable set.

Verification gate. The step's statement and proof, parts (a)–(c), and the per-order lemma were re-read from this notebook. The printed Lean type states the order-preserving step for every \(n\ge1\) and \(k<2^n\). Both indexed claims follow from it by minimizing degree, without extra hypotheses. The proof is checked by Lean.

Remaining route and process assessment. The lower bound M11 is next. It iterates this step from \(n=m(k)\) and needs relabelling a set of \(n\) variables as \(\mathrm{Option}\) of \(n-1\). After it come M14 and the assembly M15. Process: several Mathlib lemmas on MvPolynomial coefficients are now deprecated in favour of AddMonoidAlgebra ones, which cost two compile rounds. No framework change is needed.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the lower bound for every k

Formalized: thm:binary-multiplicity-lower-bound for every k and n ≥ m(k) (route item M11). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This cycle continues the bottom-up formalization of the route with the lower bound, the lower half of the formula. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof.

The formalized statement and proof

Statement (BinaryMultiplicityLowerBound.lower_bound). For every \(k\) and \(n\ge\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\), every \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\dots,x_n]\) with multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and origin order below \(k\) satisfies

\[ \deg P\ge2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor. \]

The claim's hypothesis \(k\ge1\) is not needed: for \(k=0\) the origin condition cannot hold.

Proof. This is the notebook's proof, by induction on \(n\) from \(m=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\).

  • Base. At \(n=m\), the note's deduction gives \(\deg P\ge2k-\lfloor k/2^{m-1}\rfloor\). Here \(k<2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+1}=2^{m-1}\), so the floor is 0.
  • Step. For \(n+1\) variables, relabel them as \(\mathrm{Option}\) of \(n\) (Mathlib's finSuccEquiv). Relabelling preserves multiplicities at the correspondingly relabelled points, the origin order and the total degree. Then apply the verified step, which is allowed because \(k<2^{m-1}\le2^{n+1}\). The induction hypothesis applies to its output \(S\), and \(\deg S+1\le\deg P\).

Files. claims/BinaryMultiplicityLowerBound.lean passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f6-lower-bound/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. thm:binary-multiplicity-lower-bound, now formalized with all its dependencies: the step, the linear-form avoidance lemma, the note's deduction, and multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel.

Verification gate. The theorem's statement and proof were re-read from this notebook. The printed Lean type states the bound for \(n\ge m(k)\) with the notebook's hypotheses, and with \(k\ge1\) dropped because it is not used. The proof is checked by Lean.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the construction for each origin order

Formalized: thm:catalan-truncation-per-order-construction in every dimension (route item M14). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This cycle continues the bottom-up formalization of the route with the per-order construction, the upper half of the formula. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof.

The formalized statement and proof

Statement (PerOrderConstruction.per_order_construction). Let \(\sigma\) be a finite set of variables containing a chosen \(x_1\), so \(n=|\sigma|\ge1\), and let \(\ell<k\). Then \(P=(x_1^2+x_1)^\ell\,g_{k-\ell}\) has:

  • multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point of \(\mathbb F_2^\sigma\);
  • origin order exactly \(\ell\);
  • total degree at most \(n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\).

Proof. This is the notebook's proof.

  • Nonzero points. \(y_1=x_1^2+x_1\) has multiplicity at least 1 at every point, so \(y_1^\ell\) has multiplicity at least \(\ell\). The truncated product gives \(k-\ell\). Multiplicity is superadditive on products.
  • Origin. \(y_1\) has origin order exactly 1. It is at least 1, and at most 1 because the coefficient of \(x_1\) is 1. Multiplicity is additive on products over the domain \(\mathbb F_2\), so \(y_1^\ell\) has origin order \(\ell\). Also \(g(0)=1\) gives origin order 0 for \(g\).
  • Degree. \(\deg y_1^\ell\le2\ell\), and \(\deg g_{k-\ell}\le n+2(k-\ell-1)-s_2(k-\ell-1)\). No natural-number subtraction is truncated, because \(s_2(m)\le m\).

Files. claims/PerOrderConstruction.lean passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f7-per-order-construction/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. thm:catalan-truncation-per-order-construction, now formalized in every dimension.

24 September 2026 — Lean: the degree formula, fully formalized

Formalized: thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula and conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula, down to multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel (route item M15). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment and endpoint. This cycle completes the formalization assignment of 24 September. Every obligation of the route is verified, including the imported input. The endpoint was a verified Lean proof of the formula theorem with no unverified dependency.

The formalized statement

Admissibility. Admissible n k P means that \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\dots,x_n]\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and less than \(k\) at the origin, with multiplicity the least degree of a monomial of \(P(a+z)\).

The theorem. For every \(k\ge1\), degree_formula states two things:

  1. For \(n\ge\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\), every admissible \(P\) has \(\deg P\ge2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\).
  2. For every \(n\ge1\), some admissible \(P\) has \(\deg P\le2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\).

degree_formula_exact combines them: for \(n\ge\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\) the minimum is attained with equality. degree_formula_of_lt_two_pow states the same thing in the conjecture's range \(1\le k<2^{n-1}\).

Proof.

  • Part (1) is the verified lower bound.
  • Part (2) is the verified per-order construction with \(\ell=k-2^J\), where \(J=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) and \(2^J\le k\). It has origin order \(\ell<k\) and degree at most \(n+2k-2-s_2(2^J-1)\).
  • \(s_2(2^J-1)=J\), by induction: \(2^{J+1}-1\) is odd with half \(2^J-1\).

What is verified. The dependency chain is:

  • multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel, over every field;
  • the note's deduction;
  • linear-form avoidance;
  • the one-dimension step;
  • the lower bound;
  • the truncated product;
  • the per-order construction;
  • this assembly.

Every link is checked by verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The final report, which covers the full transitive chain, is in research/results/bmd-f8-formula/lean-verification.txt. Menezes' theorem is not used; his construction enters through our own proof of the truncated product.

General statement. thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula. conj:binary-multiplicity-degree-formula is the same statement in the range \(1\le k<2^{n-1}\) and is covered by degree_formula_of_lt_two_pow.

Verification gate. The theorem and the conjecture were re-read from this notebook. The printed Lean types state the lower bound for \(n\ge m(k)\), the upper bound for every \(n\ge1\), and the exact minimum on the conjecture's range, with the notebook's definition of admissibility. The equivalence of \(n\ge m(k)\) with \(k<2^{n-1}\) (for \(k\ge1\)) is used in the stated direction via Nat.log_lt_of_lt_pow. The proof is checked by Lean.

Remaining route and process assessment. The formalization route item is complete. This is the thread's first result verified in Lean end to end. Process over the whole assignment: nine checkpoints. The friction was Mathlib renames and deprecations, a missing compiled-module cache, and one framework gap in claim registration, which is fixed. No mathematical gap appeared.

24 September 2026 — The formula holds up to k < 3·2^(n−1)

Working proof, verified in Lean: D(n,k)=2k+n−2−⌊log₂k⌋ for 1 ≤ k < 3·2^(n−1); found by the paper's fresh-context referee. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question and outcome. The fresh-context referee of the draft paper pointed out that the two verified bounds already meet above the conjecture's range. The upper bound, the per-order construction with \(\ell=k-2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}\), holds in every dimension. The lower bound is the note's bound \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\).

The statement

Working corollary (cor:binary-multiplicity-degree-extended-range). For \(n\ge1\) and \(1\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\),

\[ D(n,k)=2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor. \]

Proof. Below \(2^{n-1}\) this is the formula theorem. Above it, the upper bound is the construction, and the lower bound comes from the note's bound in two cases:

  • for \(2^{n-1}\le k<2^n\): \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor=n-1\) and \(\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor=1\), so both bounds equal \(2k-1\);
  • for \(2^n\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\): \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor=n\) and \(\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor=2\), so both equal \(2k-2\).

At \(k=3\cdot2^{n-1}\) the bounds are \(2k-3\) and \(2k-2\), so the range is where the two known bounds meet, not a proved limit of the formula. The note's bound is therefore attained exactly for \(2^{n-2}\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\).

Files. claims/BinaryMultiplicityDegreeExtendedRange.lean (degree_formula_extended) passes verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The report is in research/results/bmd-f9-extended-range/lean-verification.txt.

General statement. cor:binary-multiplicity-degree-extended-range, for every \(n\ge1\).

Verification gate. The two cases were checked by hand. The Lean proof uses only the verified formula theorem, the upper bound and the note's bound. The printed type states the exact minimum for \(1\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\), \(n\ge1\).

Assessment. This is a free extension found in review; it needs no new idea. The open questions are the true value for \(k\ge3\cdot2^{n-1}\) and the per-order lower bound for \(m(k)\le n<k-1\). Process: a referee reading the whole chain at once found a consequence that the cycle-by-cycle work missed.

24 September 2026 — Correction: the Schwartz–Zippel bound is attained for every k ≥ 2^(n−2), and D(n,k) is known for all n, k

Working proof, verified in Lean: D(n,k) for all n,k ≥ 1. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question and outcome. This entry corrects the extended-range entry. Here the Schwartz–Zippel bound means the note's bound \(D(n,k)\ge2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). The extended-range entry ended with the sentence "The note's bound is therefore attained exactly for \(2^{n-2}\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\)", and its assessment called the value for \(k\ge3\cdot2^{n-1}\) open. Both statements are wrong. The Lean corollary of that entry is correct, but its scope is the formula for \(k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\) and nothing about larger \(k\). The two statements were inferred from the gap between the bounds we had at \(k=3\cdot2^{n-1}\). Bishnoi, Boyadzhiyska, Das and Mészáros (arXiv 2101.11947v1, Theorem 1.2(a); read from the primary text in this cycle) had already constructed hyperplane covers of the matching size for \(k\ge2^{n-2}\). Their product is a polynomial meeting the bound. This entry formalizes that construction and the complete value of \(D(n,k)\).

The hyperplane covers

Imported theorem (third-party:BBDM-hyperplane-cover-large-k). BBDM Theorem 1.2(a) with \(d=1\): for \(k\ge2^{n-2}\) the least size of a multiset of affine hyperplanes in \(\mathbb F_2^n\) covering each nonzero point at least \(k\) times and the origin at most \(k-1\) times is \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). The part used here is the polynomial form of their construction for \(k\ge2^{n-1}\). For \(n\ge1\) and \(k\ge2^{n-1}\), some \(k\)-admissible \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) has \(\deg P\le2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). The range \(2^{n-2}\le k<2^{n-1}\) is already covered by our per-order construction, which has degree \(2k\) there.

Proof (BBDM's Section 2 construction, with their arbitrary parallel pairs chosen as \(x_1=0\), \(x_1=1\), written in the polynomial language of the Lean proof). Let \(m=2^{n-1}\) and write \(k=am+b\) with \(a\ge1\), \(0\le b<m\). Put \(F=\prod_{u\ne0}(1+u\cdot x)\) over the \(2^n-1\) nonzero \(u\in\mathbb F_2^n\), and \(y=x_1^2+x_1\).

  • \(F\) at nonzero points. Fix \(x\ne0\) and a coordinate \(i\) with \(x_i=1\). Flipping \(u_i\) is an involution on \(\mathbb F_2^n\) that changes \(u\cdot x\) by one. So exactly \(2^{n-1}=m\) vectors \(u\) satisfy \(u\cdot x=1\), and all of them are nonzero. Each such factor vanishes at \(x\), so it has multiplicity at least one there. Multiplicities of a product add, so \(\operatorname{mult}_x F\ge m\).
  • \(F\) at the origin. Every factor equals \(1\) at the origin, so \(F(0)=1\) and \(\operatorname{mult}_0F=0\).
  • The factor \(y\). \(y=x_1(1+x_1)\) vanishes on all of \(\mathbb F_2^n\), so \(\operatorname{mult}_x(y^b)\ge b\) everywhere. Also \(\operatorname{mult}_0(y^b)=b\), since \(y^b=x_1^b(1+x_1)^b\) and \((1+x_1)^b\) is a unit at the origin (mult_zero_y_pow of the per-order construction).

Hence \(P=F^ay^b\) has multiplicity at least \(am+b=k\) at every nonzero point, and multiplicity \(b<k\) at the origin. Its degree is at most \(a(2^n-1)+2b=2(am+b)-a=2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). In the language of covers: \(a\) copies of the \(2^n-1\) hyperplanes \(u\cdot x=1\) plus \(b\) copies of the parallel pair \(x_1=0\), \(x_1=1\).

The complete formula

Working theorem (thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-complete). For all \(n\ge1\) and \(k\ge1\),

\[ D(n,k)=\begin{cases}2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor,& k<2^{n-1},\\ 2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor,& k\ge2^{n-1},\end{cases} \]

and an admissible polynomial of exactly this degree exists. Moreover, some \(k\)-admissible polynomial has degree exactly \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) if and only if \(2^{n-2}\le k\).

Proof. For \(k<2^{n-1}\) this is the formula theorem. For \(k\ge2^{n-1}\), the note's bound gives the lower bound and the hyperplane construction above gives the upper bound. For the equivalence, every admissible polynomial has degree at least \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\), so we consider three cases.

  • If \(k<2^{n-2}\), then \(n\ge3\), \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\le n-3\) and \(\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor=0\). The formula theorem gives every admissible polynomial degree at least \(2k+1>2k\).
  • If \(2^{n-2}\le k<2^{n-1}\), then \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor=n-2\), so \(D(n,k)=2k=2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\).
  • If \(k\ge2^{n-1}\), the first part applies.

The two expressions agree on \(2^{n-2}\le k<2^{n-1}\) (both equal \(2k\)) and on \(2^{n-1}\le k<3\cdot2^{n-1}\) (the extended-range corollary). At \(k=3\cdot2^{n-1}\) the logarithmic expression is \(2k-2\) while \(D=2k-3\). This last comparison is arithmetic on the verified theorem, not a separate Lean statement.

Files. third-party-claims/HyperplaneCoverUpperBound.lean (card_dot_eq_one, hyperplane_cover_upper_bound) and claims/BinaryMultiplicityDegreeComplete.lean (degreeMin, degree_complete, cover_bound_attained_iff) pass verify.sh with only the standard axioms. The reports are in research/results/bmd-f10-correction/.

Correction scope. This corrects the last sentence of the statement paragraph and the first open question of the assessment in the extended-range entry, and the registry summary of cor:binary-multiplicity-degree-extended-range. The corollary's formula and its Lean proof are unaffected. The draft preprint repeated both errors and is revised in the same checkpoint. The prior-art entry is unaffected: BBDM's covers do not reach \(k<2^{n-2}\), where our lower bound is needed.

General statement. thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-complete, for every \(n\ge1\) and \(k\ge1\).

Verification gate. BBDM Theorem 1.2(a) was read from the arXiv v1 PDF: "If \(k\ge2^{n-d-1}\), then \(f(n,k,d)=2^dk-\lfloor k/2^{n-d}\rfloor\)". Their construction is Section 2. The note's bound and the formula theorem were used through their verified Lean statements. The printed Lean types state the upper bound for \(k\ge2^{n-1}\), \(n\ge1\), the two-sided minimum for all \(n,k\ge1\), and the equivalence. Hypothesis use: \(n\ge1\) is needed in degree_complete and cover_bound_attained_iff, since at \(n=0\) degreeMin 0 k is not the true value. \(k\ge1\) is needed in degree_complete, since no admissible polynomial exists at \(k=0\). In cover_bound_attained_iff, \(k\ge1\) is removable: at \(k=0\) both sides are false. In hyperplane_cover_upper_bound, \(n\ge1\) is removable: at \(n=0\), \(P=1\) works. Both are kept to match the setting \(n,k\ge1\) and BBDM's \(n\ge d=1\). A fresh-context reviewer checked the construction, the case analysis and the Lean types and found no mathematical error. Its wording corrections, on this paragraph, the attribution and the status line, are applied.

Assessment. The goal question now has a complete answer for all \(n\) and \(k\). The one remaining mathematical question on this thread is the optional per-order lower bound below \(n=k-1\). Process lesson: the false sentence was prose added beside a Lean statement that did not contain it. It was not checked against the cited paper whose theorem bears on it. The publication checklist and the claim tooling now guard against this.

25 September 2026 — The per-order formula holds for every k ≤ 16 and every n ≥ m(k)

Computer-assisted working proof for k ≤ 16; conjecture for larger k. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The missing implication on this route is the per-order lower bound \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\) for \(m(k)\le n<k-1\), where \(m(k)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\). The upper bound is the verified per-order construction. The earlier table had only one case at the base dimension \(n=m(k)\) outside Menezes' range, \((n,k)=(5,8)\). This cycle tests the base dimension, which decides every larger \(n\), for all \(k\le16\). One mismatch would refute the line.

Outcome. All 136 per-order values at the base dimension for \(1\le k\le16\) equal the formula. Hence the formula holds for every \(n\ge m(k)\) when \(k\le16\). The computation reaches \(k=16\) through a reduction of admissible polynomials to their jets at the origin, which is recorded as a lemma.

Admissible polynomials are determined by their origin jet

Working lemma (lem:binary-origin-jet-parametrization). Let \(n,k\ge1\), \(y_i=x_i^2+x_i\), \(2^K\ge k\) and \(g_b(x)=x^b(1+x^{2^K})\in\mathbb F_2[x]\).

  1. Every \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) has a unique expansion \(P=\sum c_{\varepsilon,e}x^\varepsilon y^e\) with \(\varepsilon\in\{0,1\}^n\) and \(e\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^n\). Moreover \(\deg P=\max\{|\varepsilon|+2|e|:c_{\varepsilon,e}\ne0\}\).
  2. Let \(T(P)\) be \(P\) with the terms \(|e|\ge k\) removed. For a jet \(v=\sum_{|\beta|<k}v_\beta x^\beta\) put \(P(v)=T\bigl(\sum_\beta v_\beta\prod_ig_{\beta_i}(x_i)\bigr)\). Then \(P(v)\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point of \(\mathbb F_2^n\), and \(P(v)\equiv v\) modulo \(\mathfrak m_0^k\).
  3. If \(P\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point, then \(T(P)=P(v)\), where \(v\) is the jet of \(P\) at the origin modulo \(\mathfrak m_0^k\). Moreover \(\deg T(P)\le\deg P\).

Consequently \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=\min\{\deg P(v):\operatorname{ord}v=\ell\}\) for \(0\le\ell<k\).

Proof.

  • (a) Division by the monic \(y_i\) shows that \(\mathbb F_2[x_i]\) is free over \(\mathbb F_2[y_i]\) with basis \(1,x_i\), so the products \(x^\varepsilon y^e\) form a basis. The top form of \(x^\varepsilon y^e\) is the monomial \(x^{\varepsilon+2e}\), and \(\varepsilon+2e\) determines \((\varepsilon,e)\) by parity. Distinct basis elements of the same degree therefore have distinct top monomials, and the degree of a combination is the largest degree present.
  • (b) The \(y_j\) vanish at every point of \(\mathbb F_2^n\), so \((y)^k\subseteq\mathfrak m_a^k\) for every \(a\). Removing terms with \(|e|\ge k\) changes a polynomial by an element of \((y)^k\), which affects no multiplicity condition below order \(k\). At \(a\ne0\), pick \(i\) with \(a_i=1\). Then \(g_{\beta_i}(x_i)=x_i^{\beta_i}(1+x_i)^{2^K}\) has order at least \(2^K\ge k\) at \(a\), so each product lies in \(\mathfrak m_a^k\). At the origin, \(\prod_ig_{\beta_i}(x_i)=x^\beta\prod_i(1+x_i^{2^K})\equiv x^\beta\) modulo \(\mathfrak m_0^{k}\).
  • (c) Put \(Q=T(P)-P(v)\). By (b) and the choice of \(v\), \(Q\in\mathfrak m_a^k\) for every \(a\in\mathbb F_2^n\). The ideals \(\mathfrak m_a^k\) are pairwise comaximal, so by the Chinese remainder theorem \(\mathbb F_2[x]/\bigcap_a\mathfrak m_a^k\) has dimension \(2^n\binom{n+k-1}{n}\). By (a), \(\mathbb F_2[x]/(y)^k\) has the same dimension, with basis \(x^\varepsilon y^e\), \(|e|<k\). Multiplying by \(x_i\) never lowers a \(y\)-exponent, since \(x_i^2=y_i+x_i\), so \((y)^k\) is spanned by the basis elements with \(|e|\ge k\). As \((y)^k\subseteq\bigcap_a\mathfrak m_a^k\), the two ideals are equal. So \(Q\in(y)^k\) is spanned by terms with \(|e|\ge k\), while it is supported on \(|e|<k\), and hence \(Q=0\). The degree claim is (a).

For the consequence: an admissible \(P\) of origin order \(\ell\) gives \(\deg P\ge\deg T(P)=\deg P(v)\) with \(\operatorname{ord}v=\ell\), and each \(P(v)\) with \(\operatorname{ord}v=\ell\) is admissible of origin order \(\ell\). \(\square\)

The computation. research/tools/bmd_jet_orders.cpp takes as unknowns the jets \(v_\beta\), \(|\beta|<k\). These are \(\binom{n+k-1}{n}\) columns, in descending \(|\beta|\), in place of all monomials of degree up to \(d\). Each row is the coefficient of one basis term \(x^\varepsilon y^e\), \(|e|<k\), in \(P(v)\). It is a product of one-variable coefficients of \(g_b\), so rows are generated in the kernel. \(\deg P(v)\le d\) exactly when \(v\) is orthogonal to every row of degree \(>d\). Rows are added in descending degree to a leftmost-pivot echelon form, so the rank of every column prefix \(\{|\beta|\ge\ell\}\) is its number of pivots. A jet of order exactly \(\ell\) survives exactly while the pivots among the \(|\beta|=\ell\) columns are fewer than those columns. One elimination therefore gives \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) for every \(\ell\). As a control, the kernel reproduced every per-order value \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) recorded in the dense exact table (69 values in 24 cases, compared order by order) with zero mismatches (research/results/bmd-r1-base-dimension/validate/). The largest case, \((6,16)\), took 25 s with 54,264 columns.

The base dimension decides every larger dimension

Working lemma (lem:per-order-base-reduction). Let \(k\ge1\), \(m=m(k)\) and \(0\le\ell<k\). If \(\delta(m,k,\ell)\ge m+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\), then \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\) for every \(n\ge m\).

Proof. The per-order construction gives the upper bound in every dimension \(n\ge1\), so each \(\delta(n',k,\ell)\) is finite. For \(n'>m\) we have \(n'\ge2\) and \(k<2^{m-1}<2^{n'}\), so the per-order step gives \(\delta(n'-1,k,\ell)\le\delta(n',k,\ell)-1\). Chaining from \(n\) down to \(m\) gives \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge\delta(m,k,\ell)+(n-m)\), which is at least the formula. \(\square\)

The finite check and its corollary

Finite check (check:per-order-base-dimension-through-16). For every \(1\le k\le16\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), the exact value \(\delta(m(k),k,\ell)\) equals \(m(k)+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\). The check covers 16 cases and 136 values, with zero mismatches (research/results/bmd-r1-base-dimension/summary.txt). Of these values, 81 are orders not settled by earlier results: \(\ell\le k-3\) with \(s_2(k-\ell-1)<\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\), which attain neither \(D(n,k)\) nor fall under the high-origin cover theorem.

Computer-assisted corollary (cor:per-order-formula-through-16). For \(1\le k\le16\), \(0\le\ell<k\) and every \(n\ge m(k)\), \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\). This follows from the check and the base-dimension lemma. For \(n\ge k-1\) it agrees with Menezes' Corollary 1.3; below \(n=k-1\) no proved per-order lower bound was recorded before for the orders that do not attain \(D\).

The general statement and what does not prove it

Conjecture (conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-formula). For every \(k\ge1\), \(0\le\ell<k\) and \(n\ge m(k)\), \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\). By the base-dimension lemma it suffices to prove the lower bound at \(n=m(k)\).

The construction \(y_1^\ell Q\) gives \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\le2\ell+\delta(n,k-\ell,0)\) in every dimension, and the conjecture says this is an equality. So the conjecture splits into two claims: an origin-nonvanishing case, \(\delta(n,c,0)=n+2c-2-s_2(c-1)\), and an exchange rule saying that each unit of origin order costs exactly two degrees.

The obvious route fails. For \(1\le n_0\le n\) with \(\ell<2^{n_0+1}-1\), the per-order step applies at every dimension from \(n\) down to \(n_0+1\) and gives \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge\delta(n_0,k,\ell)+n-n_0\). In dimension \(n_0\), multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel over the full grid bounds the sum of all multiplicities by \(d\,2^{n_0-1}\). Here that sum is at least \(k(2^{n_0}-1)+\ell\), so \(d\ge2k-\lfloor(k-\ell)/2^{n_0-1}\rfloor\). This route yields \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge n+2k-\min_{n_0}\bigl(n_0+\lfloor(k-\ell)/2^{n_0-1}\rfloor\bigr)\). That is below the formula already at \((n,k,\ell)=(5,5,0)\): it gives 11, while the true value is 12. So a proof must use more than the point counts.

General statement. The all-parameter claim is conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-formula. This cycle proves it for \(k\le16\) (cor:per-order-formula-through-16), reduces it to the base dimension (lem:per-order-base-reduction), and gives an exact finite-dimensional form (lem:binary-origin-jet-parametrization).

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read from this notebook: the per-order step and its hypotheses \(n\ge2\), \(k<2^n\), and the per-order construction in every dimension. The kernel was validated order by order against the dense exact table, and the 136-value summary was produced by script from the saved outputs. One claim search found no recorded result implying the jet lemma. A fresh-context reviewer checked the jet lemma, the kernel's \(y\)-basis division, row entries, prefix-rank test and degree thresholds, the base reduction and the count of 81 open orders. It found no gap and asked for three changes, all made: this paragraph, a derivation of the Schwartz–Zippel route, and a precise description of the control.

Assessment. The missing implication has narrowed to the base-dimension lower bound, and it now holds for every \(k\le16\). No counterexample appeared in 81 open orders. The jet lemma turns the question into the rank of explicit tensor-structured matrices. That is the natural object for a proof: rows are products of one-variable coefficients of \(x^b(1+x)^{2^K}\) in the \(y\)-basis. The line's prospects improved. Process: the new kernel made every case fast, the largest taking 25 s where the dense kernel had stalled at \(n=6\), \(k=5\). The next step is mathematical, not more cases.

25 September 2026 — A per-order formula valid in every dimension, consistent with the complete D(n,k)

Conjecture with a finite check and a proved consistency lemma. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The per-order conjecture only needs a proof at the base dimension \(n=m(k)\). The only available lower-bound mechanism, the one-dimension step, gains just one degree per dimension. A proof by induction on \(n\) therefore needs the true values below the base dimension. This cycle computes them and asks whether they follow a law. The plan was to study certificate rows first. The data below the base dimension gave a sharper target, so the cycle went there instead.

Outcome. With the jet kernel, every \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) for \(1\le n\le5\) and \(1\le k\le16\) was computed, together with \((6,16)\): 81 cases and 696 per-order values. Every value equals

\[ \Phi(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\Bigl\lfloor\frac{k-\ell-1}{2^j}\Bigr\rfloor . \]

Two laws hold on the same data:

  • the halving law \(\delta(n,c,0)=c+\delta(n-1,\lceil c/2\rceil,0)\), with \(\delta(1,c,0)=c\), on all 81 values of \(\delta(n,c,0)\). The recursion solves to \(\Phi(n,c,0)\), since \(\lceil c/2\rceil-1=\lfloor(c-1)/2\rfloor\) and \(\lfloor\lfloor m/2\rfloor/2^j\rfloor=\lfloor m/2^{j+1}\rfloor\);
  • the exchange law \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=2\ell+\delta(n,k-\ell,0)\), on the 681 values for which \(\delta(n,k-\ell,0)\) was computed. For \((6,16)\), only \(\ell=0\) is among them.

The outputs are in research/results/bmd-r2-certificate-rows/ (halving-check.txt, order0-table.txt). In the table, \(\delta(n,c,0)-n\) grows with \(n\) and stabilizes at \(2(c-1)-s_2(c-1)=v_2((2c-2)!)\) from \(n=\lceil\log_2c\rceil\) on.

The all-dimension conjecture

Conjecture (conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions). For all \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).

The upper bound for \(\ell>0\) would follow from the case \(\ell=0\) by the construction \(y_1^\ell Q\), with \(y_1=x_1^2+x_1\). The factor \(y_1^\ell\) has multiplicity at least \(\ell\) everywhere and exactly \(\ell\) at the origin, and multiplicities add under products. This gives only the \(\le\) half of the exchange law; its lower bound is open too. The case \(\ell=0\) needs a construction in every dimension, which is not yet known. The conjecture implies the per-order conjecture by part (i) of the lemma below, since \(n\ge m(k)\) gives \(k<2^{n-1}\), hence \(k-\ell-1<2^n\) for every \(\ell\). Its value is that it holds in every dimension, including the small ones where the one-dimension step loses: \(\Phi(n,c,0)-\Phi(n-1,c,0)=1+\lfloor(c-1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). An induction on \(n\) then needs a step that gains \(1+\lfloor(c-1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor\), or, in the halving form, one that trades a dimension and half of the multiplicity for \(c\) degrees.

A falsification test the conjecture passes, proved

Minimizing over \(\ell\) must give the verified complete value of \(D(n,k)\) for every \(n\) and \(k\), including BBDM's covering range. \(\Phi\) was fitted only to \(n\le6\), \(k\le16\), so this tests it far outside that range, though only through its minimum over \(\ell\), not value by value. A check of 8,400 pairs (\(n\le14\), \(k\le600\), phi-vs-complete.txt) found no mismatch. The identity holds in general.

Working lemma (lem:per-order-all-dimension-minimum). Let \(n,k\ge1\).

  1. If \(k-\ell-1<2^n\), then \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\).
  2. \(\min_{0\le\ell<k}\Phi(n,k,\ell)\) equals \(2k+n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) for \(k<2^{n-1}\), and \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) for \(k\ge2^{n-1}\). This is the value of \(D(n,k)\) in the complete formula.

Proof. Let \(m=k-\ell-1\), and write \(m=q2^n+r\) with \(0\le r<2^n\). Legendre's formula \(\sum_{j\ge0}\lfloor m/2^j\rfloor=2m-s_2(m)\) gives

\[ \sum_{j<n}\lfloor m/2^j\rfloor=(2m-s_2(m))-(2q-s_2(q)). \]

Since \(s_2(m)=s_2(q)+s_2(r)\), this sum is \(2m-2q-s_2(r)\), and

\[ \Phi=n+2(k-1-m)+2m-2q-s_2(r)=n+2k-2-h(m),\qquad h(m)=2q+s_2(r). \]
  • (i) If \(m<2^n\), then \(q=0\) and \(h=s_2(m)\).
  • (ii) The minimum is \(n+2k-2-\max_{0\le m\le k-1}h(m)\). Write \(k-1=Q2^n+R\) with \(0\le R<2^n\). The largest digit sum among \(0,\ldots,R\) is \(\sigma(R)=\lfloor\log_2(R+1)\rfloor\): it is attained at \(2^{\sigma}-1\), and every \(r\le R<2^{\sigma+1}-1\) has at most \(\sigma\) binary ones. With \(q=Q\) the best value of \(h\) is \(2Q+\sigma(R)\). With \(q\le Q-1\), possible only if \(Q\ge1\), it is at most \(2Q-2+n\), attained at \(m=Q2^n-1\). So \(\max h=2Q+\sigma(R)\) if \(Q=0\), and \(\max h=2Q+\max(\sigma(R),n-2)\) if \(Q\ge1\).
    • If \(Q=0\), that is \(k\le2^n\), the minimum is \(n+2k-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\). This is the first case when \(k<2^{n-1}\). For \(2^{n-1}\le k<2^n\) it is \(2k-1\), and for \(k=2^n\) it is \(2k-2\). Both equal \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\).
    • If \(Q\ge1\), then \(k=Q2^n+(R+1)\) with \(1\le R+1\le2^n\), and \(\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor=2Q+\lfloor(R+1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). Moreover \(\max(\sigma(R),n-2)=n-2+\lfloor(R+1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) in each of the three ranges: \(R+1<2^{n-1}\), where \(\sigma\le n-2\); \(2^{n-1}\le R+1<2^n\), where \(\sigma=n-1\); and \(R+1=2^n\), where \(\sigma=n\). For \(n=1\) the first range is empty. So the minimum is \(n+2k-2-2Q-(n-2)-\lfloor(R+1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor=2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). \(\square\)

Finite check (check:per-order-all-dimension-values). All 696 per-order values \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) for \(n\le5\), \(k\le16\), together with \((6,16)\), equal \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)\), with zero mismatches. The comparison uses \(2\ell+\Phi(n,k-\ell,0)\), which equals \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)\) identically.

General statement. The all-parameter claim is conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions. By lem:per-order-all-dimension-minimum it implies conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-formula and is consistent with the verified thm:binary-multiplicity-degree-complete.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the complete formula's statement from this notebook. The checks were produced by script from the saved kernel outputs, and the kernel was validated in the previous entry. A fresh-context reviewer checked the lemma line by line, including \(n=1\), \(k=2^n\) and the three ranges, and found it correct. It asked for precise check counts, an explicit maximum step, the identity behind the halving recursion, the range of the implication, and weaker assessment wording. All are made.

Assessment. The missing implication is unchanged in kind: a lower bound for the per-order minimum. It now has a conjectured formula valid in every dimension, which gives a target for an induction on \(n\). The target step is a dimension reduction that halves the multiplicity and saves \(c\) degrees, as the halving law says. The natural tool is the \(y_n\)-adic expansion \(P=\sum_j(A_j(x')+x_nB_j(x'))y_n^j\). There, multiplicity \(c\) at \((a',0)\) and \((a',1)\) gives triangular order conditions on the \(A_j,B_j\) at \(a'\), and the degree is \(\max_j\max(\deg A_j+2j,\deg B_j+2j+1)\). The line's prospects improved: a clean all-dimension law passed, provably, a falsification test through its minimum over \(\ell\) in every dimension. Process: the change of plan followed the data. Every computation took seconds.

25 September 2026 — The upper half of the all-dimension formula holds in every dimension

Working proof: δ(n,k,ℓ) ≤ Φ(n,k,ℓ) for all n ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ ℓ < k. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The all-dimension conjecture \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)\), with \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\), had no construction attaining \(\Phi\) below the base dimension. The per-order construction has degree \(n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1)\). That equals \(\Phi\) only when \(k-\ell-1<2^n\). This cycle looks for the missing construction, guided by minimal-degree witnesses from the jet kernel.

Outcome. Products of the truncated product with BBDM's hyperplane product attain \(\Phi\) in every dimension. So only the lower bound of the conjecture remains open.

The construction

Working theorem (thm:per-order-all-dimension-upper-bound). Let \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\). Put \(c=k-\ell\) and write \(c-1=q2^n+r\) with \(0\le r<2^n\). Define

\[ P=y_1^{\,\ell}\,F^{2q}\,g_{r+1},\qquad F=\prod_{u\in\mathbb F_2^n\setminus\{0\}}(1+u\cdot x),\qquad y_1=x_1^2+x_1, \]

where \(g_s\) is the truncated product. Then \(P\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and exactly \(\ell\) at the origin, and \(\deg P\le\Phi(n,k,\ell)\). Hence \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\le\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).

Proof. The facts used are recorded:

  • Fix \(a\ne0\) and \(i\) with \(a_i=1\). Flipping \(u_i\) is an involution that changes \(u\cdot a\) by one, so exactly \(2^{n-1}\) vectors \(u\) satisfy \(u\cdot a=1\). All of them are nonzero, so they index factors of \(F\) that vanish at \(a\). Hence \(\operatorname{mult}_aF\ge2^{n-1}\). Also \(F(0)=1\) and \(\deg F\le2^n-1\). These are steps of the hyperplane cover proof, whose Lean proof (hyperplane_cover_upper_bound, with card_dot_eq_one) establishes them for the same \(F\).
  • \(g_s\) has multiplicity at least \(s\) at every nonzero point, \(g_s(0)=1\), and \(\deg g_s\le n+2(s-1)-s_2(s-1)\) (truncated product; Lean mult_g_ge, eval_zero_g, totalDegree_g_le).
  • \(y_1^\ell\) has multiplicity at least \(\ell\) everywhere and exactly \(\ell\) at the origin (Lean one_le_mult_y, mult_zero_y_pow).

Multiplicities of a product are at least the sum. At the origin, \(F^{2q}g_{r+1}\) has constant term \(1\), so it is a unit in the local ring, and multiplying by it keeps the origin order \(\ell\) of \(y_1^\ell\). At \(a\ne0\) the total is at least \(\ell+2q\cdot2^{n-1}+(r+1)=\ell+(q2^n+r+1)=\ell+c=k\). At the origin it is \(\ell+0+0=\ell\). The degree is at most

\[ 2\ell+2q(2^n-1)+n+2r-s_2(r)=n+2\ell+2(c-1)-2q-s_2(r). \]

In the proof of the all-dimension minimum lemma, Legendre's formula gave \(\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(c-1)/2^j\rfloor=2(c-1)-2q-s_2(r)\), so this bound is \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)\). \(\square\)

For \(c-1<2^n\) we have \(q=0\), and \(P\) is the per-order construction. With the minimum lemma, the theorem reproves the upper half of the complete formula, since \(D(n,k)\le\min_\ell\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).

Leads that failed

At \((n,c)=(2,5)\) a minimal-degree witness from the kernel has origin jet \(1+N+N^2\). Here \(N=x_1^2+x_1x_2+x_2^2\) is the norm form of \(\mathbb F_4/\mathbb F_2\). \(N\) equals \(1\) at every nonzero point, so \(1+N\) vanishes on \(\mathbb F_2^n\setminus\{0\}\) in degree \(n\). Neither \((1+N)^{-1}\) nor \((1+N)^c\) modulo \(\mathfrak m^c\) attains \(\Phi(n,c,0)\) in general. They differ in 61 and 34 of 80 cases, respectively, for \(n\le5\), \(c\le16\) (research/results/bmd-r3-halving-witnesses/). The witnesses at \((2,3)\) and \((2,4)\) are the truncated product itself, which led to the construction above.

General statement. thm:per-order-all-dimension-upper-bound, for all \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\). It proves the upper half of conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions, which now reduces to \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the hyperplane-cover proof and its Lean declarations, the truncated product's Lean statements, and the minimum lemma's identity. The construction is a composition of these, and no new lemma was needed. The claim search for the construction found only the per-order construction, which this refines. A fresh-context reviewer checked the multiplicity count (including \(q=0\), \(r=0\), \(n=1\), \(\ell=0\)), the degree arithmetic with natural-number subtraction, and the floor-sum identity. It found no mathematical gap and asked for exact citations and a justification of the origin order; both are made.

Assessment. The conjecture's upper half is now a theorem in every dimension. The missing implication is exactly the lower bound \(\delta\ge\Phi\). It holds at the minimizing orders by the complete formula, and it holds for \(k\le16\), \(n\ge m(k)\). The line keeps its prospects; the hard half is unchanged. Next: the halving law's lower bound through the \(y_n\)-adic expansion. The construction suggests the extremal structure is \(F^{2q}g_{r+1}\), where the \(2^n\)-blocks of \(c-1\) are paid by the hyperplane product. A lower-bound proof may peel off one such block at a time. Process: witness inspection took minutes and redirected the search from norm forms to a composition of known factors.

25 September 2026 — A hyperplane dichotomy proves the order-zero value in dimensions up to three

Working proof: δ(n,c,0) = n + Σ_{j<n}⌊(c−1)/2^j⌋ for n ≤ 3 and every c; the argument first fails at (4,9). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The all-dimension conjecture reduces to the lower bound \(\delta\ge\Phi\), since the upper bound is proved. The per-order step (lem:per-order-dimension-step: \(\delta(n-1,k,\ell)\le\delta(n,k,\ell)-1\) for \(n\ge2\), \(k<2^n\)) gains one degree per dimension, while \(\Phi(n,c,0)-\Phi(n-1,c,0)=1+\lfloor(c-1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor\). So the step alone fails once \(c-1\ge2^{n-1}\). This cycle looks for a second mechanism that pays for the blocks of \(2^{n-1}\) units of multiplicity, which the construction pays with the hyperplane product \(F\).

Outcome. Restricting to the \(2^n-1\) affine hyperplanes that avoid the origin gives a dichotomy. Either the degree is at least \(2c\), or \(F\) divides \(P\). Combined with the step, this proves \(\delta(n,c,0)=\Phi(n,c,0)\) for every \(c\) when \(n\le3\). The combined recursion first falls short at \((n,c)=(4,9)\), where the true value is \(19=\Phi\).

The dichotomy

Working lemma (lem:hyperplane-restriction-dichotomy). Let \(n,k\ge1\), and let \(P\in\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) have multiplicity at least \(k\) at every nonzero point and order \(\ell\) at the origin. The value of \(\ell\) plays no role except that it is carried over to \(P'\). Then either \(\deg P\ge2k\), or \(P=F\,P'\) with \(F=\prod_{u\ne0}(1+u\cdot x)\), where \(P'\) has multiplicity at least \(k-2^{n-1}\) at every nonzero point, exactly \(\ell\) at the origin, and \(\deg P'=\deg P-(2^n-1)\). In particular, taking \(k=c\) and \(\ell=0\), with \(\delta(n,c,0)=0\) for \(c\le0\),

\[ \delta(n,c,0)\ge\min\bigl(2c,\;2^n-1+\delta(n,c-2^{n-1},0)\bigr). \]

Proof.

  • For \(n=1\): \(H_1=\{1\}\), and \(P\) vanishes there, so \(1+x=F\) divides \(P\). Now let \(n\ge2\). Fix \(u\ne0\) and an affine bijection \(\varphi:\mathbb F_2^{n-1}\to H_u=\{u\cdot x=1\}\). Composition with an affine map does not lower Hasse multiplicity, so \(P\circ\varphi\) has multiplicity at least \(k\) at every point of \(\mathbb F_2^{n-1}\): all points of \(H_u\) are nonzero. By part (c) of the origin-jet lemma, \(\bigcap_z\mathfrak m_z^k=(y)^k\), so \(P\circ\varphi\in(y)^k\).
  • By part (a), a nonzero element of \((y)^k\) has only terms \(x^\varepsilon y^e\) with \(|e|\ge k\), hence degree at least \(2k\). So either \(\deg P\ge\deg(P\circ\varphi)\ge2k\), or \(P\circ\varphi=0\). In the latter case \(P\) lies in the kernel \((1+u\cdot x)\) of \(\varphi^*\).
  • If \(\deg P<2k\), all \(2^n-1\) pairwise non-associate irreducibles \(1+u\cdot x\) divide \(P\), so \(F\mid P\) and \(\deg P'=\deg P-\deg F=\deg P-(2^n-1)\).
  • Hasse multiplicity is additive under products, because lowest homogeneous parts multiply in a domain. At \(a\ne0\) exactly \(2^{n-1}\) factors of \(F\) vanish, each simply, so \(\operatorname{mult}_aF=2^{n-1}\) and \(\operatorname{mult}_aP'\ge k-2^{n-1}\). At the origin \(F(0)=1\), so \(P'\) keeps the order \(\ell\). For \(\ell=0\) and \(c\le2^{n-1}\), the bound for \(P'\) is \(\deg P'\ge0\). \(\square\)

The order-zero value in dimensions up to three

Two exact identities of \(\Phi(n,c,0)=n+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(c-1)/2^j\rfloor\) match the two mechanisms:

  • (S) \(\Phi(n,c,0)=\Phi(n-1,c,0)+1\) when \(c-1<2^{n-1}\), since the \(j=n-1\) term vanishes;
  • (D) \(\Phi(n,c,0)=2^n-1+\Phi(n,c-2^{n-1},0)\) when \(c>2^{n-1}\), since subtracting \(2^{n-1}\) lowers the \(j\)-th term by exactly \(2^{n-1-j}\).

Moreover \(\Phi(n,c,0)\le2c\) exactly when \(2q+s_2(r)\ge n-2\), where \(c-1=q2^n+r\) with \(0\le r<2^n\). This follows from the Legendre form \(\Phi=n+2(c-1)-2q-s_2(r)\) of the minimum lemma.

Working theorem (thm:order-zero-value-small-dimensions). For \(1\le n\le3\) and every \(c\ge1\), \(\delta(n,c,0)=\Phi(n,c,0)\):

  • \(\delta(1,c,0)=c\);
  • \(\delta(2,c,0)=c+1+\lfloor(c-1)/2\rfloor\);
  • \(\delta(3,c,0)=c+2+\lfloor(c-1)/2\rfloor+\lfloor(c-1)/4\rfloor\).

Proof. The upper bound is the all-dimension upper bound. For the lower bound, induct on \(n\), and within \(n\) on \(c\).

  • \(n=1\). \((1+x)^c\) divides \(P\), so \(\deg P\ge c\).
  • Small \(c\). For \(n=2,3\) and \(c-1<2^{n-1}\), we have \(c\le2^{n-1}<2^n\), and \(\delta(n,c,0)\) is finite by the upper bound. So the per-order step applies with \(\ell=0\) and gives \(\delta(n,c,0)\ge\delta(n-1,c,0)+1\ge\Phi(n-1,c,0)+1=\Phi(n,c,0)\) by (S).
  • Large \(c\). Otherwise \(c>2^{n-1}\) and the dichotomy gives \(\delta(n,c,0)\ge\min(2c,\Phi(n,c,0))\) by induction and (D). It remains to check \(\Phi(n,c,0)\le2c\). For \(n=2\) this is \(1+\lfloor(c-1)/2\rfloor\le c\). For \(n=3\) and \(c\ge5\) it follows from \(\lfloor(c-1)/2\rfloor+\lfloor(c-1)/4\rfloor\le3(c-1)/4\le c-2\). \(\square\)

Where the two mechanisms stop

Let \(R(n,c)\) be the recursion \(R(n,c)=\max\bigl(R(n-1,c)+1,\ \min(2c,\,2^n-1+R(n,c-2^{n-1}))\bigr)\), with the step term used only when \(c-1<2^{n-1}\), \(R(1,c)=c\) and \(R(n,c)=0\) for \(c\le0\). It is a proved lower bound for \(\delta(n,c,0)\). Shortfalls start where \(c-1\ge2^{n-1}\) and \(2q+s_2(r)<n-2\). There the step does not apply and the dichotomy only gives \(2c<\Phi\). They are then inherited through both rules: \((4,17)\) inherits one from \((4,9)\) through (D), and \((5,9)\) through (S). An arithmetic comparison over \(n\le7\), \(c\le64\) (research/tools/bmd_dichotomy_bound.py, output in research/results/bmd-r4-hyperplane-dichotomy/) finds \(R=\Phi\) for all \(n\le3\), and \(R\le\Phi\) everywhere. The first shortfall is \((4,9)\): \(R=18\), while the exact value is 19. For \(n=4\) the shortfalls are \(c\equiv1\pmod8\), \(c\ge9\). In the gap, a polynomial of degree between \(2c\) and \(\Phi-1\) has a nonzero restriction to some \(H_u\). That restriction lies in \((y)^c\) of \(H_u\), which alone is compatible with degree \(2c\). The missing ingredient has to use the origin and the hyperplane restrictions at the same time.

General statement. lem:hyperplane-restriction-dichotomy holds for all \(n,k,\ell\). thm:order-zero-value-small-dimensions proves the order-zero case of conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions for \(n\le3\).

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the origin-jet lemma (a) and (c), the per-order step and its hypotheses \(n\ge2\), \(k<2^n\), the minimum lemma's Legendre form, and the all-dimension upper bound. The recursion's comparison with \(\Phi\) was computed by script. A fresh-context reviewer checked the dichotomy (affine restriction, \((y)^k\) on the hyperplane, divisibility, \(\operatorname{mult}_aF=2^{n-1}\)), the identities (S) and (D), the criterion \(\Phi\le2c\), and the induction for \(n\le3\), including the bases. It found no mathematical error. It asked for the case \(n=1\), the unused hypothesis \(\ell<k\), a link to the step, and a precise description of where the recursion falls short; all are made.

Assessment. For the first time the lower bound \(\delta\ge\Phi\) is proved on an infinite family below the base dimension, and the missing mechanism is now located. The missing implication is the set where \(R<\Phi\), which starts at \((4,9)\) and is inherited upward. It needs an argument that improves "degree \(\ge2c\) on a hyperplane" using the other hyperplanes and the origin. Possible routes: apply the dichotomy to \(P'\) after dividing only the hyperplanes on which \(P\) vanishes, with Schwartz–Zippel on \(P'|_{H}\); or bring the origin in through the step on \(P\) restricted to linear hyperplanes. The line keeps good prospects; the case \((4,9)\) is small enough to study exactly. Process: the arithmetic comparison of the recursion with \(\Phi\) located the gap in seconds, before any proof attempt.

Measured timing
Measured categoryElapsed
Total instrumented interval4 min 35.87 s
Preparation and checkpoint work4 min 35.71 s
Individually measured conversion, checks, and local processing0.16 s

Through final snapshot; overlapping time counted once.

25 September 2026 — The per-order formula is proved: additive polynomials on the span of the y_i

Working proof: δ(n,k,ℓ) = n+2k−2−s₂(k−ℓ−1) whenever k−ℓ ≤ 2^n, hence for all n ≥ m(k). Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The first case of \(\delta\ge\Phi\) not covered by the step and the hyperplane dichotomy is \((n,c,\ell)=(4,9,0)\). This cycle looks for the mechanism that decides it. It starts from a closed form for the coefficients of the polynomial attached to an origin jet.

Outcome. The degree of \(P(v)\) is read off from \(y\)-coefficients of \(w=v\circ x\), where \(x(Y)=\sum_{t\ge0}Y^{2^t}\) is additive. The lower bound then follows from a root count, applied to multi-additive forms one variable at a time: a nonzero additive polynomial of degree below \(2^n\) cannot vanish on the \(2^n\)-element \(\mathbb F_2\)-span of \(y_1,\ldots,y_n\). This proves the per-order conjecture for every \(k\), and the all-dimension conjecture for \(n\le4\).

The degree in terms of \(y\)-coefficients

Let \(x(Y)=\sum_{t\ge0}Y^{2^t}\in\mathbb F_2[[Y]]\), the root of \(X^2+X=Y\) with \(x(0)=0\). Substituting \(x_i=x(y_i)\) is an automorphism of \(\mathbb F_2[[y]]\) that preserves the order.

Working lemma (lem:y-coefficient-degree-formula). Let \(v\) be an origin jet (\(|\beta|<k\)), let \(P(v)\) be as in the origin-jet lemma, and put \(w(y)=v(x(y_1),\ldots,x(y_n))\). Then the coefficient of \(x^\varepsilon y^e\) (\(|e|<k\)) in \(P(v)\) is

\[ [y^e]\Bigl(w\prod_{i:\varepsilon_i=0}\bigl(1+x(y_i)\bigr)\Bigr), \]

and therefore

\[ \deg P(v)=\max\Bigl\{n-|S|+2|e|:\ S\subseteq[n],\ |e|<k,\ [y^e]\bigl(w\,u^S\bigr)\ne0\Bigr\},\qquad u^S=\prod_{i\in S}\bigl(1+x(y_i)\bigr). \]

Proof.

  • One variable. Let \(f\in\mathbb F_2[x]\) vanish to order at least \(k\) at \(x=1\), and write \(f=\sum_j(p_j+q_jx)y^j\). In \(\mathbb F_2[[y]]\), the substitutions \(x\mapsto x(y)\) and \(x\mapsto1+x(y)\) both send \(x^2+x\) to \(y\). The second has \(f(1+x(y))\equiv0\) modulo \(y^k\), since \(x(y)\) has order 1. So modulo \(y^k\):
    • \(\sum_j q_jy^j\equiv f(x(y))+f(1+x(y))\equiv f(x(y))\), because \((p+qx)+(p+q+qx)=q\);
    • \(\sum_jp_jy^j\equiv\sum_jq_j(1+x(y))y^j\equiv(1+x(y))f(x(y))\), from \(f(1+x(y))\equiv0\).
  • Several variables. \(P(v)=T\bigl(\sum_\beta v_\beta\prod_ig_{\beta_i}(x_i)\bigr)\), and \(g_b(x)=x^b(1+x^{2^K})\) vanishes to order \(2^K\ge k\) at \(1\). The coefficient of \(x^\varepsilon y^e\) factors over the variables: in variable \(i\) it is \([y_i^{e_i}]\bigl((1+x(y_i))^{1-\varepsilon_i}g_{\beta_i}(x(y_i))\bigr)\). Since \(x(y)^{2^K}\) has order \(2^K\ge k>e_i\), we may replace \(g_b(x(y))\) by \(x(y)^b\). Summing over \(\beta\) gives the displayed coefficient.
  • The degree. The degree formula is part (a) of the origin-jet lemma, with \(S=\{i:\varepsilon_i=0\}\). \(\square\)

As a control, the formula was compared with the kernel's degree of \(P(v)\) for 240 random jets (\(n\le3\), \(k\le8\)), with zero mismatches (research/results/bmd-r5-additive-roots/ycoef-check.txt).

The per-order value when \(k-\ell\le2^n\)

Working theorem (thm:per-order-value-small-multiplicity). Let \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\) with \(k-\ell\le2^n\). Then

\[ \delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2k-2-s_2(k-\ell-1). \]

In particular this holds for every \(n\ge m(k)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\), since then \(k<2^{n-1}\). So the per-order conjecture is proved.

Proof. The upper bound is the per-order construction. For the lower bound, put \(c=k-\ell\) and \(h=s_2(c-1)\). The only use of \(c\le2^n\) is the degree bound \(c-1<2^n\) in step 3. Suppose \(\deg P\le n+2k-3-h\) for some admissible \(P\) of origin order \(\ell\). By the origin-jet lemma we may take \(P=P(v)\) with \(\operatorname{ord}v=\ell\). Then \(w\) has order \(\ell\): its degree-\(d\) parts \(w_d\) vanish for \(d<\ell\), and \(w_\ell\ne0\).

  • Step 1: the degree conditions. Take \(|e|=k-1\) and \(|S|\le h\) in the lemma. Then \(n-|S|+2(k-1)>\deg P\), so the degree-\((k-1)\) part of \(w\,u^S\) vanishes. Expanding \(u^S=\sum_{T\subseteq S}x^T\), with \(x^T=\prod_{i\in T}x(y_i)\), and inducting on \(|T|\) gives \((w\,x^T)_{k-1}=0\) for every set \(T\) of at most \(h\) distinct indices.
  • Step 2: the multi-additive forms. For \(0\le j\le h\) let \[ E_j(Y_1,\ldots,Y_j)=\sum_{t_1,\ldots,t_j\ge0}Y_1^{2^{t_1}}\cdots Y_j^{2^{t_j}}\,w_{k-1-\sum_a2^{t_a}} \] (terms with \(k-1-\sum2^{t_a}<\ell\) vanish). Since \(x(y_i)=\sum_ty_i^{2^t}\), we have \((w\,x_{i_1}\cdots x_{i_j})_{k-1}=E_j(y_{i_1},\ldots,y_{i_j})\). So \(E_j\) vanishes at every tuple of distinct generators. \(E_j\) is symmetric and additive in each variable. Its degree in each variable is at most \(c-1<2^n\), because only exponents with \(\sum2^{t_a}\le k-1-\ell=c-1\) occur.
  • Step 3: induction on \(j\). We show \(E_j=0\) as a polynomial. For \(j=0\) this is \(w_{k-1}=0\), which is step 1 with \(S=\emptyset\). Assume \(E_{j-1}=0\).
    • At a tuple with a repeated generator \(Y_1=Y_2\), the terms with \(t_1\ne t_2\) cancel in pairs. The terms with \(t_1=t_2=t\) carry coefficients \(w_{k-1-2^{t+1}-\ldots}\), which are coefficients of \(E_{j-1}\) and hence zero. So \(E_j\) vanishes on \(\{y_1,\ldots,y_n\}^j\).
    • By additivity it vanishes on \(V^j\), where \(V\) is the \(\mathbb F_2\)-span of the \(y_i\): \(2^n\) distinct elements of the domain \(\mathbb F_2[y]\).
    • Viewed as a polynomial in \(Y_1\) over \(\mathbb F_2[y][Y_2,\ldots,Y_j]\), specialized at any point of \(V^{j-1}\), it has degree \(<2^n\) and \(2^n\) roots, so it is zero. Each \(Y_1\)-coefficient therefore vanishes on \(V^{j-1}\), and induction on the number of variables gives \(E_j=0\).
  • Step 4: the contradiction. Write \(c-1=\sum_{a=1}^h2^{t_a}\) in binary. The coefficient of \(\prod_aY_a^{2^{t_a}}\) in \(E_h\) is \(w_{k-1-(c-1)}=w_\ell\), so \(w_\ell=0\), a contradiction. Hence \(\deg P\ge n+2k-2-h\). \(\square\)

For \(k-\ell-1<2^n\) the value equals \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)\) (part (i) of the minimum lemma). So this theorem is the all-dimension conjecture in the range \(k-\ell\le2^n\). It covers the case \((4,9,0)\) and all 136 values of the computer-assisted check. As a consistency check, all 477 exact values with \(k-\ell\le2^n\) among the 81 computed cases agree with it, 306 of them below \(m(k)\) (theorem-vs-data.txt).

Every order in dimensions up to four

Working corollary (cor:per-order-all-dimensions-small-n). For \(1\le n\le4\) and all \(0\le\ell<k\), \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).

Proof. The upper bound is the all-dimension upper bound. For the lower bound, induct on \(c=k-\ell\). If \(c\le2^n\), apply the theorem above. If \(c>2^n\), the first sentence of the dichotomy gives \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\ge\min\bigl(2k,\,2^n-1+\delta(n,k-2^{n-1},\ell)\bigr)\), since \(P'\) keeps order exactly \(\ell\). The right side is defined because \(k-2^{n-1}-\ell=c-2^{n-1}\ge1\). By induction, \(\delta(n,k-2^{n-1},\ell)\ge\Phi(n,k-2^{n-1},\ell)\). Now \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)=2\ell+\Phi(n,c,0)\), and identity (D) of the small-dimension entry at \(c>2^{n-1}\) gives \(2^n-1+\Phi(n,k-2^{n-1},\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)\). Finally, \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)\le2k\) is equivalent to \(2q+s_2(r)\ge n-2\), where \(c-1=q2^n+r\). This holds because \(q\ge1\) and \(n\le4\). \(\square\)

What remains. The all-dimension conjecture is open only for \(n\ge5\) and \(k-\ell>2^n\). For \(\ell=0\), an arithmetic comparison of the combined recursion with \(\Phi\) over \(n\le8\), \(c\le300\) (research/results/bmd-r5-additive-roots/closure.txt) shows shortfalls only there, the first at \((5,33)\). In that regime the forms \(E_j\) have degree up to \(c-1\ge2^n\). Additive polynomials vanishing on \(V\) are then multiples of the subspace polynomial \(L_V(Y)=\prod_{a\in V}(Y-a)\), of degree \(2^n\). The natural next step is to reduce the \(E_j\) modulo \(L_V\), matching the blocks \(F^{2q}\) of the construction.

Route review of the per-order line

This entry is also the route review due after six research entries on this item.

  • The line's general claim. For every \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)\) (conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions). It contains the per-order formula for \(n\ge m(k)\).
  • What the goal needs. The thread's main goal, \(D(n,k)\), is proved and verified. This line is the user's chosen extension: the per-order lower bound below Menezes' range, which makes the preprint a per-order theorem. That need is now met: the per-order formula holds for every \(n\ge m(k)\).
  • Falsification attempts. \(\Phi\) was fitted on 696 values. It survived:
    • the provable identity \(\min_\ell\Phi=D\) in every dimension;
    • the recursion of proved bounds, which never exceeds \(\Phi\) over \(n\le8\), \(c\le300\);
    • the theorem itself, which agrees with all 477 computed values in its range.
    A counterexample would have to lie at \(n\ge5\), \(k-\ell>2^n\), beyond the computed range.
  • Assessment. The line has advanced as far as the goal needs: in five cycles it went from a finite check to a proof. The remaining range is a side question in BBDM's covering regime, where \(D\) is already known. It is lower priority than turning the results into the preprint, which is the user's decision.
  • Next step on the highest-risk item. The open range \(n\ge5\), \(k-\ell>2^n\), through the subspace polynomial of the span. Formalizing the additive-roots theorem in Lean would also be natural, but that needs a Formalize prompt.

General statement. thm:per-order-value-small-multiplicity proves conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-formula for every \(k\), and conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions for \(k-\ell\le2^n\). cor:per-order-all-dimensions-small-n proves the latter for \(n\le4\).

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the origin-jet lemma (a)–(c), the per-order construction, the minimum lemma (i), the dichotomy and identity (D). The coefficient formula was checked against the kernel on random jets, and the theorem against all 477 computed values in its range. A thorough fresh-context reviewer checked the one-variable derivation, the multivariate factorization, steps 1–4 (including the repeated-argument cancellation, the root count over \(\mathbb F_2[y]\) and the final coefficient \(w_\ell\)), the corollary and the result files. It found no mathematical error. It asked for the general-\(\ell\) form of the dichotomy and of (D) in the corollary, the word "nonzero" in the root-count statement, and a note that only \(c\le2^n\) is used; all are made.

Assessment. This closes the line's goal. The characteristic-two per-order formula now holds in Menezes' range and below it, down to \(k-\ell\le2^n\), and the proof is short. The key is that \(x(Y)=\sum Y^{2^t}\) is additive, so the multiplicity conditions become root conditions for multi-additive forms on an \(\mathbb F_2\)-space of size \(2^n\). The remaining open part, \(k-\ell>2^n\) with \(n\ge5\), is BBDM's covering regime, where \(D\) is already known. Process: the closed coefficient form came from the kernel's one-variable tables. Hand work on the smallest open case then exposed the additive structure, which carried to the general proof in one step.

25 September 2026 — The per-order minimum in every dimension: δ(n,k,ℓ) = n + 2ℓ + Σ_{j<n}⌊(k−ℓ−1)/2^j⌋

Working proof: the all-dimension conjecture holds for all n ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ ℓ < k. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The additive-roots theorem proves \(\delta\ge\Phi\) when \(k-\ell\le2^n\). There the forms \(E_j\) have degree below \(2^n\), so vanishing on the span \(V\) of \(y_1,\ldots,y_n\) forces them to vanish identically. The open range is \(k-\ell>2^n\), first case \((n,c)=(5,33)\). This cycle removes the degree restriction by reducing modulo the subspace polynomial of \(V\).

Outcome. The lower bound holds in every case, so the all-dimension conjecture is a theorem. The proof couples the degree conditions at \(|e|=k-1-t\) for all \(t\). Their forms \(E^{(t)}_j\) satisfy a recursion under repeated arguments, which makes them vanish on \(V^j\). Reducing modulo \(L_V\) then isolates the coefficient \(w_\ell\).

The theorem

Working theorem (thm:per-order-value-all-dimensions). For all \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\),

\[ \delta(n,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\Bigl\lfloor\frac{k-\ell-1}{2^j}\Bigr\rfloor . \]

Setup. The upper bound is the all-dimension upper bound. For the lower bound, write \(m=k-\ell-1=q2^n+r\) with \(0\le r<2^n\), and let \(h=2q+s_2(r)\). By the Legendre form in the minimum lemma, \(\Phi=n+2(k-1)-h\). Also \(m\) is a sum of exactly \(h\) powers of two, each at most \(2^{n-1}\): \(2q\) copies of \(2^{n-1}\) and the binary digits of \(r\). Fix such an ordered decomposition \(m=\sum_{a=1}^h2^{s_a}\), with all \(s_a\le n-1\).

Suppose some admissible \(P\) of origin order \(\ell\) has \(\deg P\le\Phi-1\). By the origin-jet lemma we may take \(P=P(v)\), where \(\operatorname{ord}v=\ell\). Let \(w=v\circ x\), with \(x(Y)=\sum_{t\ge0}Y^{2^t}\); its parts satisfy \(w_d=0\) for \(d<\ell\) and \(w_\ell\ne0\).

  • Step 1: conditions (\(C_t\)). In the \(y\)-coefficient lemma take \(|e|=k-1-t\) with \(t\ge0\). The term \(n-|S|+2(k-1-t)\) is at least \(\Phi\) exactly when \(|S|\le h-2t\). So \((w\,u^S)_{k-1-t}=0\) for every \(S\subseteq[n]\) with \(|S|\le h-2t\). Möbius inversion over \(u^S=\sum_{T\subseteq S}x^T\) gives \((w\,x^T)_{k-1-t}=0\) for every set \(T\) of distinct indices with \(|T|\le h-2t\).
  • Step 2: the forms. For \(t\ge0\) and \(j\ge0\) put \[ E^{(t)}_j(Y_1,\ldots,Y_j)=\sum_{t_1,\ldots,t_j\ge0}Y_1^{2^{t_1}}\cdots Y_j^{2^{t_j}}\;w_{k-1-t-\sum_a2^{t_a}} . \] Each \(E^{(t)}_j\) is symmetric and additive in each variable, and \(E^{(t)}_j(y_{i_1},\ldots,y_{i_j})=(w\,x_{i_1}\cdots x_{i_j})_{k-1-t}\). Setting the first two arguments equal, the terms with \(t_1\ne t_2\) cancel in pairs. The diagonal terms \(t_1=t_2=s\) give \(Y^{2^{s+1}}\), which are the terms of \(E^{(t)}_{j-1}(Y,\ldots)\) with \(Y\)-exponent \(\ge2\). The \(Y^1\) terms of \(E^{(t)}_{j-1}\) are \(Y\,E^{(t+1)}_{j-2}\). Hence \[ E^{(t)}_j(Y,Y,Z_3,\ldots,Z_j)=E^{(t)}_{j-1}(Y,Z_3,\ldots,Z_j)+Y\,E^{(t+1)}_{j-2}(Z_3,\ldots,Z_j). \]
  • Step 3: vanishing on \(V^j\). We show by induction on \(j\), for all \(t\ge0\) simultaneously, that \(E^{(t)}_j\) vanishes on \(V^j\) whenever \(j\le h-2t\). The case \((t,j)\) uses the case \((t+1,j-2)\).
    • For \(j=0\) this is \(w_{k-1-t}=0\), which is (\(C_t\)) with \(T=\emptyset\).
    • For \(j\ge1\), a tuple of generators with distinct indices is covered by (\(C_t\)). A tuple with a repeated generator can be brought to the form \((Y,Y,\ldots)\) by symmetry. By the recursion it is then covered by \(E^{(t)}_{j-1}\), with \(j-1\le h-2t\), and \(E^{(t+1)}_{j-2}\), with \(j-2\le h-2(t+1)\). Both vanish on the span by induction.
    • So \(E^{(t)}_j\) vanishes at all tuples of generators, and by multi-additivity on \(V^j\).
  • Step 4: reduction modulo \(L_V\). Let \(L_V(Y)=\prod_{a\in V}(Y-a)\). \(V\) has \(2^n\) distinct elements in the domain \(\mathbb F_2[y]\), and \(L_V\) is monic and additive over the domain \(\mathbb F_2[y]\). For \(s\ge0\) let \(\rho_s(Y)\) be the remainder of \(Y^{2^s}\) modulo \(L_V\). It is additive, of degree \(<2^n\), and equal to \(Y^{2^s}\) for \(s<n\). Since \(L_V\) divides \(Y^{2^s}-\rho_s(Y)\), \(\rho_s(a)=a^{2^s}\) for \(a\in V\). Let \(R\) be \(E^{(0)}_h\) with each \(Y_a^{2^{t_a}}\) replaced by \(\rho_{t_a}(Y_a)\).
    • \(R\) agrees with \(E^{(0)}_h\) on \(V^h\), so \(R\) vanishes there.
    • \(R\) is multi-additive of degree \(<2^n\) in each variable. Specialize all but one variable to points of \(V\): the result has \(2^n\) roots and degree \(<2^n\), so it is zero. Repeating variable by variable gives \(R=0\).
  • Step 5: the coefficient of \(M^*=\prod_aY_a^{2^{s_a}}\) in \(R\). A monomial \(\prod_aY_a^{2^{t_a}}\) of \(E^{(0)}_h\) carries \(w_{k-1-\sum2^{t_a}}\), which is zero unless \(\sum2^{t_a}\le k-1-\ell=m\). Reduction never raises the \(Y\)-degree and lowers it strictly whenever some \(t_a\ge n\). \(M^*\) has \(Y\)-degree exactly \(m\). So the only monomial of \(E^{(0)}_h\) that contributes to \(M^*\) is \(M^*\) itself, and it is unreduced because all \(s_a<n\). Its coefficient is \(w_{k-1-m}=w_\ell\). Since \(R=0\), \(w_\ell=0\), a contradiction. \(\square\)

For \(q=0\) the reduction is trivial, since all \(t_a<n\), and step 3 can be replaced by the identical vanishing used in the additive-roots proof. The new ingredients for \(q\ge1\) are the coupling of the conditions (\(C_t\)) through the repeated-argument recursion and the reduction modulo \(L_V\). In the construction \(y_1^\ell F^{2q}g_{r+1}\), each block of \(2^n\) in \(m\) is paid by \(F^2\), and correspondingly each block costs two parts of size \(2^{n-1}\) in \(h\).

Consequences

  • The all-dimension conjecture (conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions) holds.
  • With the minimum lemma, \(D(n,k)=\min_\ell\Phi(n,k,\ell)\) follows again for every \(n\) and \(k\). This gives a second proof of the lower half of the complete formula, without Schwartz–Zippel or the one-dimension step.
  • The minimizing orders are the \(\ell\) maximizing \(2q+s_2(r)\) for \(k-\ell-1=q2^n+r\).
  • The results of the last three entries (\(n\le3\), \(n\le4\), \(k-\ell\le2^n\)) are special cases.

General statement. thm:per-order-value-all-dimensions, for all \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\). It proves conj:binary-multiplicity-per-order-all-dimensions.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the \(y\)-coefficient lemma, the origin-jet lemma, the Legendre form of \(\Phi\), and the construction. An adversarial fresh-context reviewer made these checks:

  • steps 1–5, including the induction ranges and the claim that only \(M^*\) contributes;
  • the recursion of step 2, symbolically with independent symbols \(w_d\), for \(k<30\), \(t\le3\), \(j=2,\ldots,4\) (research/results/bmd-r6-subspace-reduction/rev6_rec.py);
  • an independent brute force computing \(\delta\) by rank over all monomials (rev6_delta.py, rerun here): all 45 values for \(n=2\), \(k\le9\), 20 of them with \(k-\ell>4\), and the three values with \(k-\ell>8\) at \(n=3\), \(k\le10\), equal \(\Phi\) (brute-n2.txt, brute-n3.txt).

It found no gap. It asked for four changes, all made: the simultaneous induction, an accurate description of the case \(q=0\), removal of unused homogeneity claims, and a research next step.

Assessment. The per-order line is complete: \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) is determined for every \(n\), \(k\) and \(\ell\) by an elementary argument, and it recovers \(D(n,k)\). The next research step moves to a new route item, the \(\mathbb F_q\) grid. There \(y_i=x_i^q-x_i\) and the local root of \(X^q-X=y\) is again an additive series, so the root-count argument may carry over. Formalization in Lean and the preprint remain on the user's request. Process: once the degree was expressed through additive power series, both the lower and the upper ranges followed within two cycles. The kernel, witness mode and the exact data guided each step.
