25 September 2026 — Lean: every side claim of the preprint, and the digit argument for the minimum over the origin order

Formalization: the preprint's remarks, examples, comparisons and classical side facts verified in Lean; the minimum lemma now also by its own digit argument. Cycle bmd-f5-formalize-remarks. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Assignment. The user asked that the binary-multiplicity preprint contain no unformalized mathematical claim. The inventory (research/results/bmd-f5-formalize-remarks/scope-map.md) found 20 claims of version 2 without a Lean proof. Fifteen are formalized below, three were rephrased because the argument does not need them, and two were already proved and only needed links. Statements about the content of other papers are cited, not formalized; the paper now says so.

Binary digit sums

Working lemma (lem:binary-digit-sum-identities, claims/BinaryDigitSums.lean). For the binary digit sum \(s_2\):

  1. \(s_2(q2^n+r)=s_2(q)+s_2(r)\) for \(r<2^n\);
  2. \(2^{s_2(r)}\le r+1\), hence \(s_2(r)\le\lfloor\log_2(r+1)\rfloor\);
  3. \(s_2(r)\ge1\) for \(r\ge1\);
  4. \(s_2(x)=1\) iff \(x\) is a power of two;
  5. \(s_2(n+1)+v_2(n+1)=s_2(n)+1\).

Proof. All five use \(s_2(m)=(m\bmod 2)+s_2(\lfloor m/2\rfloor)\).

  • (1) By induction on \(n\), since halving \(q2^{n+1}+r\) gives \(q2^n+\lfloor r/2\rfloor\) with the same last digit as \(r\).
  • (2) By strong induction: for \(r=2a+b\), \(2^{b+s_2(a)}\le2^b(a+1)\le2a+b+1\).
  • (3) and (4) By halving.
  • (5) For even \(n\), \(n+1\) is odd and both sides are \(s_2(n/2)+1\). For \(n=2a+1\), \(n+1=2(a+1)\) and the claim reduces to the case \(a\). \(\square\)

The 2-adic valuation of Catalan numbers

Imported theorem (third-party:catalan-two-adic-valuation, third-party-claims/CatalanParity.lean). \(v_2(C_n)=s_2(n+1)-1\); hence \(C_n\) is odd iff \(n+1\) is a power of two. It is due to Alter and Kubota (J. Combin. Theory A 15 (1973), 243–256), and is Theorem 2.1 of Deutsch–Sagan (arXiv:math/0407326, read on 25 September 2026). Deutsch and Sagan note that it follows from Kummer's theorem, which is the route taken here.

Proof. By Kummer's theorem in digit form (Mathlib), \(v_2\binom{2n}{n}=2s_2(n)-s_2(2n)=s_2(n)\). Since \((n+1)C_n=\binom{2n}{n}\), \(v_2(C_n)=s_2(n)-v_2(n+1)=s_2(n+1)-1\) by (5) above. Then \(C_n\) is odd iff \(s_2(n+1)=1\), iff \(n+1\) is a power of two by (4). \(\square\)

The preprint uses it to identify the truncated product \(g_s\) with Menezes' Catalan truncation over \(\mathbb F_2\).

The Artin–Schreier root

Working lemma (lem:artin-schreier-additive-root, claims/ArtinSchreierSeries.lean). In \(\mathbb F_2[[Y]]\) the series \(A=\sum_{s\ge0}Y^{2^s}\) satisfies \(A^2+A=Y\), and its truncation below degree \(2^K\) is \(\hat x_K(Y)=\sum_{s<K}Y^{2^s}\).

Proof.

  • By induction on \(K\), in characteristic 2, \(\hat x_K^2+\hat x_K=Y+Y^{2^K}\): the step adds \(Y^{2^{K+1}}+Y^{2^K}\) and a cross term \(2\hat x_KY^{2^K}=0\).
  • The coefficients of \(A\) and \(\hat x_K\) agree below \(2^K\), since \(2^s<2^K\) iff \(s<K\). So \(A=\hat x_K+Y^{2^K}B\) for some \(B\), and \(A^2+A=Y+Y^{2^K}(1+2\hat x_KB+Y^{2^K}B^2+B)\).
  • Its coefficient at \(d<2^K\) is that of \(Y\). Taking \(K=d+1\) for each \(d\) gives \(A^2+A=Y\). \(\square\)

Arithmetic of the per-order value

Working lemma (lem:per-order-value-arithmetic, claims/PerOrderArithmetic.lean). For \(\ell<k\), write \(m=k-\ell-1=q2^n+r\) with \(0\le r<2^n\).

  1. \(\Phi+2q+s_2(r)=n+2(k-1)\), by the Legendre form.
  2. If \(m\ge2^n\), then \(\Phi+s_2(m)<n+2(k-1)\), because \(s_2(m)=s_2(q)+s_2(r)\le q+s_2(r)<2q+s_2(r)\). The construction \(y_1^\ell F^{2q}g_{r+1}\) therefore has degree below Menezes' form there.
  3. \(\Phi(n,k+2^n,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)+2^{n+1}-2\): item 1 for \(q+1\) and \(q\).
  4. \(\Phi=n+2k-2-s_2(m)\) when \(k-1\le n\), since then \(m\le n<2^n\). This includes \(k\ge2\), \(n\ge2k-3\).
  5. \(\Phi(n,k,k-1)=n+2k-2\).
  6. \(\Phi(n+1,k,\ell)=\Phi(n,k,\ell)+1+\lfloor m/2^n\rfloor\).
  7. The construction has degree exactly \(\Phi\): at most \(\Phi\) by the construction theorem, at least \(\Phi\) by the lower bound.
  8. For \(m<2^n\), every admissible \(P\) of origin order \(\ell\) has degree at least \(n+2k-2-s_2(m)\).
  9. \(s_2(m)\ge1\) for \(\ell+1<k\).
  10. The examples:
    • \(\Phi(1,3,0)=3\), attained by \((1+x)^3\), which has multiplicity \(\ge3\) at \(1\) (the factor \(1+x\) vanishes there) and value 1 at 0, with degree \(\le3\) and hence \(=3\) by the lower bound; Menezes' form gives \(4\).
    • \(\Phi(2,5,0)=8\) against \(9\).
  11. \(\operatorname{mult}_a(P)\le\operatorname{ord}_0P(a+z)\) for every substitution \(z\) without constant terms: \(P(a+z)\) is the shift \(P(a+x)\) at \(x=z\), and substitution of order-one elements does not lower the order.
  12. \(F\) has \(2^n-1\) factors, one for each nonzero \(u\in\mathbb F_2^n\). \(F^2\) has multiplicity \(\ge2\cdot2^{n-1}=2^n\) at every nonzero point, since multiplicities add under products, and degree \(\le2(2^n-1)\).
  13. Let \(\delta(n,k,\ell)\) be the least element of the set of total degrees of polynomials with multiplicity \(\ge k\) off the origin and exactly \(\ell\) at it (deltaSet). The per-order theorem says this least element is \(\Phi\). Items 4–6 and 10 therefore hold for \(\delta\) itself: the real range, the top order, \(\delta(n+1,k,\ell)=\delta(n,k,\ell)+1+\lfloor m/2^n\rfloor\), and \(\delta(1,3,0)=3\), \(\delta(2,5,0)=8\).

Arithmetic of D(n,k)

Working lemma (lem:degree-formula-arithmetic, claims/DegreeFormulaArithmetic.lean). From the closed form of \(D(n,k)\):

  • \(D(n,1)=n\) for \(n\ge2\); \(D(n,4)=n+4\) for \(n\ge4\); \(D(n,8)=n+11\) and \(D(n,15)=n+25\) for \(n\ge5\).
  • For \(1\le k<2^{n-2}\), \(D\) exceeds \(2k-\lfloor k/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) by \(n-2-\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\ge1\).
  • \(D(n+1,k)=D(n,k)+1\) for \(n\ge\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor+2\).
  • At \(k=3\cdot2^{n-1}\), \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor=n\) and \(D=2k-3\).
  • \(D(n,k)+1\le D(n+1,k)\) for \(n,k\ge1\). The last follows from the minimum over \(\ell\) and item 6 above: at a minimizing order \(\ell\) for \(n+1\), \(D(n,k)\le\Phi(n,k,\ell)\le\Phi(n+1,k,\ell)-1\).
  • Every \(k\)-admissible polynomial is nonzero, since \(\operatorname{mult}_0(0)=\infty>k-1\).

These identities first concern the closed form. Let \(D(n,k)\) be the least element of the set of degrees of \(k\)-admissible polynomials (degreeSet). The complete formula says this least element is the closed form, so every item holds for \(D\) itself; Lean states them in that form (D_examples, D_excess, D_succ, D_three_mul, D_add_one_le).

Each of these is a case computation with \(\lfloor\log_2\rfloor\).

The minimum over the origin order by the digit argument

claims/PerOrderMinimumDirect.lean proves part (ii) of the minimum lemma by the preprint's own digit argument (min_phi_direct), independently of the Schwartz–Zippel route that min_phi used.

  • With \(h(m)=2\lfloor m/2^n\rfloor+s_2(m\bmod2^n)\), item 1 above reads \(\Phi+h(k-\ell-1)=n+2(k-1)\).
  • The closed form \(h_{\max}\) is \(\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\) when \(Q=0\), and \(2Q+n+\lfloor(R+1)/2^{n-1}\rfloor-2\) when \(Q\ge1\), for \(k-1=Q2^n+R\).
  • It satisfies \(D+h_{\max}=n+2(k-1)\), bounds \(h\) on \([0,k-1]\) (by \(s_2(r)\le\lfloor\log_2(r+1)\rfloor\) and \(\lfloor\log_2x\rfloor+2\le n+\lfloor x/2^{n-1}\rfloor\) for \(1\le x\le2^n\)), and is attained at \(2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}-1\), at \(Q2^n+2^{\lfloor\log_2(R+1)\rfloor}-1\), or at \(Q2^n-1\), as in the preprint.
  • The same module proves the characterization of the extremal orders: \(\Phi=D\) iff \(h(k-\ell-1)\) is maximal; for \(k\le2^n\), iff \(s_2(k-\ell-1)=\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor\); and \(\ell=k-2^{\lfloor\log_2k\rfloor}\) is such an order.
  • With \(\delta=\Phi\) and \(D=\) the closed form, these characterize the orders with \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=D(n,k)\) for the least degrees themselves (delta_eq_D_iff, delta_eq_D_of_le_two_pow, delta_extremal_example).

The preprint

  • Every claim of version 2 now carries a Lean link, except the grid-vanishing lemma, which is Mathlib's.
  • Three claims the arguments do not need were rephrased or removed:
    • the exact degree and multiplicity of \(F^2\), now stated as bounds;
    • that a form of high degree can vanish on \(V\) without being zero, and that the reduction is not needed for \(q=0\);
    • \(g_0=g_1\) in Lean.
  • The substitution fact of Section 2 is now stated over \(\mathbb F_2\), where it is used and proved.
  • The hyperplane-cover remark now says the origin order is below \(k\), which is what the theorem proves.
  • Alter–Kubota and Deutsch–Sagan are cited for the Catalan parity.
  • A fresh-context audit found nine further assertions without a Lean link. They were resolved as follows:
    • the zero polynomial, the factors of \(F\) and the bounds for \(F^2\) are now formalized;
    • the outline now says multiplicity at least \(2^{n-1}\);
    • the sentence on products of covers now cites Corollary 1.3;
    • two unneeded asides (why reduction fails, and the valuation of \(r!\)) were dropped;
    • the Hasse-derivative sentence is now DKSS's terminology.
    The audit also found that the new modules stated the facts about \(D\) for the closed form only, and compared the construction with the digit-sum bound rather than with the degree of Menezes' polynomial. The first is fixed by the least-degree restatements. For the second, the text now says "has degree below that bound".
  • The aggregate module claims/BinaryMultiplicityDegreePaper2.lean lists 64 declarations, and the Lean links point to commit 168a0c8e.

General statement. The all-parameter claims are lem:per-order-value-arithmetic, lem:degree-formula-arithmetic, lem:binary-digit-sum-identities, lem:artin-schreier-additive-root and third-party:catalan-two-adic-valuation.

25 September 2026 — Novelty check for the per-order value: citing works of Sauermann–Wigderson, Bishnoi et al. and Menezes

Literature check: no prior determination of δ over F₂ for 2 ≤ n < k−1 found; two imported statements added. Cycle bmd-p2-novelty-check. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, low effort).

Question. Before submission, is the per-order value \(\delta(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) already known for \(n<k-1\)? The earlier check (prior art) used two web searches only.

What was searched

  • Menezes' versions. arXiv lists only version 1 of 2609.19009 (14 August 2026).
  • Citing works. The Semantic Scholar citation lists were read on 25 September 2026:
    • Sauermann–Wigderson (arXiv 2010.00077): 18 citing works;
    • Bishnoi–Boyadzhiyska–Das–Mészáros (arXiv 2101.11947): 9;
    • Menezes: none.
  • Checked in full text. Each citing work that could bear on polynomial degrees:
    • Huang–Wang–Wang (2603.14262v3) and Ghosh–Kayal–Nandi–Venkitesh (2307.16881) work over \(\mathbb R\).
    • Srinivasan–Venkitesh (2111.05445) consider positive characteristic but only simple vanishing.
    • Nagy–Pach–Tomon (hyperplane covers of finite spaces) study irredundant covers without multiplicities.
    • Aichinger–Schmitt–Zhan (2506.15281) prove punctured Nullstellensätze without multiplicities, and refer to Ball–Serra (2009) for the version with multiplicities. Ball–Serra predates the problem, which Sauermann–Wigderson and Bishnoi et al. posed as open.
    • Bishnoi–Boyadzhiyska–Das–den Bakker (2305.00825) cover grids in \(\mathbb R^2\) by lines.
  • Keyword searches: high-order vanishing on the cube in characteristic two, punctured multiplicity Nullstellensätze, and answers to the question of Bishnoi et al. They found nothing beyond the works above. The ECCC hit on the multiplicity Schwartz–Zippel lemma was not opened.

Findings

  • Menezes names the small-dimension case as open. His Section 8, "Smaller dimension", says the hypothesis \(n\ge k-1\) is substantive. It gives \(\delta_F(1;k,\ell)=k+\ell\) over every field (his equation (36)), and uses \(G_{3,0}+G_{3,(1)}=(x-1)^3\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) to show that "a small-dimensional formula must account for cancellations between the Catalan basis elements". That is our range \(n<k-1\). Our value agrees at \(n=1\): \(\Phi(1,k,\ell)=1+2\ell+(k-\ell-1)=k+\ell\), now in Lean as delta_one. The case \(n=1\) is therefore his, and the new range is \(2\le n<k-1\).
  • The real range is larger than we cited. Huang, Wang and Wang (Theorem 1.8 of arXiv 2603.14262v3, specialized to \(m=1\)) prove over \(\mathbb R\) that the least degree is \(n+2k-3\) for \(\ell\le k-2\) and \(n\ge k-1\), improving Sauermann–Wigderson's \(n\ge2k-3\). Menezes cites this as their Theorem 1.7, an earlier version's numbering.
  • No prior result. No work found determines \(\delta\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) for \(2\le n<k-1\), or answers the question of Bishnoi et al. below \(k\ge2^{n-2}\). This is a bounded search; it does not establish that no such result exists.

Changes to the preprint.

  • The \(n=1\) example credits Menezes' Section 8.
  • The Menezes paragraph reports his univariate value and his small-dimension remark.
  • The comparison with the reals cites Huang–Wang–Wang.
  • The search paragraph describes this check.
  • The Lean pin moved to ab7b3377, which adds delta_one; the aggregate lists 65 declarations.

General statement. The all-parameter claim under test is thm:per-order-value-all-dimensions; this entry changes its prior-art context only. The arithmetic lemma lem:per-order-value-arithmetic gains the univariate case.

Verification gate. Menezes' Section 8 and Theorem 1.1, and Huang–Wang–Wang's Theorem 1.8, were read in their own text, not from summaries. verify.sh passes for the aggregate at the new commit (research/results/bmd-p2-novelty-check/lean-verification-paper2.txt), and a fresh-kernel replay passes in 202 s (kernel-replay.txt). No reviewer was used, since no new mathematics beyond the one-line identity was drafted.

25 September 2026 — The per-order value over every field of characteristic two, by descent to F₂

Working proof, verified in Lean: δ_R = Φ for every commutative ring R of characteristic two. Cycle bmd-f7-char2-descent. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, low effort).

Question. A blind referee of preprint version 2 suggested that the per-order theorem holds over every field of characteristic two, not only over \(\mathbb F_2\). Does it, and can it be proved cheaply enough to state in the paper with a Lean proof?

The descent lemma

Let \(R\) be a commutative ring of characteristic two, so that \(\mathbb F_2\subseteq R\). For an additive map \(\varphi\colon R\to\mathbb F_2\) and \(P=\sum_uc_ux^u\in R[x_\sigma]\) put \(P^\varphi=\sum_u\varphi(c_u)x^u\in\mathbb F_2[x_\sigma]\).

Lemma. For every \(a\in\mathbb F_2^\sigma\), each coefficient of \(P^\varphi(a+z)\) is the image under \(\varphi\) of the corresponding coefficient of \(P(a+z)\), and \(\deg P^\varphi\le\deg P\).

Proof. The coefficient of \(z^\gamma\) in \(P(a+z)\) is \(\sum_uc_ut_{u,\gamma}\), where \(t_{u,\gamma}\in\mathbb F_2\) is the coefficient of \(z^\gamma\) in \((a+z)^u\); the entries of \(a\) lie in \(\mathbb F_2\). For \(t\in\{0,1\}\), \(\varphi(ct)=t\varphi(c)\), and \(\varphi\) is additive, so \(\varphi\) maps this coefficient to \(\sum_u\varphi(c_u)t_{u,\gamma}\), the coefficient of \(z^\gamma\) in \(P^\varphi(a+z)\). A monomial with coefficient zero in \(P\) has coefficient \(\varphi(0)=0\) in \(P^\varphi\). \(\square\)

The theorem

Theorem (thm:per-order-value-char-two). For every commutative ring \(R\) of characteristic two (in particular every field of characteristic two), \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), the least degree of \(P\in R[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\) with multiplicity \(\ge k\) at the nonzero points of \(\{0,1\}^n\) and exactly \(\ell\) at the origin is \(\Phi(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\), and it is attained. For \(n,k\ge1\) the least degree with multiplicity \(\ge k\) off the origin and \(\le k-1\) at it is \(D(n,k)\), the value of the complete formula.

Proof.

  • Upper bound. The \(\mathbb F_2\) polynomial of the per-order construction, read in \(R[x]\), has the same Hasse coefficients at the cube points, as images under the injective map \(\mathbb F_2\to R\), and the same degree.
  • Lower bound. Let \(P\) have multiplicity \(\ge k\) at the nonzero cube points and origin order \(\ell\). Then \(P\ne0\), and some coefficient \(c\) of degree \(\ell\) of \(P(z)\) is nonzero. \(R\) is an \(\mathbb F_2\)-vector space, so some \(\mathbb F_2\)-linear, hence additive, \(\varphi\colon R\to\mathbb F_2\) has \(\varphi(c)\ne0\). By the lemma, \(P^\varphi\) has multiplicity \(\ge k\) at the nonzero cube points, its coefficients of degree below \(\ell\) at the origin vanish, and its coefficient at the monomial of \(c\) is \(\varphi(c)\ne0\); so its origin order is \(\ell\). By the per-order theorem over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(\deg P\ge\deg P^\varphi\ge\Phi(n,k,\ell)\).
  • \(D\). A polynomial with origin order \(\le k-1\) has origin order \(\ell\) for some \(\ell<k\); minimize with the minimum lemma, which is attained at an \(\mathbb F_2\) polynomial. \(\square\)

Relation to the record. For fields this is a consequence of recorded results: the counting principle holds on \(\mathbb F_2\)-subspace grids of any characteristic-two field (thm:root-curve-theory-char-p). With \(S=\mathbb F_2\), the binary construction supplies its attained condition, and the binary count its counted condition. The descent is a shorter proof of that consequence. It extends to commutative rings, and unlike the counting route it is formalized. A first draft used an \(\mathbb F_2\)-basis of the span of the coefficients; the Lean proof uses a single functional, which is enough.

Lean. formalization/claims/PerOrderCharTwo.lean, for every CommRing R with CharP R 2:

  • coeffMap and coeff_shift_coeffMap give the lemma, via the commutation map_shift of the shift with \(\mathbb F_2\to R\);
  • lower_bound_char_two and per_order_value_char_two give the theorem;
  • degree_complete_char_two gives \(D\).

Points of the cube are the images of points of \(\mathbb F_2^n\). The needed functional comes from Mathlib's Module.forall_dual_apply_eq_zero_iff, with \(R\) viewed as an \(\mathbb F_2\)-module.

Preprint. Section 5 of publications/binary-multiplicity-degree/ gains the descent lemma and the theorem (Lemma 5.4, Theorem 5.5). The abstract and the introduction say the values hold over every field of characteristic two, and the closing remark now names odd characteristic and characteristic zero below Menezes' range as the open fields.

General statement. thm:per-order-value-char-two: for every commutative ring \(R\) of characteristic two, all \(n\ge1\) and \(0\le\ell<k\), \(\delta_R(n,k,\ell)=n+2\ell+\sum_{j<n}\lfloor(k-\ell-1)/2^j\rfloor\), and \(D_R(n,k)=D(n,k)\).

Verification gate.

  • Dependencies re-read: the Lean statements of per_order_value and min_phi, and the recorded counting principle.
  • Record searches: four phrasings (extension of scalars, every field of characteristic two, subfield coefficients, the cube in characteristic two). They found no descent lemma; the counting-principle relation is stated above.
  • Reviewer: a fresh-context reviewer found no error. It required that each basis component in the first draft be shown nonzero, that the \(D\) clause state \(k\ge1\), and that the unused hypothesis "field" be dropped. All three are applied: the theorem is stated for commutative rings, and the single-functional proof needs no basis.
  • Lean: verify.sh passes for the module (6 declarations) and for the aggregate (69), with the standard axioms only, and a fresh-kernel replay of the aggregate passes in 204 s. The reports are in research/results/bmd-f7-char2-descent/.

25 September 2026 — Order zero on the 3-cube is 2k − ⌊k/5⌋: two collision lines meet at each double point

Working proof: δ(3,k,0) = 2k − ⌊k/5⌋ in characteristic ≠ 2, and ℓ₀(d+1,m) ≥ 2 for m ≤ 5d − 2. Cycle bmd-r43-scheme-review. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. Open statement 1 asks for \(\ell_0(d+1,4d+\rho-1)\ge B(d,\rho)\). Its weakest case, \(\ell_0\ge1\) whenever \(j=d-\rho\ge1\), is the order-zero lower bound \(\delta(3,k,0)\ge2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\). The local-limits lemma showed that evaluation at a point, and restriction to one collision divisor, cannot exclude \(m\ge4d+1\). The double-point computation located the whole lower bound at the three double points, where two collision divisors cross. What does each of the two divisors impose at their common point, and do the two conditions together force \(W_m\) to vanish there? The test ends with a proof, or with the span of the two conditions missing \(T^m\).

Setting

\(F\) has characteristic \(\ne2\), \(R=F[y_1,y_2,y_3]\), \(d\ge1\), \(m\ge0\). Put \(w_i=(1+4y_iT)^{1/2}\) and \(z_i=r_0(y_iT)=(1-w_i)/2\), the root of \(z^2-z=y_iT\) with \(z(0)=0\). A solution is \(W=(W_0,\ldots,W_m)\in R^{m+1}\) with \(\sum_cW_c[T^c]f=0\) for the basis \(f=T^Qz^r\) (\(r\in\{0,1\}^3\), \(2Q+|r|\le d\)) of \(V_{3,d}\). By the threshold-ideal lemma the \(W_m\) of homogeneous solutions form the ideal \(I^{(d+1)}_m\), whose initial degree is \(\ell_0(d+1,m)\). For a vector \(W(P)\) of values and a series \(g\in F[[T]]\), "\(W(P)\) kills \(g\)" means \(\sum_{c\le m}W_c(P)[T^c]g=0\).

The two-line window lemma

Working lemma (lem:cube-double-point-windows). Let \(W\) be a solution, \(c\in F^\times\), \(P=(c,c,0)\), and \(w=(1+4cT)^{1/2}\in F[[T]]\). Then \(W(P)\) kills \(w^i\) for every integer \(i\) with \(-(2d-1)\le i\le3d-1\). The same holds at \((c,0,c)\) and \((0,c,c)\), with the equal coordinates' \(w\).

Proof. Put \(a=4c\), so \(aT=w^2-1\).

  • Restriction to \(y_3=0\). Part 3 of the local-limits lemma (for the divisor \(y_3=0\), after permuting coordinates) shows that \(W|_{y_3=0}\) kills \(T^Qz_1^{r_1}z_2^{r_2}\) whenever \(Q+r_1+r_2\le d\). Its proof uses only that \(W\) is a solution (the lifts are integral and \(W\) kills \(\tau(V)\otimes F(y)\)), not \(W_m=1\). Since \(z_i=(1-w_i)/2\) and 2 is invertible, the span \(U\) of these functions is also spanned by the \(T^Qw_1^{r_1}w_2^{r_2}\), \(Q+r_1+r_2\le d\).
  • The arc. Put \(y(\varepsilon)=(c(1+\varepsilon),c,0)\). Then \(w_2=w\) and \(w_1^2=w^2+\varepsilon(w^2-1)\). Since \(T=(w^2-1)/a\), the functions \(T^Qw^r\) with \(Q+r\le d\), \(r\in\{0,1\}\), have degrees \(2Q+r\) in \(w\) covering \(0,\ldots,2d\) once, so they span the polynomials of degree \(\le2d\) in \(w\). Likewise the \(T^Qw_1w^r\) with \(Q+1+r\le d\) span \(w_1\cdot\{\text{polynomials of degree}\le2d-2\text{ in }w\}\). All of this is over \(F[\varepsilon]\).
  • The lifts. By induction on \(k\), \((w_1-w)^k=A_k(w)+w_1B_k(w)\) with \(A_k,B_k\in F[\varepsilon][w]\) of degrees \(\le k\) and \(\le k-1\): \(A_1=-w\), \(B_1=1\), \(A_{k+1}=((1+\varepsilon)w^2-\varepsilon)B_k-wA_k\) and \(B_{k+1}=A_k-wB_k\). So \(G_{k,e}=w^e(w_1-w)^k\) lies in the \(F[\varepsilon]\)-span of \(U\) at \(y(\varepsilon)\) when \(k=0\), \(e\le2d\), or \(k\ge1\), \(e+k\le2d-1\). Moreover \(w_1-w=\varepsilon(w^2-1)/(w_1+w)\), and \(w_1+w\in F[\varepsilon][[T]]\) has constant term 2, so it is a unit of \(F[[\varepsilon]][[T]]\). Hence \(H_{k,e}=\varepsilon^{-k}G_{k,e}=w^e(w^2-1)^k(w_1+w)^{-k}\) lies in \(F[[\varepsilon]][[T]]\), with value \(\bar H_{k,e}=2^{-k}w^{e-k}(w^2-1)^k\) at \(\varepsilon=0\).
  • Specialization. \(W(y(\varepsilon))\in F[\varepsilon]^{m+1}\) kills every \(G_{k,e}\), so it kills \(H_{k,e}\) (an identity in \(F((\varepsilon))\) whose terms lie in \(F[[\varepsilon]]\)). Setting \(\varepsilon=0\), \(W(P)\) kills \(\bar H_{k,e}\).
  • The first window. For \(-(2d-1)\le j\le-1\) take \((k,e)=(-j,0)\), and for \(0\le j\le2d\) take \((0,j)\). Then \(\bar H\) is a Laurent polynomial in \(w\) with lowest term \(\pm2^{-k}w^j\) and exponents in \([j,|j|]\subseteq[-(2d-1),2d]\). By triangularity, every \(w^i\) with \(-(2d-1)\le i\le2d\) is a combination of these \(\bar H\), as Laurent polynomials and hence as series. So \(W(P)\) kills them.
  • The Möbius transport. The boundary theorem substitutes \(T=\tilde T/(1-a_2\tilde T)\) with \(a=4y\), \(\tilde a=(a_1-a_2,-a_2,a_3-a_2)\), and shows that \(f\mapsto\tilde w_2^{\,d}f\) maps \(V_d(a)\) onto \(\tilde V_d(\tilde a)\) by a unitriangular change of basis with coefficients polynomial in \(a_2\). Let \(\sigma\) be the substitution \(\tilde T=T/(1+a_2T)\); then \(\sigma(\tilde w_2)=w_2^{-1}\). Let \(\Lambda\) send the truncation of \(g\in F[a][[\tilde T]]\) to the truncation of \(w_2^{\,d}\sigma(g)\). It is well defined modulo \(T^{m+1}\), polynomial in \(a_2\), and \(\Lambda(\tilde T^m)\equiv T^m\). Hence \(\tilde W=W\circ\Lambda\), read as a polynomial in \(\tilde y=\tilde a/4\), is a solution in the variables \(\tilde y\), with \(\tilde W_m=W_m\).
  • The second window. \(P\) maps to \(\tilde P=(0,-c,-c)\) on \(\tilde y_1=0\). The first window for \(\tilde W\) there (permuted, with \(\tilde w=(1-4c\tilde T)^{1/2}\)) says that \(W(P)\) kills \(\Lambda(\tilde w^{\,j})=w_2^{\,d}\sigma(\tilde w)^j\) for \(-(2d-1)\le j\le2d\). At \(P\), \(w_2=w\) and \(\sigma(\tilde w)=(1-4cT/(1+4cT))^{1/2}=w^{-1}\). So \(W(P)\) kills \(w^{d-j}\), that is \(w^i\) for \(-d\le i\le3d-1\).
  • The two windows together cover \([-(2d-1),3d-1]\). The other double points follow by permuting coordinates, which preserves the system. \(\square\)

Each window alone has \(4d\) exponents, which is the dimension of \(V_{3,d}\). Their union has \(5d-1\).

The order-zero value

Theorem (thm:cube-order-zero-value). Over every field of characteristic \(\ne2\):

  1. for \(d\ge1\) and \(0\le m\le5d-2\), every solution \(W\) vanishes (all of \(W_0,\ldots,W_m\)) at every point \((c,c,0)\), \((c,0,c)\), \((0,c,c)\) with \(c\ne0\); so every homogeneous \(W_m\) vanishes at \((1{:}1{:}0)\), \((1{:}0{:}1)\), \((0{:}1{:}1)\), and \(\ell_0(d+1,m)\ge2\);
  2. \(\delta(3,k,0)=2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\) for every \(k\ge2\).

Proof.

  • Part 1. Fix \(c\ne0\), so \(w=1+2cT+O(T^2)\). For \(0\le c'\le m\), the Laurent polynomial \(E_{c'}=(2c)^{-c'}w^{-(2d-1)}(w-1)^{c'}\) has exponents in \([-(2d-1),c'-2d+1]\), inside the lemma's range because \(c'\le m\le5d-2\). As a series, \(E_{c'}=T^{c'}+O(T^{c'+1})\). So the truncations of \(E_0,\ldots,E_m\) are unitriangular and span all of \(F^{m+1}\), and \(W(P)\) kills them all by the lemma. Hence \(W(P)=0\), and in particular \(W_m(P)=0\). The same holds at the other two lines of points. For a homogeneous solution, \(W_m\) is a form, and \(c=1\) gives the projective points. A nonzero constant does not vanish at \(P\). A nonzero linear form cannot vanish at three non-collinear points, and these three are non-collinear because \(\det\begin{pmatrix}1&1&0\\1&0&1\\0&1&1\end{pmatrix}=-2\ne0\). So \(I^{(d+1)}_m\) has no nonzero element of degree \(\le1\).
  • Part 2. For \(n=3\) the savings are \(h=2k+1-\delta\). By the threshold-ideal lemma, \(h(k,0)\ge h\) exactly when \(I^{(h)}_{k-1}\) has a nonzero element of degree 0, and \(I^{(h)}\) decreases in \(h\). Let \(d=\lfloor k/5\rfloor+1\). Then \(m=k-1\le5d-2\), so \(I^{(d+1)}_{k-1}\), and every \(I^{(h)}_{k-1}\) with \(h\ge d+1\), has no element of degree 0. So \(h(k,0)\le d\), which gives \(\delta(3,k,0)\ge2k+1-d=2k-\lfloor k/5\rfloor\). The order-zero upper bound gives equality for \(k\ge2\). \(\square\)

This proves part 2 of conj:cube-dimension-three-thresholds. Part 1 covers \(m=4d+\rho-1\) for every \(1\le\rho\le d-1\). Where the zero-scheme lemma applies (\(\rho\ge2\)), it shows that each double point lies in \(Z_{4d+\rho-1}\), which was previously only computed.

Why single restrictions failed. The earlier check found the restricted problem on one collision divisor solvable for \(m\ge4d+1\). That is consistent: one window has \(4d\) exponents, and its truncations contain \(T^m\) only for \(m\le4d-1\). The new ingredient is the double point, where the restrictions to the two divisors impose windows shifted against each other by the Möbius transport.

The local ideals, and a fat-point route for ρ ≥ d/2

Computation. research/tools/bmd_cube_point_ideal.py is new. It reads the echelon rows written by the point-scheme kernel and prints a basis of \(\mathcal J_{N,K}\), the truncated stalk at \(P=(1{:}1{:}0)\), in the coordinates \(s=y_3\), \(t=y_1-y_2\) (chart \(y_2=1\)). The \(N,K\) are those at which the colengths had stabilized in the previous entry. Results over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\), in research/results/bmd-r43-scheme-review/ideal-*.txt:

  • \((d,\rho)=(3,2)\): \(J=(s^2,t^2)\), colength 4.
  • \((5,3)\): \(J=(s^4,s^2t^2,t^4)=(s^2,t^2)^2\), colength 12.
  • \((4,2)\): \(J=(s^4+t^4,\,s^2t^2)\), colength 16.
  • \((5,2)\): the lowest elements are \(s^4t^2+\tfrac1{16}t^8+\cdots\) and \(s^2t^4+\tfrac1{16}s^8+\cdots\), with \(s^{10}-t^{10}\) in degree 10; colength 44.

Every computed ideal is invariant under \(s\leftrightarrow t\) and \(t\mapsto-t\). This fits a symmetry of the problem: the coordinate permutations and the Möbius transport \(y\mapsto(y_1-y_2,-y_2,y_3-y_2)\) generate a group acting linearly on \(\mathbb P^2\). The transport sends \(P\) to \((0,-1,-1)\); composed with the transposition \(y_1\leftrightarrow y_3\) it fixes \(P\) and exchanges the lines \(y_1=y_2\) and \(y_3=0\). The symmetry of the ideals is only observed, not proved.

A second check of the windows. The new compiled kernel research/tools/bmd_cube_arc_limits.cpp saturates \(\tau(V)\) over \(\mathbb F_p[[\varepsilon]]\) along arcs through \(P\). It was validated against a Python reference on seven arcs at \(d=3\), \(m=13\). It prints each limit space, which \(w^i\) it contains, and the span of the limits. Along \((s,t)=(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^7)\) and \((\varepsilon^7,\varepsilon)\), for \(d=3,\ldots,6\) and \(m=5d-2,5d-1\) (extreme-arc-windows-d3-d6.txt), the limit spaces are exactly the spans of \(w^i\) for \(i\in[-d,3d-1]\) and for \(i\in[-(2d-1),2d]\). Their span has dimension \(5d-1\). It contains \(T^m\) at \(m=5d-2\) and not at \(m=5d-1\), where order-zero solutions exist. So, as a finite check over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\) for these two arcs and \(d\le6\), the lemma's windows are the full limits. A single transverse arc gives less: at \(d=3\), \(m=13\), the limit along \((s,t)=(\varepsilon,\varepsilon)\) spans \(w^i\) for \(i\in[-4,7]\), and at \(d=4\), \(m=18\), it has orders \(0,\ldots,14,16\) (runs in the session archive of this cycle).

Fat-point lemma (lem:cube-double-point-fat-points). For every \(j\ge1\), a form of degree \(<3j\) with multiplicity \(\ge2j\) at the three double points \((1{:}1{:}0)\), \((1{:}0{:}1)\), \((0{:}1{:}1)\) is zero.

Proof. The three lines \(y_1+y_2-y_3=0\), \(y_1-y_2+y_3=0\) and \(-y_1+y_2+y_3=0\), whose product is \(L_2\) up to sign, each pass through exactly two of the points, and each point lies on exactly two of them. Let \(f\ne0\) have degree \(\ell<3j\) and multiplicity \(\ge2j\) at the points. If one of the lines does not divide \(f\), Bezout along it gives \(\ell\ge4j>\ell\). So \(L_2\) divides \(f\), and \(f/L_2\) has degree \(\ell-3<3(j-1)\) and multiplicity \(\ge2(j-1)\) at each point. By induction down to \(j=0\), where a nonzero form of negative degree is impossible, no such \(f\) exists. \(\square\)

Conjecture (conj:cube-double-point-fat-ideal). Let \(\rho\ge2\), \(m=4d+\rho-1\), \(j=d-\rho\) with \(1\le j\le\rho\), \(Z=Z_m\), and \(J=J_{Z,P}\) at each double point \(P\). Then \(J\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^{2j}\).

Here \(B(d,\rho)=3j\) (\(q=j\) parts, all equal to 2). Every element of \(I_m\) lies in the saturated ideal of \(Z\) (zero-scheme lemma), so \(\ell_0=\operatorname{indeg}I_m\ge\alpha(Z)\ge\alpha(\text{double-point part of }Z)\). By the fat-point lemma the conjecture therefore gives the lower bound of open statement 1, \(\ell_0\ge B\), for all \(\rho\ge d/2\). The upper bound \(\ell_0\le B\) is the product bound. Evidence. The cases with \(d\le7\), \(\rho\ge2\), \(1\le j\le\rho\) are \((d,\rho)=(3,2)\), \((4,3)\), \((4,2)\), \((5,4)\), \((5,3)\), \((6,5)\), \((6,4)\), \((6,3)\), \((7,6)\), \((7,5)\), \((7,4)\). In each, the previous entry's colength sequence shows \(\mathcal J_{N,K}\subseteq\mathfrak m^{2j}\) with \(K\ge2j\) (\(K\ge6\), \(j\le3\)). By the certified local bound, whose review this cycle completed (below), that gives \(J\subseteq\mathfrak m^{2j}\) over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\). For \(j>\rho\) fat points are not enough: at \((5,2)\), \(J\subseteq\mathfrak m^6\) gives only 9, not \(B=11\).

General statement. thm:cube-order-zero-value and lem:cube-double-point-windows, for every \(d\ge1\) and \(m\ge0\) in characteristic \(\ne2\). lem:cube-double-point-fat-points for every \(j\ge1\). conj:cube-double-point-fat-ideal is the local statement that would give the lower bound of open statement 1 for \(\rho\ge d/2\).

Verification gate. The argument passed the gate.

  • Dependencies re-read: Part 3 of the local-limits lemma and its proof, the collision-lift lemma, the boundary theorem's Möbius transport, Part 1 of the threshold-ideal lemma, and the order-zero upper bound.
  • Record searches: order zero, lower bound, double point, window, restriction to two divisors, arc limits. None found an order-zero lower bound or a window statement; the recorded order-zero lower bound was only computed.
  • Checks: the arc kernel was validated against a Python reference, and its extreme-arc limits equal the two windows for \(d\le6\). The theorem agrees with the earlier per-order kernel thresholds at order zero (\(k\le30\) over \(\mathbb F_{101}\), \(k\le32\) over \(\mathbb F_3\)).
  • Reviewer: a fresh-context referee found the lemma and both parts of the theorem correct. It confirmed that \(c\ne0\) and characteristic \(\ne2\) are both used. Its required changes are made: Part 1 is stated on the lines \((c,c,0)\), so it covers non-homogeneous solutions; the fat-point lemma is stated for every \(j\) and registered; the conjecture is stated precisely, with conditional evidence, listed cases and \(K\ge2j\); the symmetry needs the transposition; the arc-limit claims are qualified as finite checks; and the scope of the zero-scheme remark is stated.
  • Pending review of the certified local bound. A second fresh-context referee reviewed the certified lower bound from truncated local solutions and the kernel bmd_cube_point_scheme.cpp. It found both correct, including the Möbius-frame lifts with the factor \(w_2^{\,d}\), the truncated system and the output rows. Only the inclusion \(w_2^{\,d}\tilde V\subseteq F[y]\cdot V\) is used. Its required changes are recorded here, since the earlier entry is not rewritten. The kernels divide by \(j+1\le m+2\), so they now refuse \(p\le m+2\); every recorded run used \(p=1000003\). Each certificate holds only in the characteristic of its run. The closing Artin–Rees remark there is unproved and is not used. The lemma's status is now a working proof.

Assessment. This cycle moves open statement 1. Its weakest case, order zero, is now proved in every characteristic \(\ne2\), together with \(\ell_0\ge2\) below it. The proof comes from the mechanism that the double-point data suggested: the two collision lines through a double point each impose a window of \(4d\) powers of \(w\), and the Möbius transport shifts one window against the other, so together they cover \(5d-1\). What remains is higher order. The law needs \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^{2j}\) for \(\rho\ge d/2\), and contact conditions beyond fat points otherwise. The natural next step is to push the same two restrictions to higher order in the arc parameter, where \(W\)'s derivatives at \(P\) enter. Process: the arc tool first ran as a Python loop and hit the guard against repeated short runs; moving it into a compiled kernel that takes a whole series in one run was the right fix.

25 September 2026 — Solutions vanish to order 2j at the 3-cube's double points, and first-order arcs cannot see it

Finite check and conjecture: local solutions vanish to order 2j at the double points (certified in four cases). Cycle bmd-r44-first-order. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The order-zero theorem shows that every solution vanishes at the three double points when \(j=d-\rho\ge1\). For \(\rho=d-1\) the torsion-partition law needs one more order: \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^2\), which gives \(\ell_0\ge3=B\). The previous entry proposed to get it from the same two restrictions at first order. Do the first-order conditions along arcs force the linear part of \(W_m\) to vanish? If not, what do the true local solutions do at first and higher order? The test ends with a proof of \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^2\), or with the exact vanishing order of the local solutions and the reason the arc method misses it.

First-order arc conditions are too weak

Write a solution near \(P=(1{:}1{:}0)\) (chart \(y_2=1\), \(s=y_3\), \(t=y_1-y_2\)) as \(W=sA+tB+O(\mathfrak m_P^2)\); this uses \(W(P)=0\). Along an arc with \(v(s)=a\), \(v(t)=b\) and leading coefficients \(\sigma,\tau\), the leading coefficient of \(W(\gamma(\varepsilon))\) is \(\sigma A\) if \(a<b\), \(\tau B\) if \(b<a\), and \(\sigma A+\tau B\) if \(a=b\). By the argument of the window lemma, it kills the arc's limit space \(S_\gamma(0)\). The compiled kernel research/tools/bmd_cube_arc_limits.cpp gains a mode (:first) that collects these conditions over a set of arcs and asks whether they force \(A_m=B_m=0\).

  • One side at a time (research/results/bmd-r44-first-order/one-sided-arc-families-d3-d4.txt). Six arcs with \(v(s)<v(t)\), including curved ones, span a space not containing \(T^m\), and likewise six arcs with \(v(t)<v(s)\). Dimensions: 13 of 14 at \(d=3\), \(m=13\); 17 of 19 at \(d=4\), \(m=18\).
  • Jointly (first-order-arcs-d3-d4.txt). Fourteen arcs of all three kinds, including the directions \((1,\pm1)\) of the triangle sides. They leave a two-dimensional space of \((A,B)\) (rank 26 of 28 at \(d=3\), 36 of 38 at \(d=4\)), and \(A_m\), \(B_m\) are not forced.

So the first-order conditions along arcs do not give \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^2\). The true local solutions satisfy more, as the next check shows. This is the same phenomenon as at order zero, one level up. There, single arcs could not force \(W(P)=0\), and the span over arcs could. Here the missing conditions tie the linear part to higher-order terms of the lattice along an arc (the coefficient \(G'\) in \(G=\bar G+\varepsilon G'+\cdots\)), or come from lifts integral in both variables at once.

The local solutions vanish to order 2j

Finite check (check:cube-double-point-vector-jets). The point-scheme kernel research/tools/bmd_cube_point_scheme.cpp gains a mode all. It reports the dimension of the image of the truncated local solutions in \((F[s,t]/\mathfrak m^{K'})^{m+1}\), for every coordinate \(W_c\) and not only \(W_m\). By the argument of the certified local bound, the true local solutions truncate into the computed space. So image 0 modulo \(\mathfrak m^{K'}\) certifies that every local solution at \(P\) vanishes to order \(\ge K'\) in every coordinate. Results over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\), at the \(N\) where the \(W_m\) colengths had stabilized (vector-jets-*.txt):

\((d,\rho)\)\(j\)\(N\)image dimension for \(K'=0,1,\ldots\)certified order
(3,2)1140, 0, 0, 2, 6, 12\(\ge2\)
(4,2)2220, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 6\(\ge4\)
(5,3)2180, 0, 0, 0, 0, 3, 9\(\ge4\)
(5,2)3260, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 6\(\ge6\)

In each case the order is exactly \(2j\) in the computed space. This covers \(j\le\rho\) and, at \((5,2)\), \(j>\rho\). At the boundary \(\rho=1\) the same order appears for \(W_m\): \(L_2\) (\(d=2\), \(j=1\)) has multiplicity 2 at the double points, and the degree-8 generator (\(d=3\), \(j=2\)) has multiplicity 4 (route review); the other coordinates were not computed there. At order zero (\(j=0\)) solutions with \(W_m=1\) exist, which fits order 0.

The conjecture

Conjecture (conj:cube-double-point-vanishing-order). On \(\{0,1\}^3\) over every field of characteristic \(\ne2\), let \(d\ge1\), \(1\le\rho\le d\), \(m=4d+\rho-1\) and \(j=d-\rho\). Then every solution of the \((d+1)\)-system at \(m\), and every local solution at a double point, vanishes to order \(\ge2j\) there in every coordinate. Equivalently, the dual polynomial \(\Phi=\sum_cW_c\) (dual form) vanishes to order \(\ge2j\) along the three lines \(F(1,1,0)\), \(F(1,0,1)\), \(F(0,1,1)\). This is because each \(W_c\) is homogeneous, so vanishing to order \(k\) at the projective point is vanishing along the punctured line, and the homogeneous parts separate by scaling.

  • What is proved. Order \(\ge1\) for all \(j\ge1\) is part 1 of the order-zero theorem.
  • What it gives. For \(j\le\rho\) it implies the fat-ideal conjecture \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^{2j}\). By the fat-point lemma and the product bound it then gives \(\ell_0=B(d,\rho)=3j\) for \(\rho\ge d/2\), the half of open statement 1 with \(\rho\ge d/2\).
  • What it does not give. For \(j>\rho\) order \(2j\) is not enough, and the law needs more: at \((5,2)\) three points of multiplicity 6 give 9, not \(B=11\), and \(J\) there has larger contact along the collision lines (the previous entry's lowest elements contain \(t^8\) and \(s^8\)).

General statement. conj:cube-double-point-vanishing-order, for all \(d\ge1\), \(1\le\rho\le d\) in characteristic \(\ne2\).

Verification gate.

  • Dependencies re-read: the window lemma and the order-zero theorem, the certified local bound (whose review the previous entry completed), and the dual form.
  • Record searches: vanishing order, double point, multiplicity along lines, dual polynomial. They found the fat-ideal conjecture, which this one implies for \(j\le\rho\), the recorded multiplicities of \(L_2\) and the degree-8 generator, and the Hilbert-function check, where the computed global elements \(W_m\) at \((3,2)\) and \((4,3)\) vanish to order \(\ge2\) at the double points. None concerns the whole solution vector.
  • Checks: in its default mode the modified point kernel reproduces the previous entry's colengths and echelon rows at \((3,2)\) exactly. The arc kernel's new mode only appends output. The all mode's certification is the certified local bound's argument applied to every coordinate. No new lemma is proved in this entry, so no separate reviewer pass was run. The claims are a finite check and a conjecture whose evidence is these certificates.

Assessment. This cycle did not prove \(J_P\subseteq\mathfrak m_P^2\). It found why the planned method cannot: arcs taken one at a time, even jointly, leave a two-dimensional space of linear parts. It also found the right statement. The local solutions vanish to order \(2j\) in every coordinate, uniformly in \(\rho\), including at the boundary. That is a cleaner target than the ideal of \(W_m\), and its first case, order \(\ge1\), is already a theorem. The next step is to find the conditions the arcs miss. The natural candidates are the lifts integral in both variables, such as \(w^e(w_1-w_2)^k(w_3-1)^l/(t^ks^l)\), which are exact identities for every local solution and carry information at every order. Process: the first-order test was cheap and decisive. The all mode then turned a plan to prove \(J\subseteq\mathfrak m^2\) into a sharper conjecture.

Individually measured computation12.95 s
Individually measured conversion, checks, and local processing1.43 s

25 September 2026 — Route review of the cube, with lines through a double point: the torsion-partition law splits into a vanishing order and a splitting type

Route review with a working proof of a line-restriction bound; vanishing order plus splitting type equals B through d = 7. Cycle bmd-r45-mixed-lifts. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The vanishing-order conjecture says that every solution vanishes to order \(2j\) at the double points. With fat points it gives open statement 1 only for \(\rho\ge d/2\). For \(j>\rho\) the law needs more than multiplicities at three points. The zero scheme's contact along lines suggested looking at lines through a double point. On such a line, a global solution restricts to a solution of a one-parameter problem, which has a splitting type. How do the vanishing order and that splitting type combine, and do they give \(B\)? The cycle also tests the two-variable lifts proposed in the previous entry.

The line-restriction bound

Setting. As in the order-zero theorem: a solution of the \((d+1)\)-system at \(m\) is \(W=(W_0,\ldots,W_m)\), \(W_c\in F[y_1,y_2,y_3]\) homogeneous of degree \(l+m-c\) (its excess is \(l\)), killing the truncations of the basis of \(V_{3,d}\). For a line \(L=\{u_0P+u_1Q\}\) of \(\mathbb P^2\), let \((E_L)_l\) be the space of \(W\) with \(W_c\) binary forms in \((u_0,u_1)\) of degree \(l+m-c\) (zero if negative) satisfying the same identities with \(y=u_0P+u_1Q\).

Working lemma (lem:cube-line-restriction-bound). Over any field \(F\), let \(P\) be a point of \(\mathbb P^2\) at which every global solution vanishes to order \(\ge k\) in every coordinate. If \((E_{L_0})_{b-1}=0\) for one line \(L_0\) through \(P\), then every solution with \(W_m\ne0\) has excess \(\ge k+b\); that is, \(\ell_0(d+1,m)\ge k+b\). Suppose \(L_0\) and \(P\) have integer coordinates, \((E_{L_0})_{b-1}=0\) over \(\mathbb F_p\), and the vanishing hypothesis holds over \(\mathbb F_p\) and over a field of characteristic 0. Then the conclusion holds over \(\mathbb F_p\) and over every field of characteristic 0.

Proof.

  • Restriction. Let \(W\) have excess \(l\) and \(W_m\ne0\), and let \(L\) be a line through \(P\) with \(W_m|_L\ne0\). All but finitely many lines through \(P\) qualify, since only finitely many of them lie in the curve \(W_m=0\). Parametrize \(L\) with \(P=\{u_1=0\}\). Each \(W_c\) vanishes to order \(\ge k\) at \(P\), so \(W_c(u_0P+u_1Q)\) is divisible by \(u_1^k\). The quotient \(X=W(u_0P+u_1Q)/u_1^k\) satisfies the same linear identities, has degrees \(l-k+m-c\), and \(X_m\ne0\). So \((E_L)_{l-k}\ne0\).
  • One degree suffices. Multiplication by \(u_0\) maps \((E_L)_{l'}\) injectively into \((E_L)_{l'+1}\), so \((E_L)_{b-1}=0\) implies \((E_L)_{l'}=0\) for all \(l'<b\). The condition on the line is therefore a single open condition.
  • Semicontinuity. \((E_L)_{l'}\) is the kernel of a linear map whose matrix depends polynomially on the coordinates of \(Q\) (lines through \(P\)), and on the reduction from \(\mathbb Z\) to \(\mathbb F_p\). Its dimension can only rise under specialization. So \((E_{L_0})_{l'}=0\) implies \((E_L)_{l'}=0\) for all lines \(L\) through \(P\) outside a proper closed set. Over \(\mathbb Q\) it also implies it for \(L_0\) itself, whenever it holds over \(\mathbb F_p\). Both conditions on \(L\) are nonempty open conditions on the lines through \(P\); over the algebraic closure (dimensions do not change under field extension) choose \(L\) satisfying both. Then \(l-k\ge b\). \(\square\)

The module \(\bigoplus_l(E_L)_l\) is the kernel of a map of free graded modules over the two-dimensional regular ring \(F[u_0,u_1]\). So it is a second syzygy, of depth 2, and free by Auslander–Buchsbaum: \(E_L\cong\bigoplus_iF[u_0,u_1](-b_i)\). The lemma uses \(b_{\min}=\min_ib_i\). The proof gives slightly more, \(\ell_0\ge k+\min\{l:\text{the }W_m\text{-image of }(E_L)_l\ne0\}\); in all computed cases that minimum equals \(b_{\min}\). The order-zero theorem supplies \(k=1\) at the double points for \(m\le5d-2\), that is \(\rho\le d-1\), in every characteristic \(\ne2\). So a computed \(b_{\min}\) gives a rigorous bound \(\ell_0\ge1+b_{\min}\), both in characteristic \(p\) and in characteristic 0.

The splitting type on a line through a double point

Finite check (check:cube-line-splitting). The compiled kernel research/tools/bmd_cube_line_splitting.cpp is new. It computes \(\dim(E_L)_l\) and the \(W_m\)-image for a range of \(l\), including negative \(l\), and recovers the \(b_i\) from second differences. It was validated on a line in general position at the boundary \(\rho=1\), \(d=3\), where it gives the single degree \(8=d^2-1\) of the boundary theorem. Results over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\) on the line through \(P=(1{:}1{:}0)\) and \(Q=(7{:}3{:}11)\) (research/results/bmd-r45-mixed-lifts/line-splitting-generic-through-P.txt), for every \(3\le d\le7\) and \(2\le\rho\le d-1\), with \(j=d-\rho\). The case \((7,2)\) was left out of the run (it needs degrees up to about 15). In every run \(\dim(E_L)_{-3}=0\), and the number of recovered \(b_i\) equals \(\rho\), so the lists are complete:

\((d,\rho)\)\(b_i\)\(2j+b_{\min}\)\(B\)certified \(1+b_{\min}\)
(3,2)1, 2332
(4,3)1, 1, 2332
(4,2)2, 4663
(5,4)1, 1, 2, 2332
(5,3)2, 3, 4663
(5,2)5, 611116
(6,5)1, 1, 1, 2, 2332
(6,4)2, 2, 3, 4663
(6,3)3, 5, 6994
(6,2)8, 816169
(7,6)1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2332
(7,5)2, 2, 3, 3, 4663
(7,4)3, 4, 5, 6994
(7,3)6, 7, 814147

In all fourteen cases, \(2j+b_{\min}=B(d,\rho)\) exactly. This includes \(j>\rho\), where fat points fail: at \((5,2)\) and \((6,2)\) the partitions are \(\{3,2\}\) and \(\{3,3\}\). The last column is a rigorous bound in characteristic 0 and in characteristic 1000003, by the lemma with \(k=1\).

Special lines. On the triangle side through \((1{:}1{:}0)\) and \((0{:}1{:}1)\) (line-splitting-triangle-side.txt), \(E_L\) has generators of negative degree (for example, excess \(-1\) at \((2,1)\)), so that line gives no bound. By semicontinuity such lines are special, and a line in general position through \(P\) is the right one.

The conjecture

Conjecture (conj:cube-line-splitting). On \(\{0,1\}^3\) in characteristic \(\ne2\), for \(d\ge2\), \(1\le\rho\le d-1\), \(m=4d+\rho-1\) and a line \(L\) in general position through a double point, \(b_{\min}(E_L)=B(d,\rho)-2(d-\rho)\).

The vanishing-order conjecture asserts order \(2j\) for every coordinate \(W_0,\ldots,W_m\), as the lemma needs. Together with it (\(k=2j\)), the lemma gives \(\ell_0\ge B\), and the product bound gives equality. So the two conjectures imply the whole torsion-partition law for \(2\le\rho\le d-1\). The case \(\rho=1\) is the boundary theorem, and \(\rho=d\) is order zero. The law is thus split into a local statement at one point (the vanishing order) and a one-parameter statement on one line (the splitting type).

The two-variable lifts

The point-scheme kernel gains a mode mix:I:J:O. It adds the elements \(M\,(w_I-w_J)^k(w_O-1)^l/((y_I-y_J)^ky_O^l)\), for \(k+l\ge1\) and monomials \(M\) of weight \(\le d-k-l\). Their truncations \(M(4T)^{k+l}(w_I+w_J)^{-k}(w_O+1)^{-l}\) are polynomial in \(y\), so every local solution kills them exactly.

  • At \(d=3\), \(m=13\) they leave the stabilized results unchanged (N=14).
  • They certify vanishing order 2 already at truncation \(N=6=2d\), where the single-divisor lifts needed about 14.
  • They do not make the system exact at \(N=K+1\): at \(N=4\) the image modulo \(\mathfrak m\) is 2-dimensional, while the truth is 0.

So they shorten certification but do not by themselves give a finite proof.

General statement. lem:cube-line-restriction-bound, for every \(d\), \(m\), point \(P\) and field (only the input \(k\) uses characteristic \(\ne2\)). conj:cube-line-splitting is the all-parameter statement the check supports.

Verification gate.

  • Dependencies re-read: the order-zero theorem (its Part 1 supplies \(k=1\)), the vanishing-order conjecture, the product bound and the boundary theorem.
  • Record searches: line restriction, splitting type, semicontinuity, restriction to a pencil. They found no earlier bound of this kind. The odd-prime thread's pencil splitting identity (lem:pencil-splitting-identity) uses the same device, the splitting of a kernel sheaf on \(\mathbb P^1\), for a different problem.
  • Checks: the line kernel reproduces the boundary degree \(d^2-1\) at \(d=3\) on a line in general position.
  • Reviewer: a fresh-context referee found the lemma, the freeness and the kernel correct, and checked the table and the condition \(m\le5d-2\). Its precision fixes are made: the vanishing hypothesis must hold in each characteristic used; one degree \(b-1\) suffices; the lemma holds in every characteristic; the freeness argument; the completeness of the \(b_i\); the conjecture's all-coordinate form; \(j\) defined; \((7,2)\) explained; and the sharper form with the \(W_m\)-image.

Route review

The line's general claim. The line aims to determine \(\delta_F(n,k,\ell)\) on \(\{0,1\}^n\) in characteristic \(\ne2\), below Menezes' range. That is the thread's goal. In dimension 3 the target is the torsion-partition law, open statement 1. Since the last review:

  • order zero is proved (theorem);
  • every solution vanishes at the double points for \(\rho\le d-1\), and to order \(2j\) in the computed cases;
  • the lower half reduces to a vanishing order and a splitting type, by the lemma above;
  • the certified local bound has completed its review.

What the goal needs. Two statements: open statement 1 in dimension 3, and open statements 2 and 3 for \(n\ge4\). Dimension 3 is the first target, and the line is on it directly.

Falsification attempt. The splitting data above could have refuted the reduction. If \(2j+b_{\min}\) had been below \(B\) anywhere, the two conjectures could not both hold with the law. It was tested at all fourteen reachable cases through \(d=7\), including every partition shape with parts 2 and 3 that occurs there, and it equals \(B\) in each. The triangle side, a special line, was also tested. It gives no bound, which semicontinuity predicts and which does not contradict the general-position statement.

Assessment of the line. Strong. In three cycles the lower half went from a statement about \(\alpha\) of an uncomputed scheme to the following:

  • a theorem at order zero;
  • a proved reduction to two concrete statements, each verified wherever computed;
  • rigorous bounds beyond fat points.

The risk has moved to proving the splitting type. That is a question about a single family of elliptic curves over \(\mathbb P^1\), of the kind the line has already answered (collision exponents, the boundary theorem).

Other items. For \(n\ge4\) (open statements 2 and 3) nothing has changed. The dimension-3 methods, windows at crossing points and restriction to lines, should transfer, and \(n=4\) is the natural next dimension once \(n=3\) is done.

Next step (highest-risk item, cube-odd-characteristic): the splitting type of \(E_L\), starting with \(j=1\).

Assessment. This cycle changes the shape of open statement 1 again, and for the better. Both remaining pieces are now finite-dimensional in a strong sense. The vanishing order is a statement about jets at one point. The splitting type is a statement about a vector bundle on one \(\mathbb P^1\), a family of elliptic curves over a line through the double point. In fourteen cases through \(d=7\), including the partitions with parts 3 where fat points fail, their sum is exactly \(B\). The lemma also gives the first rigorous lower bounds beyond 2 for \(j>\rho\) (9 at \((6,2)\), 7 at \((7,3)\)). The next step is the splitting type: the elliptic curves over \(L\) degenerate only at \(P\) and at four other points. The \(b_i\) should come from a degree count on that family, like the collision exponents did along the divisors. Process: the line kernel was the right tool, written in one pass with a series interface. One result file was truncated because compute.sh shows only the last 8000 bytes and the output was redirected; the full log replaced it. The same happened to an earlier file, research/results/bmd-r40-solution-generators/base-locus-d3-m13-l5-7.txt, whose full output is in that cycle's session archive. A framework fix follows this entry.

25 September 2026 — The splitting conjecture fails for parts ≥ 4; the vanishing order is Σ⌊p²/2⌋, and the corrected pair gives B everywhere computed

Refutation of the line-splitting conjecture as stated; corrected conjectures, checked in 22 cases. Cycle bmd-r46-line-splitting. Produced by Claude Code (Claude Opus 5.5, default reasoning).

Question. The route review reduced the torsion-partition law to two conjectures: vanishing order \(2j\) at the double points, and \(b_{\min}(E_L)=B-2j\) on a line through a double point. It checked them only where every part of the balanced partition is 2 or 3. Do they hold at the ends of the range, \(\rho=1\) and \(\rho=d\), and at the first case with a part 4, \((7,2)\)? The test ends when these cases agree or one of them breaks the pair.

The ends break the conjecture as stated

Refutation (check:cube-line-splitting-refutation). With the line kernel on the same line through \((1{:}1{:}0)\) and \((7{:}3{:}11)\) over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\) (research/results/bmd-r46-line-splitting/line-splitting-ends.txt; every run had \(\dim(E_L)_{-3}=0\) and \(\rho\) generators):

\((d,\rho)\)partition\(b_i\)\(B-2j\)
(2,1){2}11
(3,1){3}44
(4,1){4}79
(5,1){5}1216
(7,2){4,3}11, 1213
(3,3), (4,4), (5,5)none0,0,1; 0,0,0,1; 0,0,0,1,10

A second line, through \((5{:}17{:}2)\), gives the same \(b_i\) at \((4,1)\), \((5,1)\) and \((7,2)\) (line-splitting-second-line.txt). So conj:cube-line-splitting fails at \((4,1)\), \((5,1)\) and \((7,2)\). The last lies in the range \(2\le\rho\le d-1\) that the reduction needs. The failures are exactly the cases with a part \(\ge4\).

A rigorous case. A computed \(b_{\min}\) on a special line could be smaller than on a general one, so the table alone does not refute. At \((4,1)\) the refutation is rigorous. There \(E\) is the line bundle generated by the boundary solution \(W\) (rank \(\rho=1\), \(c_1=-(d^2-1)=-15\)). \(E_L\) is the saturation of \(W|_L\), so \(\deg E_L=-15+\) (the total vanishing of \(W|_L\) on \(L\)). That is at least \(-15+8\), by the order certified below. So \(b=-\deg E_L\le7<9=B-2j\) for every line through \(P\).

The vanishing order is Σ⌊p²/2⌋

At the double point, the order of the local solutions for a part \(p\ge4\) is larger than \(2(p-1)\). The point kernel in mode all (vector-jets-*.txt) certifies:

  • \((4,1)\): order \(\ge8\), with nonzero image modulo \(\mathfrak m^9\), at \(N=22\) with the two-variable lifts;
  • \((7,2)\): order \(\ge12\) at \(N=30\).

Here \(2j\) is 6 and 10. At \(N=24\) the \((7,2)\) run showed a nonzero image modulo \(\mathfrak m^{11}\); that was a truncation artifact, since the certificate at \(N=30\) excludes it. The earlier \(W_m\) data agree: the double-point computation had \(J\subseteq\mathfrak m^{12}\) at \((7,2)\). So did the multiplicities of the boundary generators, 2 and 4 at \(d=2,3\).

For the balanced partition \(p_1,\ldots,p_q\) of \(j+q\) into \(q=\min(\rho,j)\) parts, put

\[ \mu(d,\rho)=\sum_i\lfloor p_i^2/2\rfloor,\qquad \beta(d,\rho)=\sum_i\bigl(\lceil p_i^2/2\rceil-1\bigr),\qquad \mu+\beta=\sum_i(p_i^2-1)=B. \]

For parts 2 and 3, \(\lfloor p^2/2\rfloor=2(p-1)\), so \(\mu=2j\) whenever every part is at most 3. That covers all fourteen cases of the review, whose data are therefore unchanged.

Conjecture (conj:cube-double-point-sharp-order). On \(\{0,1\}^3\) in characteristic \(\ne2\), for \(d\ge1\) and \(1\le\rho\le d\), every local solution of the \((d+1)\)-system at \(m=4d+\rho-1\) vanishes to order \(\ge\mu(d,\rho)\) at each double point, in every coordinate.

Conjecture (conj:cube-line-splitting-sharp). For the same range and a line \(L\) in general position through a double point, \(b_{\min}(E_L)=\beta(d,\rho)=B(d,\rho)-\mu(d,\rho)\).

By the line-restriction bound the two give \(\ell_0\ge\mu+\beta=B\), and with the product bound, the torsion-partition law. Evidence, all over \(\mathbb F_{1000003}\):

  • the splitting prediction holds in all 22 computed cases: the fourteen of the review, the four with \(\rho=1\), the three with \(\rho=d\), and \((7,2)\);
  • the order prediction is certified at \((3,2)\), \((4,2)\), \((5,3)\), \((5,2)\), \((4,1)\) and \((7,2)\).

The previous vanishing-order conjecture (order \(\ge2j\)) follows from the sharp one, since \(\mu\ge2j\). It is not refuted. The previous splitting conjecture is.

What μ suggests. The boundary generator \(\Delta_{3,p}/g\) for a single part \(p\) has degree \(p^2-1\). It vanishes to order \(\lfloor p^2/2\rfloor\) at a double point: 2, 4 and 8 for \(p=2,3,4\), the last from the \((4,1)\) certificate. The remaining \(\lceil p^2/2\rceil-1\) is its degree along a line. Both halves are additive over the parts, as for the products that realize \(B\). So the conjectures say that the product witnesses are extremal twice: in vanishing order at the double points, and in splitting degree on the lines through them.

General statement. conj:cube-double-point-sharp-order and conj:cube-line-splitting-sharp, for all \(d\ge1\), \(1\le\rho\le d\) in characteristic \(\ne2\). The refutation check:cube-line-splitting-refutation corrects conj:cube-line-splitting.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the line-restriction bound (its order hypothesis is for every coordinate, as the sharp-order conjecture states), the product bound's partition, and the previous certificates. The identity \(\mu+\beta=B\) is termwise: \(\lfloor x\rfloor+\lceil x\rceil=p^2\) for \(x=p^2/2\). A computed \(b_{\min}\) can only be smaller on a special line. So the refutation is rigorous at \((4,1)\), by the degree argument, and at \((5,1)\) and \((7,2)\) it rests on two lines in agreement. The claims are a refutation and two conjectures, and no new proof is recorded, so no reviewer pass was run.

Assessment. The review's pair of conjectures was wrong in its first unchecked shape, a part \(\ge4\). It was caught one cycle later by testing the ends, which is what the review's falsification step should have included. The corrected pair has a structural reading: each part \(p\) of the partition contributes \(\lfloor p^2/2\rfloor\) of vanishing and \(\lceil p^2/2\rceil-1\) of splitting degree. It matches every computed case, including the one that broke the first version. The reduction lemma is unaffected. Process: the ends \(\rho=1\) and \(\rho=d\) cost seconds and should have been in the review's test. The \((7,2)\) order needed truncation \(N=30\); at \(N=24\) a spurious order-11 image appeared, a reminder that non-zero images are not evidence.
